# 100 ways to break RatesWeekly

**A second scenario catalogue, numbered 101–200.** Produced 2026-08-31 against `v0.17.0`
(`b0c24d5`). The existing harness in `tests\RateDesk.Scenarios` holds 64 scenarios on ids 1–64
and 99; these start at 101 so they can be merged into `Registry` without an id collision.

---

## Read this part first

Four findings cut across the whole catalogue. Each was derived by reading the shipping code, and
each is independently checkable in under five minutes. If nothing else here gets acted on, these
four should.

### 1. The weekly email's decision gate is not wired to its own marks

`MarksAsOfLondon` is the property that makes the boards read the clock **the prices belong to**
rather than the wall clock. It is set in exactly one place in the entire product:

    src\RateDesk.Weekly.Core\Daily\DailyBuilder.cs:230

`EmailBuilder` pins the same marks to the 16:15 snap at line 120 — and throws the mode away
(`var (_, snapNote) = SnapDiscipline.Apply(...)`) — then calls `svc.BuildWeekly()` at line 171 with
`MarksAsOfLondon` still `null`. So the desk's weekly email, the client-facing product, judges
"has this bank announced?" against the moment somebody pressed the button.

`DailyBuilder`'s own comment states the consequence: *"otherwise a run pressed after a late
statement rolls the board past a decision every price in it predates (desk 2026-08-27)."* That fix
landed in one of the two builders. A WEEKLY RUN pressed at 19:30 on an FOMC day publishes a board
with the Fed meeting deleted and `Priced` re-based onto a pre-statement expectation carrying a `†`
that vouches for it as the delivered rate.

**The suite cannot see it.** `tests\RateDesk.Scenarios\Harness\Surfaces.cs:83` sets
`MarksAsOfLondon` itself before calling `BuildWeekly`, so scenario 58 exercises the engine's rule
and passes green while the shipped weekly path never reaches it. This is the general hazard worth
naming: **the harness tests the engine, not the wiring.** Scenarios 101, 102 and 106.

### 2. Five of six rung-map consumers never receive Bloomberg's own record

`MeetingRungMap` exists because five call sites each built their own boundary list and the copies
drifted. Its constructor takes an optional `recordedEffective` — Bloomberg's own statement of what
each rung pointed at on each day — and its doc comment says that argument is what stopped the app
publishing **−10.6bp where the truth was −0.6bp** on SKSF, 27-Aug-26, and **−8.7bp where the truth
was +4.6bp** on the 10-Feb row. Of the six construction sites in `src\`, one passes it:

| site | records | observed shifts |
|---|---|---|
| `PricingServiceBoards.cs:1551` — the stitcher | yes | yes |
| `PricingServiceWeekly.cs:257` — weekly Δ1d fallback | **no** | no |
| `RollingStrip.cs:223` — the 28 public dashboards | **no** | no |
| `MoverScan.cs:310` — the movers hub | **no** | no |
| `FallbackIngest.cs:74` — manual-override ingest | **no** | no |
| `DailyBook.cs:133` — save-down history | **no** *(deliberate, see `FINDINGS.md`)* | no |

`RollingStrip` has the store in hand and reads maturity records eleven lines further down for the
hard-data gate; `store.EffectiveOn(ticker, day)` is the exact signature the parameter wants; the
stitcher builds a reusable cached lambda for it in ten lines at `PricingServiceBoards.cs:1540`.
Only the `DailyBook` omission is a documented decision. The other four are drift — the same failure
`MeetingRungMap` was written to end, recurring one argument higher up.

The consequence is that **the app's defences are concentrated on the internal email path and are
thinnest on the investor-facing pages.** The dashboards and the movers hub get neither Bloomberg's
records nor the price-based renumber detector (`ObservedShifts` reaches only `DailyBuilder` and
`EmailBuilder`). Scenarios 117, 135, 167, 183 and 184.

### 3. The CHECK gate protects the email and not the public website

`ConfirmChecks` runs **after** `RenderAll` has already written the ~29 dashboard pages
(`MainWindow.xaml.cs:551` vs `:564`). Its own doc comment admits it: *"data-store upkeep and
dashboards from the build phase have already run."* Answering **No** to *"verify these before
distributing"* withholds the email fragments and leaves the flagged numbers standing on the pages
external people read without signing in. Nothing has ever gated that surface. Scenarios 144, 140.

Related, and cheap: the modal's default button is **Yes**, so Enter or Space publishes (scenario
199), and every `CHECK:`-prefixed note goes into it — including `InflHistory.CoherenceNotes`, whose
own doc comment says it is "NON-BLOCKING" (scenario 146). One bad quote on a single UK RPI tenor
therefore cancels a daily product about nine central banks, roughly weekly, which trains the desk
to click through the gate that matters.

### 4. The DST late-roll is deliberate — but nothing can check it, and 28 October 2026 doubles the exposure

**Corrected after review.** This began as "two dated config errors". It is not. `DESIGN.md:362-366`
records the convention: *"the config's single London time is chosen as the LATEST the announcement
occurs across the DST year (RBA 05:30, RBNZ 03:00, BOJ 04:00) — the gate can roll up to ~2h late in
the opposite season, never early."* A late roll is an accepted trade-off, and the RBA / RBNZ / BOJ
offsets are working as designed. What survives is narrower.

`decisionTimeLondon` is a fixed string per bank, so it is only right while London and the bank's own
city change clocks together. For the two North American banks they do not:

| date | bank | true statement (London) | config says | window |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Wed 28-Oct-2026** | FOMC | 18:00 | 19:00 | UK back 25-Oct, US back 01-Nov |
| **Wed 28-Oct-2026** | BOC | 14:00 | 14:45 | same week |
| Wed 17-Mar-2027 | FOMC | 18:00 | 19:00 | US forward 14-Mar, UK 28-Mar |
| Wed 15-Mar-2028 | FOMC | 18:00 | 19:00 | US forward 12-Mar, UK 26-Mar |
| Wed 01-Nov-2028 | FOMC | 18:00 | 19:00 | UK back 29-Oct, US 05-Nov |

Three things follow.

**The invariant is unenforceable.** "The configured time is the latest the statement lands across the
DST year" is a real invariant, and nothing can check it: the source local time and zone are nowhere
in the repo, so `CalendarHealth` can only confirm the string *parses* (`CalendarHealth.cs:81-82`) and
`DecisionClockTests.cs` has no DST case. Record `"decisionLocal": "14:00 America/New_York"` and
derive the London string, and the invariant becomes arithmetic — guardrail 9.

**BOC may violate it in the wrong direction, which the convention does not permit.** Config's 14:45
London implies an **09:45 ET** statement. If the Bank of Canada in fact announces at **10:00 ET**,
the latest across the year is 15:00 and 14:45 fires 15 minutes *early* every ordinary week — rolling
a period off the board and re-basing `Priced` before the statement exists. Early is the one
direction `DESIGN.md` rules out. A question for the desk, not a finding: confirm the source local
time first.

**28-Oct-2026 concentrates the accepted risk.** BOC and FOMC decide the same day, in the one week
both are an hour adrift, so the deliberate late-roll window covers 14:00–14:45 and 18:00–19:00
London on a twin-decision day. Scenario 103.
---

## How this was produced, and how far to trust it

Fifteen agents, one per attack surface, each reading the actual product code and required to cite
`path:line` for every claim. Then an adversarial critic per surface, whose default was to DROP,
re-opened the cited lines and ruled on each one. **Every scenario here has been through that second
pass**, and its verdict is printed on it.

| critic's verdict | count |
|---|---|
| **confirmed** — cited lines check out, premise reachable, severity and kind already right | 31 |
| **corrected** — kept, but severity, kind, confidence or the set-up itself was wrong | 62 |
| **critic-authored** — written by a critic as something its own batch had missed | 7 |
| dropped as impossible, duplicative or unsupported by its citations | 0 |

Nothing was dropped at the second pass, but **33 carry a correction to the set-up or the
arithmetic**, printed on the entry after *"Change before writing it:"*. Read that line before
implementing any scenario — several premises do not hold as first written even though the underlying
fault does.

**The vetting moved the severity distribution a long way.** The finders claimed 70 P0 and 30 P1;
after review it is **53 P0 and 47 P1**. Seventeen claims of "a wrong number reaches a published
surface" turned out to be a missing note, a silent skip or a misleading label. Kind moved too: 16
are now marked *needs a desk call* rather than a defect, because the correct behaviour is genuinely
undecided; 3 are regression locks on code that already works.

**So this is a work list, not a defect list** — but it is now a reviewed one. Confidence, as
re-rated: 78 high, 19 moderate, 3 low. By implementability: 57 are expressible in today's harness as
it stands, 35 need it extended, and 8 are live-terminal or manual-only.

One finding was **retracted** at this pass and is named rather than quietly edited: finding 4
originally claimed the DST offsets were an undetected config error. `DESIGN.md:362-366` documents
them as a deliberate choice.
## Where the existing 64 stop

The current suite is very good at one thing and does not attempt four others.

- **It is one-fault-at-a-time.** Almost every existing scenario moves a single variable. Real bad
  days are compound: a stale feed *on* a roll day, a source switch *on* a decision day, a print
  hole *on* a re-base day. Several of the hundred below are deliberately two-fault.
- **It tests the engine, not the wiring** (finding 1). Every scenario drives `BuildWeekly` through
  `Surfaces.cs`, which pre-sets state the shipped callers do not.
- **Its scope excludes what the desk now ships.** `README.md`: *"Inflation, forward grids and
  dashboards are out of scope."* Since then the inflation runs went into both emails, the
  save-down books became the archive of record, and the dashboards went public. Those three carry
  21 scenarios below (147–155, 170–175, 182–187) and have no coverage today.
- **It never attacks the calendar file.** Every scenario writes a *correct* `meetings.json` and
  tests the code's response. Six below attack the datum instead: a transposed digit, a decision
  entered on the wrong day of a two-day meeting, an override file left beside the exe.
- **`Cal.D(n)` reads the wall clock**, which `FINDINGS.md` already records as costing 8 of 64
  scenarios on any given weekday. Pin the harness's own weekday before trusting it as a release
  gate; that is a prerequisite for everything here.

One clean gap worth naming on its own. Announcement day is a 2×2 — {announced, not announced} ×
{feed re-pointed, not re-pointed}. Scenarios 2, 4 and 5 cover three cells. **Nobody tests the
fourth**, and in it `RollCorrectionDue` returns `false` precisely when the feed *has* re-pointed,
so Δ1d differences two different contracts and prints a phantom full step on a flat tape. That is
scenario 120, and it is implementable in the harness today.

---

## The catalogue

Each scenario states the desk question, the set-up with numbers, the arithmetic of the correct
answer, and the exact wrong output with the branch that produces it. The house rules from
`tests\RateDesk.Scenarios\README.md` apply: **write the expected numbers first, by hand, and never
edit an expectation to make a scenario pass.**


## The announcement clock

*When a statement counts as out, and which clock the run is reading.*

### 101. A run pressed at 23:50 that finishes after midnight rolls the ECB off the board the night before the meeting

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | moderate confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** Three separate wall-clock reads govern one run. If the run spans 00:00 London, the marks belong to yesterday and the decision gate belongs to tomorrow. What does the desk publish?

**Set-up.** London box, correct clock. DAILY pressed 23:50 on Wed 09-Sep-2026; the ~1000-ticker snapshot plus one IntradayBarRequest per snapSet ticker takes ~15 minutes, so the run completes 00:04 on Thu 10-Sep-2026 — the shipped ECB decision date (config/meetings.json:115, decisionTimeLondon 13:15 at :126, period start 16-Sep-2026 at :95). Market seeding, all from the 09-Sep 16:15 bars: ESTRON 1.900; EESF0A (run-down to 16-Sep) 1.900; EESF1A (eff 16-Sep, mat 04-Nov) 1.660; EESF2A (04-Nov→23-Dec) 1.560; EESF3A (23-Dec→10-Feb) 1.500; EESF4A (10-Feb→24-Mar) 1.470. A 25bp cut is ~96% priced into 10-Sep and nothing has been announced. Faked: Bloomberg only (snapshot + bars). What must be injected is the clock skew between the three reads — SnapDiscipline.Apply resolves at 23:52 (London date 09-Sep, mode Snap1615, pins to the 09-Sep 16:15 bars), DailyBuilder:230 re-reads LondonNow() at 00:04 (London date 10-Sep) and sets MarksAsOfLondon = 10-Sep 16:15 — a timestamp SIXTEEN HOURS IN THE FUTURE — and MeetingRun:858 consumes it.

**What must publish.** The marks are 09-Sep's 16:15 close, so by the desk's own rule (scenario 58: 'don't roll — the marks are the close') nothing rolls and nothing re-bases. gateShift = 0, RefPct = the printed ESTRON 1.900, RefRebased = false. Rows: 16-Sep→04-Nov Mid 1.660 Priced (1.660−1.900)×100 = −24.0 Step blank; 04-Nov→23-Dec 1.560 Priced −34.0 Step −10.0; 23-Dec→10-Feb 1.500 Priced −40.0 Step −6.0; 10-Feb→24-Mar 1.470 Priced −43.0 Step −3.0. Front table: ECB · EU | Decision 10-Sep-26 | Start 16-Sep-26 | 1.660 | 1.900 | −24.0 | −96%. Blast title dated 09-Sep-26.

**How it breaks.** With nowLdn = 10-Sep 16:15, DecisionClock.Announced(10-Sep,"13:15",10-Sep 16:15) returns true (PricingServiceBoards.cs:862-863), so gateShift = 1 and the 16-Sep period — the row the whole desk reads that week — is DELETED from the board. The re-base loop (:891-900) then sets lastDec = 10-Sep, effStart = 16-Sep, windowEnd = 16-Sep (ECB FixingLagDays = 0), today = 10-Sep ≤ 16-Sep, and matches the contract with Effective == 16-Sep — post-shift quotes[0] = EESF1A — so RefPct = 1.660 with RefRebased = true. Published: ECB · EU | Decision 29-Oct-26 | Start 04-Nov-26 | 1.560 | 1.660† | −10.0 | −40%, with the footnote '† fixing re-based onto the just-decided period's OIS — the new rate has not printed yet' (WeeklyEmail.cs:186-187) for a decision that is still thirteen hours away. Every remaining Priced is 14bp shallow (−10.0/−16.0/−19.0 instead of −24.0/−34.0/−40.0) and the front's % 25bp reads −40% where the market is at −96%. Nothing in DailyBuilder clamps MarksAsOfLondon to the marks' own date, and LateAnnouncementNotes (:341-355) cannot fire because 13:15 ≤ 16:15, so no note explains the missing row.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 58 (16:15 close vs 19:00 statement) and 4 (decision today BEFORE announcement time), but attacks the opposite direction and a different mechanism: 58 asks whether the pin correctly SUPPRESSES a roll; this asks whether the pin can INVENT one. No existing scenario has MarksAsOfLondon dated later than the marks — Surfaces.cs:84 builds it as Cal.LondonNow().Date + offset, so the harness cannot currently express it.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** DailyBuilder.cs:229-231 does set MarksAsOfLondon from a fresh LondonNow().Date with no clamp to the bars' own date, and Boards:858/861-869/895-935 shift and re-base exactly as described (post-shift quotes[0]=EESF1A matches Effective==16-Sep, so RefPct=1.660). But the expected "blast title dated 09-Sep-26" cannot hold even after a clock fix — DailyBlast.cs:43 dates the title from rep.AsOf = DateTime.Now (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:163), which is 10-Sep at 00:04. **Change before writing it:** Drop the blast-title assertion from expected (or attribute it separately to 105's rep.AsOf mislabel); assert only gateShift=0, RefPct=1.900 unrebased and the four Priced/Step values.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SnapDiscipline.cs:37` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SnapDiscipline.cs:66` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:229` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:230` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:858` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:862` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:891` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:900` | `src/RateDesk.Core/Dates/DecisionClock.cs:37` | `config/meetings.json:126`

### 102. The client-facing weekly email rolls the board past a Fed decision every one of its prices predates

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | confirmed`

**The question.** DailyBuilder pins the decision gate to 16:15 once the marks are pinned. EmailBuilder pins the marks and forgets the gate. Do the two products built from the same snap agree?

**Set-up.** FOMC decision Wed 16-Sep-2026 (config/meetings.json:55, decisionTimeLondon 19:00 at :68, period start 16-Sep at :30, FixingLagDays 1 at :23). WEEKLY RUN pressed 19:30 London — 30 minutes after the statement, which is exactly when someone re-runs it. Marks (both products) pinned to the 16:15 bars: FEDL01 4.330; USSOFED0 (run-down to 16-Sep) 4.330; USSOFED1 (eff 16-Sep, mat 28-Oct) 4.180; USSOFED2 (28-Oct→09-Dec) 4.050; USSOFED3 (09-Dec→27-Jan) 3.930. Feed not re-pointed at 16:15 (it re-points at the announcement). Faked: Bloomberg. Run both DailyBuilder.Build and EmailBuilder.Build against the identical snapshot and diff the boards.

**What must publish.** Both products must publish the SAME board, because both publish the same 16:15 marks. RefPct = 4.330 (unrebased), gateShift = 0. Rows: 16-Sep→28-Oct Mid 4.180 Priced (4.180−4.330)×100 = −15.0 Step blank; 28-Oct→09-Dec 4.050 Priced −28.0 Step −13.0; 09-Dec→27-Jan 3.930 Priced −40.0 Step −12.0. Front: FOMC · US | 16-Sep-26 | 16-Sep-26 | 4.180 | 4.330 | −15.0 | −60%, plus the note 'FOMC announces at 19:00 London, after this run's 16:15 marks — the board is NOT rolled past today's decision'.

**How it breaks.** EmailBuilder.cs:120 calls SnapDiscipline.Apply and DISCARDS the mode (`var (_, snapNote) = ...`), then EmailBuilder.cs:171 calls svc.BuildWeekly() with svc.MarksAsOfLondon still null. MeetingRun:858 therefore falls through to DecisionClock.LondonNow() = 19:30, Announced(16-Sep,"19:00",19:30) is true, gateShift = 1, and the 16-Sep row disappears; the re-base at :891-900 fires (windowEnd = 17-Sep, today 16-Sep ≤ 17-Sep) and matches Effective == 16-Sep → RefPct = 4.180†. The desk email publishes FOMC · US | 28-Oct-26 | 28-Oct-26 | 4.050 | 4.180† | −13.0 | −52% with Priced −13.0/−24.0 where the daily blast built from the identical bars says 16-Sep | 4.180 | 4.330 | −15.0 | −60%. Two products, one snap, a 15bp gap on the most-read row and a whole meeting present in one and absent from the other — and the '†' claims the base is the just-delivered rate when 4.180 is a pre-statement expectation (RefRebasedStale is false, so nothing qualifies it). LateAnnouncementNotes is never called from EmailBuilder at all, so the weekly carries no explanation either way.

**Against the existing suite.** Deliberately the SAME mechanism as 58 from a new angle: 58 proves the rule inside svc.BuildWeekly, but the harness sets svc.MarksAsOfLondon itself (Surfaces.cs:83-84), so 58 passes green while the shipped weekly path never sets it. The extension needed is a scenario that asserts the PRODUCTION WIRING (EmailBuilder.Build's own svc), not the engine — otherwise the suite will keep certifying a fix that only one of the two callers received.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** EmailBuilder.cs:120 does discard the mode (`var (_, snapNote)`) and :171 calls svc.BuildWeekly() with MarksAsOfLondon never assigned — grep shows DailyBuilder.cs:230 is the only production setter, so Boards:858 falls back to the 19:30 wall clock and the FOMC row rolls; FixingLagDays 1 (meetings.json:23) gives windowEnd 17-Sep and the re-base to 4.180, as claimed. Only stray citation: the weekly path calls EmailBuilder.Build at MainWindow.xaml.cs:554, not :713.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/EmailBuilder.cs:120` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/EmailBuilder.cs:171` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:229` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:230` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:343` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:858` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:264` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:713` | `tests/RateDesk.Scenarios/Harness/Surfaces.cs:83`

### 103. 28-Oct-2026: London is on GMT and Toronto is not, so the BOC announces an hour before the app thinks it does

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | low confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** decisionTimeLondon is a single hardcoded string per bank. For the two North American banks it is only right when London and New York change clocks together. What happens in the week when they do not?

**Set-up.** Wed 28-Oct-2026 — a BOC decision (config/meetings.json:230) and an FOMC decision (:56) on the same day. UK DST ended Sun 25-Oct-2026; US/Canada DST ends Sun 01-Nov-2026. So on 28-Oct London is UTC+0 and Eastern is UTC−4: the BOC's 09:45 ET statement lands 13:45 London (config says 14:45, :241) and the Fed's 14:00 ET statement lands 18:00 London (config says 19:00, :68). DAILY pressed 14:15 London — after the BOC cut, before the app believes it. CAD seeding: CAONREPO 2.250 (pre-cut, and in arrears); CDSF0A (run-down to 29-Oct) 2.250; CDSF1A (eff 29-Oct, mat 10-Dec) 2.010; CDSF2A (10-Dec→28-Jan) 1.900; CDSF3A 1.840. The BOC has just cut 25bp to 2.00 and December is ~11bp priced. BOC period start 29-Oct (:213), lag 1 day, FixingLagDays default 0.

**What must publish.** 14:15 London is past the 13:45 statement, so the gate must fire: gateShift = 1, the 29-Oct period leaves the front, and the re-base takes the decided period's own OIS. effStart = 29-Oct, windowEnd = 29-Oct, today 28-Oct ≤ 29-Oct, (29−28) = 1 ≤ 10 → pending = post-shift quotes[0].Mid = 2.010, RefPct = 2.010, RefRebased = true. Rows: 10-Dec→28-Jan Mid 1.900 Priced (1.900−2.010)×100 = −11.0 Step blank; 28-Jan Mid 1.840 Priced −17.0 Step −6.0. Front: BOC · CA | Decision 09-Dec-26 | Start 10-Dec-26 | 1.900 | 2.010† | −11.0 | −44%.

**How it breaks.** 14:15 < 14:45, so DecisionClock.Announced returns false at PricingServiceBoards.cs:863 and again at :900. gateShift stays 0 and no re-base fires, so RefPct remains the pre-cut CAONREPO 2.250. The blast publishes BOC · CA | Decision 28-Oct-26 | Start 29-Oct-26 | 2.010 | 2.250 | −24.0 | −96% — 'a 25bp cut is 96% priced' thirty minutes after that cut was delivered — and every row below is overstated by the full 24bp (December reads −35.0 instead of −11.0, January −41.0 instead of −17.0). This is precisely the failure DecisionClock was built to prevent, reintroduced by a config string. The FOMC arm of the same day mis-states the clock in the reverse direction: LateAnnouncementNotes (DailyBuilder.cs:349) will tell the desk 'FOMC announces at 19:00 London' on a day it announces at 18:00, and the wall-clock-gated surfaces (weekly email, dashboards) flip their roll an hour late. CalendarHealth only tests that the string PARSES (CalendarHealth.cs:81-82); nothing anywhere checks it against a source-of-truth local time plus a timezone, so the error is undetectable from inside the app.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 4 (decision today BEFORE announcement time) and 6 (no decisionTimeLondon), but both of those assume the configured time is TRUE and test the code's response to it. This attacks the datum: same code path, wrong input. NEEDS DESK SIGN-OFF on one quantity before it is written — the repo documents only '14:45 London' and '19:00 London'; it never records that these derive from 09:45 and 14:00 Eastern, so the DST arithmetic rests on my market knowledge, not on the repo. Confirm the source local times first, then the scenario becomes a CalendarHealth check rather than a board scenario.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The DST arithmetic is right (UK ends 25-Oct-26, US 01-Nov-26) and CalendarHealth.cs:81 only parses the string, but DESIGN.md:362-366 records this as a deliberate design choice: the single London time is "the LATEST the announcement occurs across the DST year — the gate can roll up to ~2h late, never early". So this is an accepted trade-off, not an undetected config error, and BOC's source local time (09:45 vs 10:00 ET) is nowhere in the repo. **Change before writing it:** Reframe as a desk question on the accepted late-roll window in the 25-Oct/01-Nov gap week (twin BOC+FOMC day 28-Oct-26, and FOMC 17-Mar-27), after confirming the source local times; drop the "undetectable from inside the app" framing.

**Code.** `config/meetings.json:230` | `config/meetings.json:241` | `config/meetings.json:56` | `config/meetings.json:68` | `src/RateDesk.Core/Dates/DecisionClock.cs:39` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:863` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:900` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:347` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CalendarHealth.cs:81`

### 104. Rungs without intraday bars keep their press-time mid, so one Priced column mixes pre- and post-statement marks

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | confirmed`

**The question.** SnapDiscipline pins 'the published marks' to 16:15. It pins only the tickers that HAVE 16:15 bars; the rest stay live at press time, counted in the log and nowhere else. What does a board look like when the two halves of it are three hours apart?

**Set-up.** FOMC 16-Sep-2026, decisionTimeLondon 19:00. DAILY pressed 19:30 London. The Fed SURPRISES with 50bp (25 was priced). USSOFED1..3 are liquid and return 15-min TRADE bars; USSOFED4 and USSOFED5 are thin and return NO bars — GetLondonSnaps yields an empty list, so SnapDiscipline's `fallback` arm keeps their live mid. 16:15 bar values: FEDL01 4.330 (no bars, unchanged either way); USSOFED1 (eff 16-Sep) 4.180; USSOFED2 (28-Oct) 4.050; USSOFED3 (09-Dec) 3.930. Live at 19:30, after the 50bp surprise: USSOFED4 (27-Jan→17-Mar) 3.560; USSOFED5 (17-Mar→28-Apr) 3.480 — the far strip has repriced ~25bp lower than its own 16:15 bars (3.810 / 3.730). MarksAsOfLondon = 16:15, so nothing rolls and nothing re-bases (correct, per 58).

**What must publish.** Either every published mark is the 16:15 snap, or the run refuses to publish the rungs it could not pin. On a wholly-16:15 board: Priced = (mid − 4.330)×100 → 16-Sep −15.0; 28-Oct −28.0 Step −13.0; 09-Dec −40.0 Step −12.0; 27-Jan (3.810) −52.0 Step −12.0; 17-Mar (3.730) −60.0 Step −8.0. A smoothly steepening path, ~12bp per meeting, which is what a 25bp-priced board looked like at 16:15.

**How it breaks.** SnapDiscipline.cs:65-74 overwrites only the tickers whose last bar's London date equals today; the rest fall to `fallback++` and keep whatever the 19:30 snapshot gave them. The board then publishes 4.180/4.050/3.930 from 16:15 alongside 3.560/3.480 from 19:30, and Step differences one against the other: 27-Jan reads Priced (3.560−4.330)×100 = −77.0 with Step −77.0−(−40.0) = −37.0, and 17-Mar Priced −85.0 Step −8.0. The desk reads a −37.0bp single-meeting step — one and a half cuts priced into a single January meeting — that exists only because two rows were marked three hours apart. It is 25bp of pure clock artifact on the visible number, and the OutlierGuard cross-sectional check may well fire on it and be dismissed as 'the surprise'. Worse, the only record that any mark was unpinned is `log?.Invoke("... N ticker(s); M without bars stay live")` at SnapDiscipline.cs:76-77: the method returns (mode, null), so no report note exists, ConfirmChecks (MainWindow.xaml.cs:335) has nothing to gate on, and the count never reaches the blast, the workbook, the email or the popup.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 25 (boundary-day sources: the 16:15 snap anchors, the close never does) and 58, but neither touches the PIN'S OWN FALLBACK ARM. The README lists 'SnapDiscipline is not applied' and 'ScenarioHistory.GetLondonSnaps ignores its londonTimeOfDay' as harness limits, so this needs a bars provider that can return bars for some tickers and none for others, plus SnapDiscipline in the scenario sequence.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** SnapDiscipline.cs:65-74 pins only tickers whose last bar matches today's London date and RefDataClient.cs:500-503 returns an empty array for a ticker with no TRADE bars, so those rungs keep the 19:30 mid; the count exists only in the log at :76-77 (the method returns Note=null, so ConfirmChecks at MainWindow.xaml.cs:334 has nothing to gate on). Step arithmetic checks out: (3.560-4.330)*100 = -77.0 against -40.0 = -37.0.

### 105. DAILY pressed at 00:20 publishes yesterday's marks under today's date with every change column reading 0.0, and the only warning is the one deliberately excluded from the gate

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** The app is left open overnight and someone presses DAILY at 00:20 believing they are catching yesterday's close. Does anything on any surface tell the reader?

**Set-up.** London box, correct clock, quiet Tue/Wed 08/09-Sep-2026. DAILY pressed 00:20 on 09-Sep. At 00:20 the European meeting OIS quote nothing new: the live snapshot returns the last two-sided market from ~17:30 on 08-Sep, and PX_CLOSE_1D has already rolled to 08-Sep's close. EESF1A live mid 1.730, PrevClose 1.730; store's newest close 08-Sep 1.730; 02-Sep 1.790. 08-Sep was a real 6bp rally day (07-Sep close 1.790). Alternative same-defect path: the scheduled `RatesWeeklyCli daily` cron fires at 00:20 (Cli/Program.cs:175) and skips ConfirmChecks entirely.

**What must publish.** A run whose marks are 08-Sep's evening prints must either be labelled 08-Sep or be blocked. If it publishes, the desk must be stopped at a modal, not informed in a log pane it never opens, and Δ1d must be blank rather than 0.0 — comparing 08-Sep's last print against 08-Sep's close is not a change on any day.

**How it breaks.** SnapDiscipline.Resolve returns PreClose (00:20 < 15:30, :28), so MarksAsOfLondon is never set and the correct note is generated — 'CHECK: PRE-CLOSE RUN — London 00:20, marks are live mids, not closes — verify before distribution' (:42-43). It is then routed away from every human: ConfirmChecks filters `!n.Contains("PRE-CLOSE RUN")` at MainWindow.xaml.cs:335 so no modal fires (deliberate, and correct for a 09:00 run — but 00:20 and 09:00 are the same branch), and notes are not content on any surface, so the blast, the workbook and the email body carry nothing. Meanwhile rep.AsOf = DateTime.Now = 09-Sep 00:20, so DailyBlast.cs:43 titles it 'London EOD OIS Run — 09-Sep-26', the workbook is DRAX OIS Runs 9Sep26.xlsx and the Outlook subject is 'DRAX Swaps Closing Runs - 09 Sep 2026' — a full trading day mislabelled. Every change reads flat: Δ1d anchors on DateTime.Today.AddDays(-1) = 08-Sep, whose stored close is 1.730, against a live mid of 1.730 → 0.0 on every row of all nine banks, and the invariant 'a flat change prints 0.0 and never +0.0' makes it look like a deliberately quiet market rather than a degenerate comparison. The real 08-Sep move of −6.0bp is never published anywhere, and the 09-Sep run later that afternoon will anchor Δ1d on 08-Sep too and report the two days' moves as one.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 27 (no pre-roll history: 1d falls back to CoD) and 24 (three-week hole must blank 1w) — both about the change anchor degrading — and to 45 (notes are not content). New in two ways: the run is in the wrong HOUR rather than the data being wrong, and the PRE-CLOSE exemption at MainWindow.xaml.cs:335 is the specific reason no human sees it. The harness's own documented window is 00:10-23:50 London, so it can host the 00:20 case only with an injected clock; the ConfirmChecks filter is WPF and needs a unit test, not a scenario.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** SnapDiscipline.cs:28/41-43, ConfirmChecks' PRE-CLOSE exemption at MainWindow.xaml.cs:335, rep.AsOf = DateTime.Now (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:163 → DailyBlast.cs:43, DailyBook.cs:18) and the Today-1 anchor at PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286 all read as claimed. But every published mid is a real 08-Sep print and every Δ is arithmetically consistent with its anchor — the fault is a mislabelled run plus a degenerate anchor, which the rubric puts at P1; and the demand for a modal contradicts the documented desk call at MainWindow.xaml.cs:327-331. **Change before writing it:** Drop the "must be stopped at a modal" arm (PRE-CLOSE is deliberately non-blocking); assert the date labels (blast/workbook/subject reading 09-Sep over 08-Sep marks) and that Δ1d must blank rather than print 0.0.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SnapDiscipline.cs:28` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SnapDiscipline.cs:41` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SnapDiscipline.cs:42` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:327` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:335` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:163` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBlast.cs:42` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBook.cs:18` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:888` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Cli/Program.cs:175`

### 106. The public dashboards drop the meeting the blast still publishes, because nobody passes them the marks' clock

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | confirmed`

**The question.** RollingStrip.ForMeetings accepts a nowLondon parameter for exactly this. Both production callers omit it. What do the 28 zero-sign-in pages show on a Fed evening?

**Set-up.** FOMC 16-Sep-2026, decisionTimeLondon 19:00 (config/meetings.json:55, :68). The desk runs DAILY at 16:25 (blast + workbook + email out, marks pinned to 16:15, board correctly unrolled with the LateAnnouncementNotes line) and then WEEKLY RUN at 19:30 to refresh the dashboards and the site pack. Store's LatestDate() = 15-Sep, so the strips render asOf 15-Sep. USD strip rows from config: 16-Sep, 28-Oct, 09-Dec, 27-Jan, 17-Mar. Faked: Bloomberg.

**What must publish.** The dashboards must agree with the blast the desk sent three hours earlier: the USD 'FOMC meeting-dated OIS' panel keeps 16-Sep-26 as its first row, because the marks behind it are 16:15 marks. The movers hub must rank the same strip.

**How it breaks.** RollingStrip.cs:214 resolves `nowLondon ?? DecisionClock.LondonNow()` and both callers — CurrencyPage.cs:48 and MoverScan.cs:307 — pass no nowLondon, so the strip is gated on 19:30. The :216-217 filter drops 16-Sep (DecisionFor finds 16-Sep, Announced(16-Sep,"19:00",19:30) is true) and the published usd.html panel starts at 28-Oct-26. A client who read the 16:25 blast — 'FOMC 16-Sep, 4.180, Priced −15.0' — clicks the dashboard link in that same email and finds the meeting gone, with no note anywhere on the page (the desk rule forbids blurbs on the movers page, and notes are not content). MoverScan then ranks a strip whose front contract has silently shifted one rung, so the USD meeting entry's |z| of the 1w change is computed on a different contract than the one the email ranked. Because EmailBuilder also leaves the gate on the wall clock, the weekly email and the dashboards agree with EACH OTHER and both contradict the daily product — which makes the discrepancy look like a data problem rather than a clock problem.

**Against the existing suite.** 51, 52, 53 and 59 all exercise the dashboard strip on and around decision days, but every one of them uses a bank whose announcement precedes 16:15 (59 is the ECB at 13:15), so the strip's clock and the board's clock never diverge in the existing catalogue. This is the first case where they must. The harness already builds strips with no nowLondon (Surfaces.cs:157-162), mirroring production, so the assertion is one Custom check — but it only means anything when the suite runs after 19:00 London, which needs the pinned clock FINDINGS.md already asks for.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** RollingStrip.cs:214-218 gates on `nowLondon ?? DecisionClock.LondonNow()` and both production callers omit it (CurrencyPage.cs:48, MoverScan.cs:307), with RenderAll driven from the UPDATE click at MainWindow.xaml.cs:548 — so a 19:30 weekly drops the 16-Sep row the 16:25 blast still carries. P1 is right: a row disappears, no printed cell is arithmetically wrong.

### 107. After 16:15 the STALE line judges the live quote's age while the published mark is the 16:15 bar, so it fires on correct marks and can never fire on a bad one

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | confirmed`

**The question.** The desk rule is 'a rung off a feed quiet >1h gets a STALE line'. Once the marks are pinned to 16:15, whose age is >1h — the quote's, or the mark's?

**Set-up.** Ordinary quiet Thursday, no decision anywhere. DAILY pressed 18:40 London (a late re-run after an earlier failed pull). Marks pinned to the 16:15 bars for every ticker that has them. European meeting families stopped ticking at 16:12 (EESF1A-4A, SONIA/MPC, SKSF, NIBOR) and carry LAST_UPDATE stamps ~148 minutes old; USD and CAD families tick continuously into the US afternoon, so the snapshot's 10th-percentile baseline (RatesSnapshot.BaselineAgeMinutes) is ~2 minutes. Second arm, same run: a NORGES rung whose feed died at 09:00 and whose 16:15 bar is therefore a 09:00 print — the case the rule exists for.

**What must publish.** A run whose published marks ARE the 16:15 snap must judge staleness against the age of the 16:15 BAR, not against the live quote at press time. The Thursday-evening European rungs are marked exactly as intended and must carry no STALE line and no 'switch contributor' advice. The NORGES rung, whose 16:15 bar is seven hours stale, is the one that must be flagged.

**How it breaks.** PricingServiceBoards.cs:1073 tests `q.AgeMinutes - ageBase > 60` on the LIVE quote — SnapDiscipline.Update (SnapDiscipline.cs:71 → RatesSnapshot.cs:51-63) rewrites Bid/Ask/Last and leaves AgeMinutes untouched — so at 18:40 every European rung reads 148 − 2 = 146m and the run emits four STALE lines: 'STALE: ECB — 4 published rung(s) off a feed quiet >1h (INCLUDING THE FRONT, worst 16-Sep-26 at 146m) — consider another contributor (SOURCES)', plus MPC, RIKSBANK and NORGES. ShowStaleNotes (MainWindow.xaml.cs:346-357) pops a modal listing all of them. Every one is false: the mark is the 16:15 snap and the quote being quiet since 16:12 is the reason the snap is a close. The advice — switch contributor — would replace a correct mark with a worse one. And the arm that matters is silent in the same run: the NORGES rung whose 16:15 bar is a 09:00 print may well have re-stamped LAST_UPDATE since (a frozen market keeps re-stamping, as QuoteData.AgeMinutes' own doc comment says at RatesSnapshot.cs:22-25), so the one genuinely stale published number is the one that never trips the guard. Net effect: after 16:15 the STALE line degrades into 'is the London session over', it trains the desk to dismiss the popup, and the guard the desk asked for is off precisely when the product publishes.

**Against the existing suite.** 37 ('published rung feed >1h quiet on decision day (STALE)') proves the line FIRES when it should. This is the complement and a new angle on the same mechanism: whether the age it measures still describes the number it publishes once SnapDiscipline has replaced that number. Needs the harness to seed AgeMinutes and to run SnapDiscipline, neither of which it currently does (README: 'SnapDiscipline is not applied').

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** RatesSnapshot.Update (RatesSnapshot.cs:51-63) rewrites Bid/Ask/Last and leaves AgeMinutes untouched, so the >1h test at PricingServiceBoards.cs:1073 still judges the live quote after SnapDiscipline.cs:71 has replaced the published mark; the note at :1130-1134 and the modal at MainWindow.xaml.cs:348 then fire on correctly pinned rungs. The second arm is supported by the re-stamping caveat in QuoteData's own comment at RatesSnapshot.cs:24-26.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1040` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1044` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1073` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1131` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SnapDiscipline.cs:71` | `src/RateDesk.Core/Market/RatesSnapshot.cs:22` | `src/RateDesk.Core/Market/RatesSnapshot.cs:51` | `src/RateDesk.Core/Market/RatesSnapshot.cs:98` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:346`

## The rate that does not bite yet

*Decided today, effective later: settlement lags, fixing lags, and the window between.*

### 108. The Friday after every MPC move: GBP Priced is a full 25bp out because MPC has no fixingLagDays

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** Sterling is the desk's home currency and the MPC moves 4-8 times a year. On the morning after a Bank Rate change, what is GBP Priced measured against?

**Set-up.** MPC, decision on the previous business day at 12:00 London (Cal.Bd(-1), exactly scenario 54's shape with MPC substituted for FOMC). Bank Rate cut 25bp, 4.00 -> 3.75. GPSF period starts ON the decision date (config/meetings.json MPC dates == decisionDates, lag 0). SONIO/N Index (config/currencies/gbp.json:19) is published a day in arrears, so the live mid today is YESTERDAY's fixing = 3.970, the pre-cut level; the new Bank Rate bites today, so today's SONIA (3.720) does not print until tomorrow. Rows: 05-Nov period 3.700, 17-Dec 3.650, 04-Feb 3.620. Nothing faked but the calendar and the market - fixingLagDays is inherited from the shipped MPC entry, which does not set it (ConfigWriter.cs:46-54 overrides only dates/times/flags).

**What must publish.** The re-base must still be running: base = the decided period's own OIS = 3.720. Priced(05-Nov) = (3.700-3.720)x100 = -2.0bp; (3.650-3.720)x100 = -7.0; (3.620-3.720)x100 = -10.0. Steps blank/-5.0/-3.0. Front line: MPC GBP | 3.700 | 3.720 dagger | -2.0 | -8%. This is precisely what the FOMC gets one row up in the same table, because FOMC carries fixingLagDays:1 (config/meetings.json:23) for the identical geometry.

**How it breaks.** MPC's config entry (config/meetings.json:146-199) has NO fixingLagDays, so FixingLagDays defaults to 0 (PricingServiceBoards.cs:182) and windowEnd = eff = the decision date (PricingServiceBoards.cs:918-924). today <= windowEnd is false the very next day (:925), the whole re-base block is skipped, RefPct stays the stale SONIA and RefRebased stays false. The blast prints '{GB} MPC Run   (SONIA 3.970)' with no '(rebased)' (DailyBlast.cs:52-53), rows -27.0 / -32.0 / -35.0 in the Priced column, and the front table reads 3.970 with no dagger, Priced -27.0, '% 25bp -108%' (SheetEmail.cs:164-173) - i.e. 'a second full cut is priced for November' on a day nothing is priced. Steps are differences so they stay right, every free invariant passes, and OutlierGuard only ever looks at the change columns (OutlierGuard.cs:33-34), so nothing flags it. The code's own doc says this must not happen: 'FOMC and MPC are the exception and carry 1 in config' (PricingServiceBoards.cs:179-181) - the 1 was pasted into SNB instead (config/meetings.json:373-376), a run dropped from every published surface (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:211-212).

**Against the existing suite.** 54 (day after a Fed cut) and 55 (Fed+ECB both cut) are the same mechanism on FOMC/ECB, which both pass; 11 is FOMC cut yesterday. No scenario in the catalogue uses MPC on the day after a move, and the harness cannot vary fixingLagDays, so substituting Bank="MPC" into 54 is the whole test.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** PricingServiceBoards.cs:179-181 states "FOMC and MPC are the exception and carry 1 in config" while the MPC entry (config/meetings.json:147-199) has no fixingLagDays, so :182 defaults 0 and :925 closes the window on eff itself; ConfigWriter.cs:47-56 overrides no such flag, and scenario 54 (Group11_FixingBase.cs, FOMC, fixingLagDays 1) is the passing twin. Priced -27.0/-32.0/-35.0 vs -2.0/-7.0/-10.0 and the -108% front cell are self-consistent.

### 109. A single bad print on the decided rung becomes the fixing for the whole SEK board - +45.0 priced, hours after a cut

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** The re-base adopts the just-decided period's live mid as the base for every Priced on the board. What plausibility test does that number pass?

**Set-up.** RIKSBANK, decision TODAY 08:30 London (passed), 25bp cut 2.00 -> 1.75. SWESTR live 1.950 (yesterday's print, pre-cut). Period grid: decided period starts D+6, next D+41 (decision D+35), then D+83 (decision D+76). The decided period's honest OIS is 1.700; the rung carrying it MISPRINTS at 1.240 - the documented SKSF failure mode, a live two-sided 1.387 between 1.848/2.086 neighbours on 2026-08-03 (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1002-1005). Published rows: D+41 at 1.690, D+83 at 1.660. Seasonal variant, same code path: in December the decided period IS the Y/E turn period, whose average the config itself measures at ~1.47 against ~1.93 on the policy path (config/meetings.json:511-513).

**What must publish.** The base must never be a number the app would refuse to publish as a row. Either the misprint is rejected and the re-base falls through to step 3 (the decided contract's last close before the decision, RefRebasedStale = true, rendered '(rebased, pre-statement)'), or the base is the honest 1.700 giving Priced(D+41) = (1.690-1.700)x100 = -1.0 and Priced(D+83) = -4.0, Step -3.0, front line '-1.0 | -4%'.

**How it breaks.** The gate shift puts the decided period at quotes[0] (PricingServiceBoards.cs:846-877) and the re-base takes its Mid verbatim - no neighbour test, no turn test, no bounds check (PricingServiceBoards.cs:932-935, 973-978). RejectedBy only ever judges published INTERIOR rows and stands down for turn rows (:1023-1037, :1077), and quotes[0] is neither. So RefPct = 1.240 and the board publishes Priced +45.0 / +42.0 with the front line 'RIKSBANK SEK | 1.690 | 1.240 dagger | +45.0 | +180%' under the footnote 'dagger fixing re-based onto the just-decided period's OIS' (SheetEmail.cs:183) - the desk reads nearly two 25bp HIKES priced on the afternoon of a 25bp cut, sign-flipped. Steps are unchanged, Priced == (Mid-Fixing)x100 still holds, SEK has no guardFutures, and OutlierGuard reads only change columns: every automatic check passes.

**Against the existing suite.** 38 and 62 attack the misprint guard on ROWS; 30/31/33 attack the Y/E turn as a row; 35 and 61 attack the re-base FALLBACKS. None of them feeds a bad or turn-contaminated print into the base itself. The December turn variant is live-only (turn detection keys on the row's own calendar year, so the harness cannot build one in August).

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** PricingServiceBoards.cs:932-935 takes quotes[k].Mid verbatim as RefPct (:973-978) with no test of any kind, and RejectedBy (:1023-1037, called at :1077 for n>=1 with lo>=1) provably never judges or reads index 0 — the rung the gate shift at :861-877 puts the decided period on. RIKSBANK ships no guardFutures (config/meetings.json:473-535) and +45.0/+42.0/-3.0/+180% are arithmetically consistent.

### 110. A rate announced today but effective in three weeks: the decided period stays on the front at -100% of 25bp

`P0 - wrong number published | needs a desk call | moderate confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** Every pairing of a decision with the period it decides is capped at 10 calendar days by one constant. What happens when a bank announces a change effective further out than that - a December decision applying from January?

**Set-up.** RIKSBANK. 25bp cut 2.00 -> 1.75 announced 3 days ago at 08:30 London, applying from D+19 (a 22-day effective lag - the shape the Riksbank has used when the intervening Wednesday falls in the Christmas week; the desk must confirm the precedent before the fix, the code behaviour needs no confirmation). Bloomberg's grid follows the truth so the period start on file is D+19; the next start is D+61 (decision D+38). SWESTR 1.950, still the old rate and correctly so - nothing is in force yet. Quotes: the decided D+19 period 1.700, the D+61 period 1.690.

**What must publish.** The decided period is not market pricing of a decision and must leave the board; the base must re-base onto its own OIS (1.700); the front row becomes D+61: Priced = (1.690-1.700)x100 = -1.0, '% 25bp -4%'. At minimum, the daily surfaces must carry a note that an announced decision has no pairable period start, so the reader knows Priced is measured against a rate the bank has already changed.

**How it breaks.** DecisionClock.PairToleranceDays is a hard 10 (Dates/DecisionClock.cs:16) and 22 > 10, so DecisionFor returns null in three places at once: the front roll never fires (PricingServiceBoards.cs:861-864) and the DECIDED period is published as the front row; the re-base is gated off by (eff-dec) <= 10 (:925); and the front-table pairing finds no decision (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:235-238) so the line renders 'D+19*'. The blast publishes 'Priced -25.0 / % 25bp -100%' on the front line - 'the Riksbank is fully priced for a 25bp cut' three days after that cut was announced worldwide - against a truth of -1.0, i.e. 24bp on the most-read row plus a phantom row. The only detector that sees any of it is CalendarHealth check 2 ('no decision date for the period starting ...', CalendarHealth.cs:59-67), which the daily product never calls and the weekly writes to a side file.

**Against the existing suite.** 8 is a 6-day BOJ lag and 3 a 6-day ECB lag - both inside the 10-day pairing; 48 is an empty decisionDates list. Nothing in the catalogue puts a REAL announced decision outside the pairing window, which is the one input that silently disables the gate, the re-base and the front pairing together.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** DecisionClock.cs:16/:46 caps every pairing at 10 days and PricingServiceBoards.cs:925 carries its own independent `(eff - dec).TotalDays <= 10`, so a 22-day lag disables the gate roll (:861-864) and the re-base together; only CalendarHealth.cs:56-67 sees it and the daily never calls it. The Riksbank precedent is real (19-Dec-2019 decision, effective 8-Jan-2020), and unspecified-behaviour is the right kind because widening the cap re-opens the RBA 11-Aug-26 mispairing documented at PricingServiceWeekly.cs:230-234.

### 111. Silver Week BOJ: the Delta1w column publishes a 13-day change carrying the hike, four days running

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** The BOJ's lag varies 1-6 days, so the whole window before every period start is masked as unattributable. What does a 1w anchor landing in that mask actually publish?

**Set-up.** BOJ, unstable lag exactly as the shipped config has it (18-Sep-26 announcement -> 24-Sep start is 6 days because Mon 21, Tue 22 and Wed 23 Sep 2026 are Tokyo holidays; 22-Jul-27 -> 23-Jul-27 is 1). Re-dated relative to today: announcement A = D-12, period start S = D-6, next announcement D+31 -> start D+34. The BOJ hiked 25bp (0.50 -> 0.75) at A with 5bp priced: the 02-Nov-style contract (start D+34) closed 0.530 on D-13, jumped to 0.730 on A, and has been flat at 0.730 ever since. TONA now prints 0.730 (caught up). RecordMaturities = false - a machine whose 45-day BDH price seed reaches back but whose per-day maturity recording began later (a fresh install, or a machine that was off).

**What must publish.** Delta1w on D+0 must be 0.0 (the contract was 0.730 seven days ago) or BLANK. It must not be a 13-day change, and it must not include the announcement repricing - the desk's own rule is that 5/7/10 covers 'weekends and long holiday bridges, no more'.

**How it breaks.** LagRange over the whole calendar is (1,6), so MeetingRungMap masks s0-1..s0-6 for EVERY start (MeetingRungMap.cs:76-83) - here D-12..D-7, six days, and the announcement day itself is inside it, so even the boundary-day SNAP the stitcher is designed to anchor on is thrown away (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1704-1706). With no maturity records HasRecordFor cannot lift the mask, so the stitched series has a hole from D-12 to D-7 and its last point before the D-7 target is D-13. ChangeToBp's 7-day slack passes it (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287, :528-535), so the Delta1w cell publishes (0.730-0.530)x100 = +20.0bp for a week in which nothing moved - on the four business days whose target-7 lands in the mask. Every row is corrupted identically so the cross-sectional test's median is +20 too, and +20 is under the 30bp Delta1w absolute bar (OutlierGuard.cs:23): no note anywhere. Delta1m shows +20.0 as well, which makes the blast look internally consistent. The dashboard walks back with no cap at all (RollingStrip.cs:153-154) and no record arm, so it prints the same +20 regardless of what the store recorded.

**Against the existing suite.** 56 attacks the same unstable-lag machinery but on the day AFTER the start with the anchor inside the gap, and asserts the mis-rung read; 24 proves a three-week hole blanks 1w. Neither tests the mask's WIDTH against the anchor cap: the mask is 6 days by construction on the BOJ, the 1w cap allows 7, so a 13-day change is admitted as legal. 13 is a 1m anchor, not 1w.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** The shipped BOJ calendar (config/meetings.json:429-457) gives LagRange (1,6), so MeetingRungMap.cs:76-83 masks six days before every start and PricingServiceBoards.cs:1704-1706 drops them with no record arm; ChangeToBp (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287, :528-535) then measures its 7-day slack against the TARGET, admitting a D-13 anchor for a D-7 target and publishing a 13-day change as Δ1w — while a clean D-6 close sits one day the other side of the target and is never considered. Scenario 56's market is flat on every contract, so it structurally cannot see this.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingRungMap.cs:76` | `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingCalendar.cs:65` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1704` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:528` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/RollingStrip.cs:153` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:23`

### 112. Operator follows the config's own comment, adds the settled Riksbank decision to pastDates, and SEK Delta1w breaks

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** config/meetings.json documents pastDates as trailing-year decisions, but SKSF renumbers at the period START. What does a hand-entered settled decision do to the SEK boundaries?

**Set-up.** RIKSBANK, quiet week, no decision imminent. The last meeting was announced D-11 and its rate applied from D-5 (the documented Aug-2026 shape: 'meeting 19th, decision published 20th, rate applies from the 26th'). Following config/meetings.json:8, the operator adds 'D-11' to pastDates. The market is flat and prices further easing: the D-5 period 1.700, the D+30 period 1.550, the D+72 period 1.450 - 10-15bp apart, so a mis-rung read is unmistakable. On D-7 SKSF1A was the D-5 period at 1.700 and SKSF2A the D+30 period at 1.550; the family renumbered on D-5, as the desk probed on 2026-08-25.

**What must publish.** Delta1w on the D+30 row = (1.550-1.550)x100 = 0.0. Without the hand edit the migration puts the true START in pastDates and the boundary is right, so this scenario is the same calendar twice, with and without the extra line, and both must publish 0.0.

**How it breaks.** MeetingRungMap concatenates PastDates RAW (MeetingRungMap.cs:47-49) and the 14-day cluster keeps the earliest (:52-56), so the hand-entered decision D-11 wins and the true start D-5 is dropped - the boundary now sits 6 days before the family renumbers, for the one family the desk proved renumbers at the start. A D-7 anchor is therefore treated as post-renumber, the D+30 contract is counted at rung 1 instead of 2, and the lookback reads SKSF1A's D-7 close - the D-5 period's 1.700 - as this contract's level: Delta1w publishes -15.0bp on a flat tape, on the dashboard unconditionally (RollingStrip.cs:223, no record arm) and in the blast on any machine whose maturity records do not cover D-7. MeetingCalendar treats the same list correctly - a hand-entered pastDate is a decision and gets the derived lag (MeetingCalendar.cs:28-30, 44-46) - so two derivations over one list disagree, and the loader's own migration adds the true start under a 14-day dedup that this edit then out-clusters (PricingServiceBoards.cs:243-246).

**Against the existing suite.** 28-33 cover Riksbank period-start renumbering, the Y/E turn and trustConfigDates, all with clean calendars; 57 walks the save-down across a Riksbank decision. None of them exercises a hand-entered pastDate on a rollsAtPeriodStart family, which is the config edit the file's own header invites.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** MeetingRungMap.cs:47-49 concatenates PastDates raw and :52-56 keeps the earliest of each 14-day cluster, so a hand-entered decision six days before the true start silently replaces it as the boundary — while MeetingCalendar.cs:44-46 treats the identical list as decisions needing the derived lag, and RollingStrip.cs:223 builds the map with no record arm at all. The shipped RIKSBANK pastDates (config/meetings.json:523-532) are themselves decision dates, so this is house habit, not an exotic edit; -15.0 vs 0.0 checks out on boundaries {D-11,D+30} giving rung 1 instead of {D-5,D+30} giving rung 2.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingRungMap.cs:47` | `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingRungMap.cs:52` | `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingCalendar.cs:44` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:243` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/RollingStrip.cs:223` | `config/meetings.json:8` | `config/meetings.json:518`

### 113. The futures guard prints 'ok' on a board whose Priced column is 25bp wrong, and claims it cannot happen

`P1 - missing gate or note | regression lock | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** FuturesGuard's docstring says a wrong re-base moves the blend by a step and will be caught. Does the guard read anything the re-base touches?

**Set-up.** The scenario-1 setup - MPC, cut on the previous business day, base stuck on the pre-cut SONIA - with DisableGuardFutures = false and the SFI contract for the first fully-covered IMM quarter seeded through Extras. Rows: 3.700 from D+66, 3.650 from D+108, 3.620 from D+157, 3.600 from D+199. For a Dec-quarter window (3rd Wed Dec to 3rd Wed Mar, 91 days: 1 day at 3.700, 49 at 3.650, 41 at 3.620) the day-weighted blend is (3.700 + 178.85 + 148.42)/91 = 3.6370, so the future is seeded at 96.363.

**What must publish.** The pre-publish gate must either catch a base fault or stop claiming it does. Guard line: implied 3.637 vs blend 3.637, delta 0.0bp, inside the 2.5bp default tolerance - so 'ok' is arithmetically right, and the scenario's job is to lock that the desk must NOT read 'futures guard ok' as 'Priced is sound'. A CHECK note naming the base would be the fix.

**How it breaks.** FuturesGuard blends row.MidPct only (FuturesGuard.cs:70, :97-102, :108-121, :125-129) and never touches RefPct, RefRebased or PricedBp. A base fault shifts every Priced by the same amount and leaves every mid untouched, so the guard is structurally incapable of seeing it - yet its own docstring lists 'a wrong re-base' beside a mis-rolled front and a phantom meeting as what it catches (FuturesGuard.cs:6-9), and the breach text tells the reader to treat a trigger as a roll/calendar/re-base fault (:87-88). Net effect on the day GBP Priced is 25bp out: the run notes carry 'futures guard MPC ok: ... (delta +0.0bp <= 2.5)', the strongest fault signal the app has, printed green.

**Against the existing suite.** 42 and 43 are the guard agreeing and disagreeing about the MIDS. This is the third case neither covers: mids perfect, base wrong, guard silent - and it is the case the docstring says is covered.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** FuturesGuard.cs:70/:97-102/:108-121/:125-129 provably blends only row.MidPct and never RefPct, and the docstring at :6-9 does claim "a wrong re-base" is caught — but MPC ships guardFuturesKind "imm3m" (config/meetings.json:186-187), so :70 calls CompoundedBlend, not the day-weighted average the scenario hand-derived. **Change before writing it:** Blend is the compounded imm3m figure at dcc 365, 3.6454, not the 3.6370 simple average — 0.84bp apart. Seed SFI at 96.3546 to keep the asserted note "implies 3.645 vs meeting blend 3.645 (Δ+0.0bp ≤ 2.5)"; at 96.363 the note reads Δ-0.8bp, still ok, so the finding survives either way, but the asserted text must match.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/FuturesGuard.cs:6` | `src/RateDesk.Core/FuturesGuard.cs:70` | `src/RateDesk.Core/FuturesGuard.cs:97` | `src/RateDesk.Core/FuturesGuard.cs:125` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:131` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:33`

## How the tickers roll

*Rolling generics, renumbering, and which contract a rung carried on a given day.*

### 114. After an unscheduled cut, w1/m1 read the next contract along on any week the tape actually moved

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** Scenarios 21/63/64 say an inter-meeting decision is safe because either the maturity record or the strip's own prices name the rung. On a tape that moves 3bp on one day, does either still answer?

**Set-up.** ECB, EESF{N}A composite, decisionTimeLondon 13:15, Cal.TimePassed. Ordinary grid: announcements/starts at D(-92)/D(-86), D(-43)/D(-37), then scheduled D(+20)/D(+26), D(+69)/D(+75), D(+118)/D(+124), D(+167)/D(+173) (all 49d apart, all lags 6 so LagIsStable is true). An UNSCHEDULED 50bp cut is announced TODAY, effective D(+6); the desk tops up config today, so Dates gains D(+6) and DecisionDates gains D(0). D(0)->D(+20) is 20 days, so both survive the 14-day cluster; D(+6) and D(+26) cluster into them. RecordMaturities = false — the live truth today, since recording only began 2026-08-26 and every 1w/1m anchor still reaches back past it. Store holds the 45-day price seed for rungs 1-6, raw-seeded per rung so the scenario owns the numbering. Levels flat for 45 days at P1 1.530, P2 1.650, P3 1.770, P4 1.890, P5 2.010, with ONE persistent +3bp repricing on every contract on D(-3) (a real, ordinary market day — not a spike, so Hygiene passes). Today's live quotes: rung1 = the emergency period 1.480, rung2 = P1 1.560, rung3 = P2 1.680, rung4 = P3 1.800, rung5 = P4 1.920.

**What must publish.** Published rows: P1 (+26) 1.560, P2 (+75) 1.680, P3 (+124) 1.800, P4 (+173) 1.920 — the emergency period is announced so the gate drops it. Every row: d1 = 0.0 (anchor D(-1), post-step, unchanged); w1 = anchor D(-7), pre-step: (1.560-1.530)x100 = +3.0 for P1, and +3.0 for each of P2/P3/P4 by the same 3bp step; m1 = anchor D(-30), also +3.0. So the honest ECB card reads d1 0.0 / w1 +3.0 / m1 +3.0 on all four rows.

**How it breaks.** w1 and m1 publish -9.0 on every row (d1 stays 0.0). Judge(D(-4),D(-3)) scores shift 0 with mean err 3.0bp; FitToleranceBp is 1.5, so the day returns Unknown (RungShiftScan.cs:167-170), and ShiftSince returns null for every anchor older than D(-3) because one unconfirmed day voids the whole chain (RungShiftScan.cs:196-199). With no record either, trueIdx stays null and `fixedWin.Add(p)` lets the calendar's idx stand (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1745). The topped-up calendar now counts the emergency boundary between D(-7) and each contract, so P1's D(-7) anchor is read at rung 2 = P2's own close 1.650 -> (1.560-1.650)x100 = -9.0; P2 reads rung 3 = 1.770 -> -9.0; P3 reads rung 4 = 1.890 -> -9.0; P4 reads rung 5 = 2.010 -> -9.0. AbsW1Bp is 30 and AbsM1Bp is 50, so OutlierGuard says nothing; every row moves alike so the cross-sectional arm says nothing; trueIdx was null so the 'tickers were not numbered the way the meeting calendar implies' note never fires. Blast, Runs.xlsx and email all read a coherent-looking -9bp week of easing right after an emergency cut, sign-flipped from a real +3bp. Note the internal tell the desk could catch: d1 0.0 beside w1 -9.0 on a week whose only move was 3bp.

**Against the existing suite.** Attacks the SAME mechanism as 63/64 from a new angle. 63/64 seed a tape that is perfectly flat except on the event day, which is the only regime in which RungShiftScan can return Confirmed; this adds one ordinary 3bp day and the price path goes dead. Also the live continuation of 21, whose record-based fix cannot reach any anchor older than 2026-08-26.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Verified the chain: RungShiftScan.cs:151-153 scores shift 0 over every rung so a uniform 3bp day gives mean err 3.0 > FitToleranceBp 1.5 (:167) and ShiftSince voids at :198, leaving PricingServiceBoards.cs:1745 to keep the calendar idx=2 read of 1.650 — w1/m1 -9.0 is right. But the D(-1) and D(-2)/D(-3) points DO get re-pointed (ShiftSince there = -1), so the note at PricingServiceBoards.cs:1753 fires — scenario 63 at Group17_PriceOnlyRungs.cs:127 asserts exactly that note in the same shape. **Change before writing it:** Drop the claim that the 'tickers were not numbered the way the meeting calendar implies' note never fires — it does, once per run, off the D(-1)..D(-3) re-point. Restate the tell as: the note fires and reads as normal-after-an-emergency while w1/m1 are silently wrong, and add NotesContain for it so the harness pins the behaviour.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/RungShiftScan.cs:63` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/RungShiftScan.cs:148` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/RungShiftScan.cs:151` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/RungShiftScan.cs:167` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/RungShiftScan.cs:196` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1558` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1590` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1739` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1745` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:23`

### 115. Morning after an MPC cut: records on two re-pointed rungs put -8.2bp of phantom easing on every row

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** MeetingRungMap says every recorded rung must agree on the offset or the evidence is refused. When only the rungs that HAVE re-pointed carry SW_EFF_DT, they agree — on the post-repoint numbering, while the mark being read is the 16:15 snap. What does the board publish?

**Set-up.** MPC, GPSF{N}A, decisionTimeLondon 12:00 (lag 0: Dates == DecisionDates, per README and the shipped config). Grid: D(-98), D(-49), decision+start B = D(-1) (25bp cut delivered yesterday at noon), then D(+48), D(+97), D(+139), D(+187). Every boundary 47-49 days apart, so no clustering and no mixed-state window (MeetingRungMap.cs:87-89 finds no start within 14d of any boundary). Tape quiet; the strip prices 8bp of further easing per meeting. Raw per-rung seeding, NOT ContractStep — the scenario is about the rung map. 16:15 snaps on D(-1), uniformly OLD-numbered (the documented GPSF 30-Jul-26 probe): GPSF1A 3.752, GPSF2A 3.672, GPSF3A 3.592, GPSF4A 3.512, GPSF5A 3.432. Live quotes today, feed fully re-pointed: GPSF0A eff D(-1) mat D(+48) mid 3.750; GPSF1A eff D(+48) mid 3.670; GPSF2A eff D(+97) mid 3.590; GPSF3A eff D(+139) mid 3.510; GPSF4A eff D(+187) mid 3.430. SONIA prints 3.750 today so Priced is clean and the scenario isolates d1. THE CRUX: hand-seed `Records` for D(-1) on rungs 1 and 2 ONLY — the ~17:00 daily run caught GPSF1A already pointing at D(+48) and GPSF2A at D(+97), while GPSF3A/4A/5A were price-only that evening (the price-only-far-rung shape documented at MeetingRungMap.cs:129-131).

**What must publish.** Published rows D(+48) 3.670, D(+97) 3.590, D(+139) 3.510, D(+187) 3.430 (the just-decided D(-1) period is announced, gate drops it). Each row's d1 is its own contract versus its own 16:15 snap yesterday: (3.670-3.672)x100 = -0.2; (3.590-3.592)x100 = -0.2; (3.510-3.512)x100 = -0.2; (3.430-3.432)x100 = -0.2. So d1 = -0.2 on all four rows. Control that must also pass: with RecordMaturities = false the naive path in MeetingRungMap.cs:160-168 steps back to D(-2), counts boundaries from there and answers rungs 2/3/4/5 — the correct -0.2 on every row. The records must not make the answer worse.

**How it breaks.** d1 publishes -8.2 on all four rows. For D(+48) the exact scan hits recorded(1,D(-1)) = D(+48) and returns rung 1 (MeetingRungMap.cs:120-123), so the stitcher's idx=2 window point at D(-1) is re-pointed to rung 1 = 3.752 -> (3.670-3.752)x100 = -8.2. For D(+139) the exact scan misses, and the offset calibration fires: rung 1 gives naive = boundaries in (D(-1), D(+48)] = 1, offset 0; rung 2 gives naive = 2, offset 0; consistent, so n1 = boundaries in (D(-1), D(+139)] + 0 = 3 (MeetingRungMap.cs:146-157). The window carrying D(-1) for that contract is idx=4, so the code drops GPSF4A's 3.512 and reads GPSF3A's 3.592 -> (3.510-3.592)x100 = -8.2. Same for D(+97) (reads rung 2, 3.672 -> -8.2) and D(+187) (reads rung 4, 3.512 -> -8.2). The idx=3 window also admits D(-1) as `lo` purely because HasRecordFor is true (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1704), so the same wrong value enters twice. -8.2 is under AbsD1Bp = 12 and identical across rows, so OutlierGuard's absolute and cross-sectional arms both stay silent; no note fires because the re-point 'source' note only prints once per run and reads as normal-after-an-unscheduled-meeting. Desk reads: GBP repriced 8bp lower overnight on a dead tape, on all four rows, the morning after an MPC cut.

**Against the existing suite.** New angle on 5 (feed already re-pointed) and 23 (renumber-day mid vs PrevClose): those seed a UNIFORM feed state and, via ContractStep, records for every rung that agree with it. No existing scenario seeds records for a SUBSET of rungs, which is what the offset-consistency test at MeetingRungMap.cs:142-150 cannot detect. Also the mirror image of 62 (guard arms disagreeing about the front) — here it is the record and the snap that disagree.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** Walked it: MeetingRungMap.cs:120-123 returns rung 1/2 off the two post-repoint records and MeetingRungMap.cs:142-157 calibrates offset 0 from those same two rungs, so D(+139)/D(+187) read rungs 3/4 — all four rows -8.2 against the old-numbered 16:15 snaps, under AbsD1Bp 12 (OutlierGuard.cs:23). The partial subset IS load-bearing: a complete record set that honestly reflected the staggered feed would break the offset-consistency test at :148 and fall through to the correct D(-2) step-back at :160.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingRungMap.cs:120` | `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingRungMap.cs:138` | `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingRungMap.cs:142` | `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingRungMap.cs:146` | `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingRungMap.cs:151` | `src/RateDesk.Core/MeetingRungMap.cs:160` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1704` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1733` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1736` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1745` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:278` | `tests/RateDesk.Scenarios/Harness/Spec.cs:72`

### 116. An extra Riksbank period 12 days after the last one vanishes into the 14-day cluster; w1 +18 vs +3

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** Both cluster sites justify 14 days on the invariant that no two meetings of one bank sit inside it. The tested invariant only covers Dates spacing. What happens the first time a real boundary lands 12 days after another?

**Set-up.** RIKSBANK, SKSF{N}A, rollsAtPeriodStart = true (boundaries are period STARTS, never announcements), trustConfigDates = true, markTurnPeriods = false. Period starts: D(-84), D(-42), D(-13), and an EXTRA period start at D(-1) — the Riksbank added a meeting and the new maintenance period began yesterday; the desk correctly entered it. Then D(+41), D(+83), D(+125), D(+167). D(-1) is 12 days after D(-13). SWESTR 1.750. Levels raw-seeded per rung, flat for the whole 45-day window at Q0 [D(-1),D(+41)) 2.000, Q1 [D(+41),D(+83)) 2.150, Q2 2.300, Q3 2.450, Q4 2.600 — 15bp apart, the ordinary SEK slope — plus one persistent +3bp repricing on every contract on D(-4). Records seeded only for D(-4)..D(0), matching a live store where recording began five days ago. The feed renumbered yesterday, at the period start, exactly as SKSF is probed to do.

**What must publish.** Published rows Q1 2.180, Q2 2.330, Q3 2.480 (post-step levels). Each row's w1 anchors on D(-7), which is pre-step and pre-roll: Q1 (2.180-2.150)x100 = +3.0; Q2 (2.330-2.300)x100 = +3.0; Q3 (2.480-2.450)x100 = +3.0. m1 likewise +3.0. d1 0.0. That is the whole truth of a fortnight in which the Riksbank held and the curve moved 3bp.

**How it breaks.** w1 and m1 publish +18.0 on every row. In MeetingRungMap.cs:52-56 the greedy cluster keeps D(-13) and DROPS D(-1) because (D(-1) - D(-13)) = 12 <= ClusterDays, so the boundary the family actually renumbered on does not exist in the map. The idx=1 window for Q1 therefore spans (D(-13), D(+41)], swallowing the whole fortnight, and D(-7) is read at rung 1 = Q0's own close 2.000 -> (2.180-2.000)x100 = +18.0. Q2's D(-7) anchor is read at rung 2 = Q1 2.150 -> +18.0; Q3's at rung 3 = Q2 2.300 -> +18.0. The one thing that could rescue it — ShiftSince, which WOULD Confirm the +1 on D(-1) — returns null because the 3bp day at D(-4) scores 3.0bp against FitToleranceBp 1.5 and voids the chain. Nothing warns: the drop is silent by construction (no note, no log); CalendarHealth check 1 pairs every live rung's SW_EFF_DT to a grid date and D(-1) IS in the grid, so it passes; check 3 finds a boundary inside the observation window (D(-1) is in `bounds`, which is the unclustered union) and explains the observed re-point away; AbsW1Bp = 30 and AbsM1Bp = 50 swallow +18.0; all rows agree so the cross-sectional arm stays quiet. The SEK card, the SEK dashboard and the movers hub all read a 15bp phantom hawkish repricing, and the movers |z| ranking will promote it because it is the largest 1w move on the board.

**Against the existing suite.** Same mechanism as 21 (emergency 12 days before a scheduled meeting) attacked from a new angle, stated explicitly: 21 is a DECISION-renumbering family where an announcement is inserted and the record path was built to catch it; this is the rollsAtPeriodStart family, where the boundary is a period START, the record window does not reach the anchor, and the inserted date is entered correctly by the desk. Distinct from 28-33, none of which puts two SEK boundaries inside 14 days. Raw per-rung seeding avoids the Hygiene collision trip at Market.cs:252, which is itself the finding.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** MeetingRungMap.cs:53-55 keeps the earliest of each cluster with `> ClusterDays`, so a period start 12 days after another is silently absent from Boundaries, and the idx=1 window at PricingServiceBoards.cs:1664-1706 then reads D(-7) off rung 1; +18.0 vs +3.0 checks out on both w1 and m1, and the config invariant at BasisGuardTests.cs:613-620 only asserts Dates spacing (and only `>= 14`, which the map's strict `> 14` already fails on an exact-14 gap). CalendarHealth.cs:44 skips effectives at or before asOf and :91-94 tests the unclustered union, so neither check sees the drop.
