### 117. Same SEK front contract, three rung maps: email 1.709, public dashboard 1.815, movers ranks the gap

`P0 - wrong number published | needs a desk call | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** MeetingRungMap is documented as THE one derivation and five call sites are named. Only one of them is handed the recorded evidence. On the day after a roll, do the desk email and the investor-facing dashboard publish the same number for the same contract?

**Set-up.** RIKSBANK, SKSF{N}A, rollsAtPeriodStart, markTurnPeriods, trustConfigDates — the live 27-Aug-26 shape. Period starts D(-84), D(-42), B = D(-1) (yesterday), then D(+35), D(+77), D(+119). The store's newest close is D(-1), so the weekly render's asOf = store.LatestDate() = D(-1), which IS the boundary. Store holds SKSF1A on D(-1) with effective D(+35) (the family renumbered at the period start, and the run recorded it), SKSF1A close on D(-1) = 1.715, SKSF2A close on D(-2) = 1.815, SKSF2A close on D(-1) = 1.860. Live SKSF1A mid today = 1.709, eff D(+35). Rungs 1-4 carry maturity records for D(-1); SKSF5A/6A are price-only.

**What must publish.** One number per contract per day, on every surface. Whichever rule the desk adopts for a boundary-day close, the D(+35) front row must carry the same mark on the blast, the email, the SEK dashboard page and the movers hub — and the app must not hold three positions at once.

**How it breaks.** Three answers, 10.6bp apart. (1) The email board takes the record path: HasRecordFor(D(-1)) is true, recorded(1,D(-1)) = D(+35), so RungFor returns 1 and Delta1d anchors on SKSF1A's 1.715 -> (1.709-1.715)x100 = -0.6. (2) The dashboard strip cannot: RollingStrip.ForMeetings builds `new MeetingRungMap(sched)` with no recordedEffective (RollingStrip.cs:223), and RolledValue never calls RungFor at all — it counts bounds itself (RollingStrip.cs:159) after walking asOf back one day because it is a boundary (RollingStrip.cs:153-154). idxThen for D(+35) from D(-2) is 2, so the published SEK front LEVEL is SKSF2A's 1.815. (3) The save-down history takes a third position, refusing the record deliberately (DailyBook.cs:161-170, and FINDINGS records the revert), so Historical_SEK carries 1.815 too. (4) MoverScan builds a FOURTH map, also record-less, with its own 14-day cluster and its own boundary count (MoverScan.cs:164-166, :180, :310), and ranks the 1w move computed off it — so the 10.6bp step can surface as the week's top SEK mover on the public hub. Desk-visible outcome: the email says the SEK front is 1.709 and moved -0.6bp; the public per-currency page says 1.815; a client reading both sees a 10.6bp discrepancy inside one publication.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 51-53 and 59, which check the strip on and after a decision day but seed records consistently via ContractStep and never compare the strip's number against the email's for the same contract; and adjacent to 57, which settled the save-down side only. The open question this forces is the one FINDINGS left open: does a run-time maturity record speak for a boundary-day close? The app currently answers yes on the boards, no in the save-down, and cannot answer at all on the public page.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** All four sites are as cited — RollingStrip.cs:223 builds the map with no recordedEffective, :153-154 steps asOf back off the boundary and :159 counts idxThen = 2, so the D(+35) row publishes SKSF2A's D(-2) close 1.815 while the boards take the record path (MeetingRungMap.cs:120-123) to 1.715/-0.6; DailyBook.cs:161-170 and MoverScan.cs:310 are record-less too. But (2), (3) and (4) all land on 1.815, so there are TWO numbers, not three. **Change before writing it:** Retitle and restate as two answers, not three: the email 1.709/-0.6 versus 1.815 on the dashboard, the save-down and the movers series. Also drop the 'inside one publication' framing — the boards run live today while the strip renders asOf = store.LatestDate() = D(-1) (MainWindow.xaml.cs:620); the defect is that the row LABELLED D(+35) carries the D(+77) contract's close, wrong on its own as-of.

### 118. Run pressed at 21:30 in Dubai: the rung record lands on the wrong day and un-masks a mid-window close

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | low confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** Maturity records are stamped with DateTime.Today, the machine's LOCAL date, while every gate reads the London clock. On a machine east of London, what does a record dated one day forward do to the mixed-state precaution?

**Set-up.** ECB, EESF{N}A, decisionTimeLondon 13:15. Announcement A = D(-6), period start S = D(0) (today), so MeetingRungMap masks D(-5)..D(-1) as mixed-state (MeetingRungMap.cs:84-92). The desk's second machine runs UTC+3 (the README names it) and the operator presses UPDATE at 21:30 local on D(-4) = 18:30 London. At that moment EESF1A and EESF2A have re-pointed but 3A/4A have not — the documented partial GPSF/EESF shape. Because DateTime.Today has already rolled locally, those records are written under date D(-3). No other run happens until today. Strip 12bp apart, quiet: contracts at 1.850 / 1.970 / 2.090 / 2.210, seeded raw per rung with the true renumber on D(-6)..D(-4) staggered by rung.

**What must publish.** D(-3) is a mixed-state day and nothing recorded that day describes it, so no close from D(-3) may source a value or an anchor (the desk rule of 2026-08-26). The board's d1 on the period-start day should therefore be BLANK, not a number measured against a mid-window close: the walk-back finds no clean day within the 5-day Delta1d cap once D(-5)..D(-1) are all excluded.

**How it breaks.** HasRecordFor(D(-3)) returns true off the mis-stamped row, so PricingServiceBoards.cs:1705 LIFTS the mixed-state exclusion for D(-3) and admits its close, then places it with RungFor(meeting, D(-3)) — a rung derived from a snapshot taken on D(-4) at 18:30 London, mid-renumber. For the row starting S+49 the calibration at MeetingRungMap.cs:138-157 is driven by the two rungs that HAD re-pointed on D(-4), so it answers one rung low, and the D(-3) close of the neighbouring contract (1.970 instead of 2.090) enters the series. Delta1d on the period-start day then publishes (2.090-1.970)x100 = +12.0 instead of blank — a full ECB inter-meeting step, sourced from exactly the day the mixed-state rule exists to exclude, and admitted only because a run in another timezone filed its evidence under the wrong date. Mirror failure on the same mechanism: HasRecordFor(D(-4)) is FALSE even though that run recorded D(-4)'s state, so the one day the desk does have evidence for is still treated as unattributable. Nothing warns; CalendarHealth never looks at record DATES, only at maturity CHANGES.

**Against the existing suite.** 14 sits inside the mixed-state window and 58 covers the marks-clock-vs-button-clock question for the DECISION GATES (MarksAsOfLondon). Neither covers the record WRITE clock: MarksAsOfLondon fixed the gates and left store.SetMaturity on DateTime.Today. No scenario writes a record under a day whose marks it does not describe.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The write clock is real (DailyBuilder.cs:279 and EmailBuilder.cs:140 stamp store.SetMaturity with DateTime.Today, HistoryStore.cs:206) and PricingServiceBoards.cs:1705 does lift the mixed-state exclusion on HasRecordFor, so a D(-3) close enters within the 5-day Δ1d cap (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286). But the stated cause is wrong: on D(-3) the calendar already counts the D(-6) boundary as passed, so two post-repoint records calibrate offset 0 at MeetingRungMap.cs:142-157 and RungFor returns the calendar's own rung, not one low. **Change before writing it:** Re-cut the break: the wrong number comes from admitting a mixed-state close whose FAR RUNGS were still old-numbered at that close, read at the post-repoint index — so the setup must state explicitly that 3A/4A are still old on D(-3)'s close (as written, 'renumber staggered over D(-6)..D(-4)' implies they had finished, which gives 0.0). Note also that the timezone mis-stamp is not required: a correctly-stamped record on any mixed-state day lifts the precaution identically, so make the mis-stamp the secondary arm (HasRecordFor(D(-4)) false) rather than the premise.

### 119. BOJ far rungs: the stitcher silently reads the retired JYOMPM root as if it were a JYSOMPM rung

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | moderate confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** ResolveMeetingDates refuses to merge the retired JYOMPM root's mid under a JYSOMPM date. FamilyHist substitutes the whole SERIES. Do the change columns know which family they are differencing?

**Set-up.** BOJ, tickers ['JYSOMPM{N}', 'JYOMPM{N}'] as shipped. Config: decision D(-52)/start D(-49), decision D(-4)/start D(-1), decision D(+45)/start D(+48), D(+94)/D(+97), D(+143)/D(+146), D(+192)/D(+195). LagIsStable false (gaps 3, 3, 3, 3, 3 here — set one pair to 1 day to reproduce the shipped 1-6 range). JYSOMPM quotes and stores rungs 1-3; JYSOMPM4A and 5A quote a mid but the store holds NO history for them, while the retired JYOMPM4/JYOMPM5 still carry stored closes — and JYOMPM stopped renumbering at the D(-49) roll, so JYOMPM4's series is the contract JYSOMPM3 now covers. Strip 12bp apart: front 0.680, then 0.800, 0.920, 1.040, 1.160. Quiet tape.

**What must publish.** A row whose rung has no history in the family the run is priced on must publish BLANK change columns — the hard-data rule ends the run where Bloomberg's documentation for THAT family ends. The D(+146) row should show d1/w1/m1 blank rather than a change measured against a different root's contract.

**How it breaks.** FamilyHist loops `foreach (var pat in sched.Tickers)`, so when JYSOMPM4A has no stored history it falls through to JYOMPM4 and returns that series AS rung 4 of the JYSOMPM family (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1627-1641). Nothing else follows it: `recorded` reads patRec = JYSOMPM only (:1545-1547), ObservedShifts scans patRec only (:1562), and RungToday probes patRec only (:1574-1586). So the rung map places a point using JYSOMPM's numbering and then reads JYOMPM's value at that index. With JYOMPM frozen one roll behind, the D(+146) row's 1w and 1m anchors come off the D(+97) contract: (1.040-0.920)x100 = +12.0bp of phantom repricing on a flat tape, on the JPY card, the Runs sheet and the email, with no note — and 12.0 sits exactly on AbsD1Bp's boundary and well under AbsW1Bp = 30, so the guards do not stop it. The live path already refuses this exact merge for MIDS, with an audit note saying why ('never merge ITS stale mid under THIS family's date', PricingServiceBoards.cs:669-673); the history path has no equivalent refusal, so the app publishes a mid it trusts and a change measured off a family it does not.

**Against the existing suite.** 8 is 'BOJ hikes today, non-contiguous family' and 56 is the unstable lag; both stay on the first pattern. The harness cannot express the second root: Market.TickerFor takes `sched.Tickers.FirstOrDefault(t => t.Contains("{N}"))` (Market.cs:19-24), so Closes/Snaps/Records can only ever land on JYSOMPM. Needs an explicit-ticker escape hatch on HistPointSpec (Extras already covers live quotes but not stored history).

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The mechanism is exactly as cited: PricingServiceBoards.cs:1627-1641 loops every sched.Tickers pattern so an empty JYSOMPM4 history falls through to JYOMPM4, while recorded (:1545), ObservedShifts (:1562) and RungToday (:1579) all key patRec only; harness limit is real too (Market.cs:19-24). But the setup's freeze point is self-contradictory — 'JYOMPM4 is the contract JYSOMPM3 now covers' makes the pre-roll rung-4 read CORRECT, and the +12.0 needs JYOMPM4 frozen one roll further back. **Change before writing it:** Fix the setup: JYOMPM must have missed the D(-49) roll, so JYOMPM4 is frozen on the D(+97) contract — the one JYSOMPM2 now covers, not JYSOMPM3. Then the D(+146) row's pre-roll idx=4 window reads 0.920 against its own 1.040 and the +12.0 follows.

### 120. The feed re-points BEFORE the statement — the one quadrant of the announcement-day 2x2 that nobody tests

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | derived by hand`

**The question.** Announcement day has four states: {announced, not announced} x {feed re-pointed, not re-pointed}. Scenarios 2, 4 and 5 cover three of them. What happens in the fourth?

**Set-up.** FOMC decision Wed 16-Sep-2026, decisionTimeLondon 19:00 (config/meetings.json:68), period start the same day (lag 0). DAILY pressed 12:00 London — seven hours before the statement. Bloomberg has ALREADY renumbered USSOFED overnight or early in the session: USSOFED1 now carries eff 16-Sep / mat 28-Oct where yesterday it carried eff 29-Jul / mat 16-Sep. Seeding: FEDL01 4.330; USSOFED1 mid 4.180 with PX_CLOSE_1D 4.330 (yesterday's close of the contract USSOFED1 pointed at THEN, i.e. the run-down period); USSOFED2 mid 4.050 PX_CLOSE_1D 4.180; USSOFED3 3.930 PX_CLOSE_1D 4.050. The market has not moved: every contract is unchanged day on day.

**What must publish.** A flat tape must publish a 1d change of 0.0 on every row. The correct change differences each contract against ITS OWN previous close, which after a re-point means mid(N) minus PrevClose(N+1): 4.180-4.180 = 0.0, 4.050-4.050 = 0.0, 3.930-3.930 = 0.0.

**How it breaks.** RollCorrectionDue (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1164) returns false. gateShift is 0 because Announced(16-Sep,'19:00',12:00) is false, so the first arm cannot fire; the second arm requires Announced() on today's announcement date and that is also false. So the correction is switched off precisely when the feed HAS re-pointed, and the naive mid(N) minus PrevClose(N) is used: 4.180-4.330 = -15.0, 4.050-4.180 = -13.0, 3.930-4.050 = -12.0. The blast publishes a 15bp rally on a day nothing traded, seven hours before the Fed speaks, and the OutlierGuard cross-sectional test cannot see it because every row moved together; only the 12bp absolute 1d bar catches the front row, and 13.0/12.0 sit under it. The code's premise — 'renumbering happens at the ANNOUNCEMENT' — is asserted from evidence of LATE re-pointing (EESF jumping between the 24-Jul and 27-Jul closes); the early direction was never probed.

**Against the existing suite.** Completes the 2x2 that 2 (announced, not re-pointed), 4 (not announced, not re-pointed) and 5 (announced, re-pointed) leave one cell short. Same mechanism, the untested cell.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Confirmed: with Announced(16-Sep,'19:00',12:00) false at DecisionClock.cs:37, RollCorrectionDue (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1163-1173) returns false on both arms, and PricingServiceWeekly.cs:314-316 publishes row.CoDBp because prevBd is clean for a lag-0 family — naive -15.0/-13.0/-12.0 on a flat tape, and config/meetings.json:68 is the 19:00 line as cited. The guard claim is wrong: OutlierGuard.cs:42 tests |v| > 12.0, so -13.0 flags as well as -15.0 and only -12.0 escapes. **Change before writing it:** Correct the guard paragraph: the CHECK/blocking note fires on TWO rows (-15.0 and -13.0), leaving only the -12.0 row silent. Also seed USSOFED4's PX_CLOSE_1D (3.930) so the third row's expected 0.0 is derivable from the market.

### 121. Operator switches SOURCES: the price-based rung scan goes blind because it never falls back to the composite

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | moderate confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** The SOURCES picker reroutes a run to a contributor. Does the strip's own-price rung derivation — the only safety net before the record window — survive the switch, and does it read one feed or two?

**Set-up.** RBA, ADSF{N}A, shipped source NABZ. Today is a Monday; last Wednesday the operator opened SOURCES and moved RBA from the composite to NABZ (or from NABZ to a second contributor — either direction). The store therefore holds 45 days of composite closes on 'ADSF1A Curncy'..'ADSF6A Curncy' and only three days of 'ADSF1A NABZ Curncy'..'ADSF6A NABZ Curncy'. Calendar: RBA decisions D(-8)/start D(-7) and D(+41)/start D(+42), D(+90)/D(+91) (lag 1, stable). RecordMaturities = false for days before D(-3). Levels raw-seeded on the COMPOSITE spelling only (Spell.Composite), 10bp apart: front 3.600, next 3.500, next 3.400, next 3.300; flat except a persistent -4bp on D(-2). Live quotes on both spellings.

**What must publish.** The change columns must either be right or blank. Because the run's ACTIVE feed has almost no history, every consumer already falls back to the composite series per rung (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1636-1640 for the stitcher, :1546-1547 for the records, DailyBook.cs:148-150, RollingStrip.cs:191-196), so w1/m1 should come out of the composite closes and read 0.0 for the quiet stretch and -4.0 across D(-2).

**How it breaks.** The one consumer with no fallback is the rung scan. ObservedShifts is bound with `n => MeetingTick(sched, patRec, n)` — the ACTIVE spelling only, no composite arm (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1562) — while the liveMid lambda three lines below DOES fall back to the composite (:1564-1567). Two failures follow. (a) If 'ADSF1A NABZ Curncy' has no stored closes at all, RungShiftScan.cs:105 hits `if (day.Count == 0) break;` at n=1 and byRung comes back EMPTY: Judge scores nothing, every day is Unknown, ShiftSince is null for every anchor, and the calendar governs unconditionally for as long as the contributor history stays thin — i.e. the app's only pre-record protection against an inserted meeting is switched off by a UI action, silently, with no note anywhere. (b) With three days of contributor history the scan builds a MIXED series: `store.GetDaily(contributor)` for the closes and, where the contributor's live mid is missing, the COMPOSITE mid for today (:1564-1567). The composite/NABZ basis is measured at 1.7bp on ADSF2A (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1631-1633) and FitToleranceBp is 1.5bp, so that basis alone flips today's judgement between Confirmed and Unknown depending on which spelling answered — and on a roll day it can make a wrong hypothesis fit better than the right one. Desk-visible outcome: after the SOURCES change the AUD card's w1/m1 columns silently stop being roll-protected; the first inserted or moved meeting then prints a full 10bp inter-meeting step on every AUD row, and nothing on the blast, the workbook or the dashboards says the switch caused it.

**Against the existing suite.** 34 covers contributor vs composite for the MIDS and 37 covers a stale feed; neither touches the rung scan's spelling, and no scenario models a store where the active spelling has less history than the composite — which is the state of every run for a week after any SOURCES change. Spell.Composite in Spec.cs:10 already makes it seedable.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The asymmetry is real and precisely as cited: PricingServiceBoards.cs:1562 binds ObservedShifts to the ACTIVE spelling with no composite arm while :1546-1547, :1564-1567, :1636-1640, DailyBook.cs:148-150 and RollingStrip.cs:191-196 all fall back. But arm (a)'s cited line cannot be reached — RungShiftScan.cs:104 writes today's live mid into `day` before the `day.Count == 0` break at :105, and the liveMid lambda answers off the active spelling, so byRung is populated and every day is Unknown via the empty-scored path at :156-158 instead. **Change before writing it:** Re-cite arm (a): the scan is blind because Judge finds no value on either past day (RungShiftScan.cs:122-123 -> :156), not because of the break at :105. And arm (b) contradicts the setup — it needs the CONTRIBUTOR live mid MISSING for the composite mid at :1564-1567 to enter, so drop 'Live quotes on both spellings' for that arm.

## The change columns

*Where the 1d, 1w and 1m anchors land, and what they are differenced against.*

### 122. SEK tape stops 19 days back: the email blanks the 1w, the public dashboard prints "+0.0"

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** The email's change columns refuse to publish a stale anchor. The 28 investor-facing dashboards run a SECOND anchor derivation with no cap at all. On a day one family's closes stop and the rest of the store is current, do the two surfaces published by the same button agree?

**Set-up.** Two banks, one run. RIKSBANK (SKSF{N}A, rollsAtPeriodStart, composite): store closes on rungs 1-4 over D(-45)..D(-19) at 1.900, and a mark on the D(-32) business day at 1.750; NOTHING after D(-19) (the last three weeks of BDH never landed - exactly scenario 24's shape). Live quotes are fresh (mid rung1 2.050, PrevClose 2.045, AgeMinutes 5) and no Riksbank period start falls inside D(-40)..D(0). FOMC is seeded alongside with closes running to D(-1), so store.LatestDate() = D(-1), which is what the render path uses as asOf (MainWindow.xaml.cs:620) and what the harness already mirrors (Surfaces.cs:156-161). Nothing is faked but Bloomberg.

**What must publish.** The dashboard strip for the Riksbank should either blank its 1w/1m or mark the row stale, because its own newest close is 18 days older than the page's stamped as-of. Email side (the correct behaviour, for contrast): Δ1w target D(-8), last stitched point D(-19), gap 11 > 7 => BLANK (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287, 528-537). Δ1m target ~D(-31), a mark sits at D(-32), gap 1 <= 10 => (2.050-1.750)*100 = +30.0. So the honest dashboard answer for 1w is blank (an em-dash), and for 1m +15.0 at most - never 0.0.

**How it breaks.** The dashboard publishes level 1.900, 1w "+0.0", 1m "+15.0" under a page header stamped as-of D(-1). Branch: RollingStrip.cs:98-99 calls RolledValue for asOf-7 and MonthAgo(asOf); RolledValue (RollingStrip.cs:147-171) ends at store.ValueAsOf, which is a bare "SELECT value ... WHERE date<=@d ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT 1" with no lower bound at all (HistoryStore.cs:422-434). Both the level read (asOf = D(-1)) and the 1w read (D(-8)) resolve to the SAME D(-19) close, so LadderPoint.W1Bp = (1.900-1.900)*100 = 0 (Panels.cs:15). Panels.BpCell formats it with a TWO-section custom format "+0.0;-0.0" (Panels.cs:82), and .NET applies the positive section to zero, so the cell literally reads "+0.0" - which the desk's own formatting invariant forbids on every other surface (Invariants.cs:256-258). An external client reads "Swedish front unchanged on the week" off a page whose numbers are nineteen days old, on the same day the desk's email declined to publish a 1w at all. Nothing in ForMeetings tests price freshness: the only truncation gate is the maturity-record day (RollingStrip.cs:236-252), which a uniformly-unrecorded family passes.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 24 (the staleness cap, email only) and to 51/52/53/59 (dashboard strips, all on DECISION days with a current tape). New angle: the same three-week hole 24 uses, aimed at the dashboard's independent anchor derivation, which was never given 24's cap. Also adjacent to the open FINDINGS entry "save-down history carries the previous close across a roll" - same failure shape ("reads back as the market did not move"), different surface and a different cause (no cap, not a walk-back stamp).

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Cites check out: RollingStrip.cs:98-99 anchors via RolledValue → HistoryStore.cs:422-434 ValueAsOf, an unbounded "date<=@d ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT 1", and Panels.cs:82's two-section "+0.0;-0.0" does render zero as "+0.0"; Surfaces.cs:159-161 builds the strip so it is testable. But the setup's isolated 15bp D(-32) mark among 1.900s is exactly the shape Hygiene.Scan refuses (Market.cs:272-277, bump 15 > 4.4 with neighbourGap 0), so it will be rejected as an authoring error. **Change before writing it:** Drop the lone D(-32) 1.750 mark (or make it a persistent level step): the 1w "+0.0" is the whole finding and reads stronger with the family uniformly flat at 1.900 to D(-19), which also makes 1m read +0.0.

### 123. A rung that last printed 12 days ago: Δ1d publishes the 12-day move because PX_CLOSE_1D carries no date

`P0 - wrong number published | needs a desk call | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** When the stitched series refuses a Δ1d as too stale, the row falls back to its own change-on-day. That fallback differences the live mid against PX_CLOSE_1D. Is anything checking how old PX_CLOSE_1D is?

**Set-up.** NORGES (NKSF{N}A, composite, refTicker NOWA Index), no announcement or period start inside D(-40)..D(+15), so no boundary or mixed-state day sits near any anchor. NOWA = 3.500 flat. Store closes on rungs 1-4 run D(-45)..D(-12) FLAT at 3.450 and stop there - the market was shut (Christmas/Golden Week) or the rungs quote episodically, which this repo documents as normal ("a ticker that legitimately doesn't print daily (gappy BGN quoting)", StoreBackedHistory.cs:23-25). The market has reopened today and gapped: live quotes Quote(1, mid 3.370, prevClose 3.450), Quote(2, mid 3.320, prevClose 3.400), Quote(3, mid 3.270, prevClose 3.350) - i.e. every rung is 8bp lower than its last real close, and Bloomberg's PX_CLOSE_1D for each is that 12-day-old close. Quote ages are 5 minutes (fresh - no STALE line will fire).

**What must publish.** Δ1d must be BLANK on every row. The last observation of any kind is D(-12); the change from it to now is a twelve-day change, and the app's own rule already says so - ChangeToBp with maxStaleDays 5 rejects it because (D(-1) - D(-12)) = 11 > 5 (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286, 534). Δ1w publishes: target D(-7), last point D(-12), gap 5 <= 7 => (3.370-3.450)*100 = -8.0. Δ1m publishes: target ~D(-31), a point sits there => -8.0. So the honest run reads d1 blank, w1 -8.0, m1 -8.0 on row 1, and the desk sees one move booked once.

**How it breaks.** The blast, the Runs sheet and the sheet email all print NORGES d1 -8.0, w1 -8.0, m1 -8.0 on every row - three columns, one move, and the Δ1d column asserts the whole twelve-day gap happened overnight. Branch: PricingServiceWeekly.cs:315-316, `wm.D1Bp ??= codClean && (row.MidSource == "ticker" ...) ? row.CoDBp : null`. `codClean` (line 314) tests ONLY `!rungMap.IsBoundary(prevBd) && !rungMap.IsMixedState(prevBd)` - it never asks how old the close behind CoDBp is, and it cannot: QuoteData.PrevClose is a bare double with no date field (RatesSnapshot.cs:12-13) and CoDBp is just (Mid - PrevClose)*100 (RatesSnapshot.cs:38). The comment block at 291-307 closed exactly one back door (mixed-state closes re-admitted through the fallback) and left the staleness one open. No guard catches it either: OutlierGuard's absolute Δ1d bar is 12bp so -8.0 passes (OutlierGuard.cs:23), and the cross-sectional test needs a row that differs from the run median - here the whole strip falls back uniformly, so the median IS -8.0 and nothing flags (OutlierGuard.cs:46-58). The >1h STALE line reads the live quote's AgeMinutes, which is 5 minutes on the day the market reopens (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1073-1074). Silent. Fixing it needs a DATE on the previous close (PX_CLOSE_DT, or HistoryStore.LastDate for the resolved rung ticker), which is a data-collection change and a desk call, not a one-line guard.

**Against the existing suite.** Directly overlaps 24, which ASSERTS this fallback as correct (+0.5) on a 19-day store hole, and 27, which asserts it for a rung with no history. It differs on the one thing that matters: in 24 and 27 the hole is APP-SIDE (the store missed a fetch) so PX_CLOSE_1D really is yesterday's close and the fallback is right; here the hole is in the MARKET, so PX_CLOSE_1D is twelve days old and the same code path is wrong. The product cannot tell the two apart. Also sharpens the brief's "d1 and w1 print identical numbers with no note": with the caps as written the two series anchors can never coincide (d1's anchor lies in [today-6, today-1], w1's at or before today-7), so this fallback is the ONLY route to that signature.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** PricingServiceWeekly.cs:314-316 gates the fallback on IsBoundary/IsMixedState only, and RatesSnapshot.cs:12-13/38 carries no date on PrevClose, so the age of PX_CLOSE_1D is genuinely unknowable — confirmed. But Group05_ChangeColumns.cs:506-512 (scenario 24) asserts the same code path is CORRECT (+0.5) on inputs the product cannot distinguish from these, so no implementation can satisfy both: which behaviour is right is a desk/data-collection call, not a defect with a fix.

### 124. FOMC statement run on a GMT+3 box: DecisionClock reads London, every change anchor reads local, one day apart

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | moderate confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** The FOMC announces at 19:00 London - 22:00 on this machine's clock. Once the local date has rolled and London's has not, does the run gate on one calendar and anchor its change columns on another?

**Set-up.** A Windows box three hours east of London (the scenario harness README records this machine as UTC+3). The operator presses UPDATE at 22:10 local on Wednesday 9-Sep, i.e. 19:10 London, ten minutes after the FOMC statement. DateTime.Today is therefore Thursday 10-Sep while DecisionClock.LondonNow() is 9-Sep 19:10. Bank: ECB (EESF{N}A, composite, decisionTimeLondon 13:15). The ECB cut 25bp on Thursday 3-Sep; the Dec-26 EESF contract traded 2.200 flat through Wednesday 2-Sep, printed 1.960 in the 16:15 snap of Thursday 3-Sep and has held 1.950 since. Store closes and snaps to 8-Sep; live mid 1.950. (The FOMC is only the trigger - it is what forces the button to be pressed after 21:00 local once a month.)

**What must publish.** With today = Wednesday 9-Sep London: Δ1w target Wednesday 2-Sep, a clean pre-announcement day, anchor 2.200 => (1.950 - 2.200)*100 = -25.0. The delivered cut is inside the week and the desk reads it there. Δ1d target Tuesday 8-Sep, anchor 1.950 => 0.0. Δ1m target 9-Aug, pre-announcement, => -25.0.

**How it breaks.** The blast goes out saying ECB Δ1w -1.0 on a week in which the ECB cut 25bp. Branch: PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287 computes the target as DateTime.Today.AddDays(-7), and DateTime.Today is the machine's LOCAL date, so the target becomes Thursday 3-Sep - the announcement day itself. That day is a boundary, admitted because it is snapped (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1706, the snapped-boundary rule), and its 16:15 snap is post-13:15, so the anchor is the POST-cut 1.960: Δ1w = (1.950-1.960)*100 = -1.0, a 24bp error. Δ1d breaks the other way: target = DateTime.Today.AddDays(-1) = Wednesday 9-Sep = the real London today, whose close the store excludes by design, so the walk lands on Tuesday 8-Sep and the Δ1d column publishes a TWO-day change (gap 1 <= 5, so the cap never fires). Meanwhile the front roll and every announcement gate are correct, because they read DecisionClock.LondonNow (DecisionClock.cs:21-29, 35-40) - so one half of the run is on the London calendar and the other half is a day ahead of it, and no surface says so. There is no local-vs-London reconciliation anywhere in the product: a grep for a guard finds none, and DateTime.Today appears raw at PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286-288, 308, 431-432, 507-508 and in WeeklyCurves/RollingStrip's asOf path. Same box, same market, 20:50 local vs 22:10 local: two different Δ1w.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 58 ("FOMC day: 16:15 close precedes 19:00 statement"), which pins the ROLL against the marks' clock via MarksAsOfLondon. New angle: 58 fixes what the run rolls; nothing pins what the change anchors count from, and the two now read different calendars on the one run per month that is guaranteed to be pressed after 21:00 local. Also the mirror image of the harness's own recorded limit (tests/RateDesk.Scenarios/README.md, "DateTime.Today is the machine's LOCAL date... anything run after 21:00 local time is a day ahead") - recorded there as a harness limit, never as a product one.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The divergence is real — PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286-288 and :308 use raw DateTime.Today while DecisionClock.cs:21-29 reads London, and nothing reconciles them (only DailyBuilder.cs:230 has a London-dated equivalent). But the clock arithmetic is wrong: in September London is BST, so a UTC+3 box is two hours ahead and the local date rolls at 22:00 London — 22:10 local is 20:10 London with DateTime.Today still 9-Sep, so the FOMC's 19:00 statement (config/meetings.json:68) does not deliver the premise. **Change before writing it:** Set the press time at or after 22:00 London (00:00+ local) — the FOMC is not what forces it; and drop the Δ1d half of the break: on this flat tape both 8-Sep and 9-Sep targets resolve to 1.950, so Δ1d is 0.0 either way and nothing breaks there.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:288` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:308` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:521` | `src/RateDesk.Core/Dates/DecisionClock.cs:21` | `src/RateDesk.Core/Dates/DecisionClock.cs:35` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1704` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:269` | `config/meetings.json:68` | `tests/RateDesk.Scenarios/Harness/Cal.cs:12`

### 125. A bad 16:15 snap next to the Riksbank Y/E turn: the stitcher's guard claims a turn stand-down it does not have

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** The stitched series' misprint guard is documented as carrying the live guard's TWO exemptions - the front row and a Y/E turn. Does the code carry the turn one, and if not, what does a turn-adjacent rung's anchor publish?

**Set-up.** RIKSBANK, real shipped shape (SKSF{N}A, rollsAtPeriodStart, markTurnPeriods, trustConfigDates; boundaries are the period STARTS). Period grid, using the harness's ToYe anchor so a period really straddles 31-Dec: PS1 = D(-40), S0 = D(6), SA = D(ToYe-66), TS = D(ToYe-24) (the TURN period, spans 31-Dec), TE = D(ToYe+18), SC = D(ToYe+60). SWESTR = 1.700. Live mids: rung1 (S0) 1.750, rung2 (SA) 1.800, rung3 (TS, the turn) 1.300 - the documented SWESTR year-end dislocation (config/meetings.json:510-513 records the Dec-26 rung printing ~1.47 against ~1.93 fair), rung4 (TE) 1.850, rung5 (SC) 1.900. History: every rung FLAT at its live level over D(-40)..D(-1) except rung 4, whose 16:15 SNAPS on the three business days D(-9), D(-8), D(-7) read 1.300 - a bad tick that printed twice and landed on the last bar, the exact mechanism the 2026-08-31 FINDINGS entry documents for BPSWIF3. Rung 4's CLOSES stay at 1.850 throughout. Seeded as a persistent 3-day snap bump so the harness's own Hygiene scan passes it (bump = min of the two neighbour gaps = 0 on each bad day).

**What must publish.** Row TE's Δ1w must be 0.0. The point on D(-7) sits 55bp below both its rung neighbours' agreeing level, so the stitcher's guard should WITHHOLD it ("the guard WITHHOLDS rather than substitutes: the point is dropped ... so the lookback walks back to the last clean day", PricingServiceBoards.cs:1775-1777) and the anchor steps to D(-10) at 1.850 => (1.850 - 1.850)*100 = 0.0. Rows S0, SA and SC read 0.0 too; TS is a turn row and publishes no numbers at all.

**How it breaks.** The TE row publishes Mid 1.850 and Δ1w +55.0 on a week in which nothing moved: (1.850 - 1.300)*100. Branch: the guard's gate at PricingServiceBoards.cs:1780 is `loN != null && hiN != null && idx - 1 >= 1 && meeting.Date != frontMeeting?.Date` - there is no TurnAt test anywhere in MeetingSeriesBuilder (grep over lines 1520-1795 finds "turn" only inside the comment at 1773-1774 that claims the exemption exists). For contract TE the recent window resolves to idx = 4, so loN = FamilyHist(3) = the TURN rung at 1.300 and hiN = FamilyHist(5) = 1.900; |a-b|*100 = 60 > 25, so the neighbours-agree precondition at line 1786 fails, the guard stands down, and the 1.300 misprint stays in the window. The live guard was fixed for exactly this - RejectedBy steps OVER a turn neighbour (`if (lo >= 1 && TurnAt(lo)) lo--; if (hi < tickMid.Length && TurnAt(hi)) hi++;`, PricingServiceBoards.cs:1027-1028) after the audit found "SKSF4A - the guard's own motivating misprint - is turn-ADJACENT, and a turn neighbour disabled the guard exactly there" (1014-1015). The stitcher's copy never got it. Nothing else stops the number: the Hampel despike is bypassed entirely on this path because snaps overwrite the despiked closes and are themselves never filtered (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1645-1656), so the guard is the ONLY withholding defence. OutlierGuard does raise a CHECK (+55.0 > the 30bp Δ1w bar, OutlierGuard.cs:23) - but scenario 23 already establishes that a correct decision-day run earns a CHECK on every row, so CHECK is noise, not a gate, and the wrong number publishes on the blast, the workbook, the sheet email and the SEK dashboard regardless.

**Against the existing suite.** Same guard as 62, which pinned the FRONT-row exemption (and produced the fix at line 1780). This is the OTHER exemption in the same comment, still unimplemented - and it is the one the live guard's own audit trail says matters most, because the Riksbank turn sits permanently next to two published rows. Also adjacent to 31/33 (turn rows and the step chain), which test the turn's own row, never its neighbour's anchor.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The core claim holds: PricingServiceBoards.cs:1780 gates on front-row only with no TurnAt, while the live guard steps over a turn neighbour at :1027-1028, and "turn" appears in the stitcher region only in the :1773-1774 comment. But the +55.0 clears OutlierGuard.cs:23 (AbsW1Bp 30) as the run's ONLY CHECK on this quiet tape, and CHECK notes gate distribution (MainWindow.xaml.cs:791, "DAILY CANCELLED — CHECK notes declined"), so it is a warned row, not a silent publish. **Change before writing it:** Drop the "SEK dashboard" from the break surfaces: RollingStrip reads store closes via ValueAsOf and never merges the 16:15 snaps, so the dashboard strip anchors on the clean 1.850 and is unaffected.

### 126. Same market, two machines: the mixed-state record lift moves the ECB Δ1w by 10bp, and the same run's Historical_EU disagrees

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | moderate confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** A recorded SW_EFF_DT lifts the mixed-state mask for the email's anchors but not for the save-down history walk. Does the number the desk publishes depend on which machine ran it, and does the run contradict its own history tab?

**Set-up.** ECB (EESF{N}A, composite, decisionTimeLondon 13:15). Boundaries: A_prev = D(-51), A = D(-9) (a 25bp cut, delivered), the decided period starting S = D(-3), next announcement A1 = D(+27) with start S1 = D(+33). Mixed-state window is therefore D(-8)..D(-4) (MeetingRungMap.cs:84-92). Front published row is the period starting S1 = D(+33) - it sits on rung 1 today and on rung 2 before the announcement. Its level: 2.200 flat to D(-10); the 16:15 snap on D(-9) reads 2.060 (post-13:15, dovish presser half digested); 1.960 from D(-8) onward; live mid 1.950. Run the SAME BankSpec twice: once with RecordMaturities = true (machine A, recording for months - a maturity record for every rung on every day) and once with RecordMaturities = false (machine B, restored from a StoreBackup snapshot last week, prices seeded but no historical records). The harness models exactly this distinction already (Spec.cs:56-59).

**What must publish.** Both machines must publish the same Δ1w, or the surface must say which regime produced it. On the evidence-lifted read (machine A) the target D(-8)... strictly the Δ1w target is D(-7), a mixed-state day the record admits, value 1.960 => (1.950 - 1.960)*100 = -1.0. That is the intended answer: the record settles the numbering, so the day is usable. Whatever the desk decides, the two runs must not differ by 10bp in silence, and the run's own Historical_EU tab must not contradict the Δ1w on its front row.

**How it breaks.** Machine A's blast prints ECB Δ1w -1.0; machine B's prints -11.0, on the same market on the same day, and neither carries a note. Branch: PricingServiceBoards.cs:1704-1706, `(!rungMap.IsMixedState(p.Date) || rungMap.HasRecordFor(p.Date))`. On machine A HasRecordFor(D(-7)) is true, the day is admitted and the re-point at 1733-1738 places it, giving 1.960. On machine B HasRecordFor is false for the whole D(-8)..D(-4) window, every day is masked, and the walk falls to the D(-9) snap admitted at the boundary under the OLD numbering (rung 2 at 2.060), giving -11.0. Second half, inside ONE run: the save-down walk has no record lift at all - DailyBook.cs:169-170 defines `Unusable(d) => map.IsBoundary(d) || map.IsMixedState(d)` unconditionally (with the 2026-08-28 comment at 161-168 explaining that the lift was tried and reverted THERE), so on machine A the Historical_EU table anchors on 2.060 while the email row beside it anchors on 1.960: the same report asserts both -1.0 and -11.0 for the same contract over the same week. The Δ1d cap differs too - DailyBook.cs:184-186 applies a flat 10 days to all three horizons where the email uses 5/7/10 - so the history tab will publish a Δ1d the email blanked.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 14 ("ECB hiked 3 days ago, run inside the mixed-state window") and 57 (save-down history across a decision), and it is the other side of the open FINDINGS entry "save-down history carries the previous close across a roll" - that entry argues the save-down should NOT take the record lift, and this scenario accepts that and asks the question it leaves open: the email's anchors DID take the lift, so the two surfaces of one report now disagree, and the email's number is not reproducible on a freshly restored machine. New angle: run the same spec twice on RecordMaturities and assert equality (or a note), rather than testing one machine state.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The RecordMaturities dependence is real (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1704-1706 lifts the mixed-state mask on HasRecordFor; Spec.cs:56-59 models both machines), but the save-down half is misderived: DailyBook.cs:169-170's Unusable covers IsBoundary too, so the walk steps over the D(-9) announcement to D(-10) at 2.200 — never the 2.060 snap. Both -1.0 and -11.0 are honest reads of documented marks under different admissibility rules, and FINDINGS already owns the save-down walk, so what is new here is silent irreproducibility, not a wrong number. **Change before writing it:** Fix the history-tab arithmetic to anchor D(-10) 2.200 (Δ1w -24.0, not -11.0) and stop comparing it cell-for-cell with the email: DailyBook measures that day's close against its own day-7, the email measures the live mid — different quantities by design. Assert equality of the two RecordMaturities runs' email Δ1w, or a note naming the regime.

### 127. A rung quiet for three weeks reopens: the movers hub ranks its 23-day move as a +22bp 1w event at 2.6 sigma

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** The email blanks a 1w whose anchor is too old. The movers hub - the site's landing page - has its own anchor walk. Does it cap it, or does it hand a stale gap a sigma and put it top of the DM table?

**Set-up.** RIKSBANK (SKSF{N}A). One published strip contract, say the Nov-26 period. Store closes for its rung: every business day D(-70)..D(-24) FLAT at 1.760 (about 33 prints), then NOTHING, then prints on D(-2) and D(-1) at 1.980 - the market repriced across a gap in the tape. No Riksbank period start inside D(-24)..D(0), so no boundary or mixed-state day is involved. Other tickers keep the store current so asOf = store.LatestDate() = D(-1) (MainWindow.xaml.cs:620, 650).

**What must publish.** Unrankable, and excluded. There is no observation between D(-24) and D(-2), so there is no 1w anchor: MoverScan should return null for the series exactly as ChangeToBp blanks the email's Δ1w (target D(-8), last stitched point D(-24), gap 16 > 7 => BLANK). The hub's own methodology line already promises this discipline - "closes older than 4 days ... are excluded" (MoverScan.cs:369).

**How it breaks.** index.html publishes "RIKSBANK <Nov-26> 1.980 +22.0bp (2.6 sigma est)" and, ranked by |z|, it lands at or near the top of the DM table and possibly on a hero card. Arithmetic: MoverScan.cs:79 gates only the LATEST point - (asOf - h[^1].Date) = 0 <= StaleDays 4 - and then At() at :83-86 walks back with no bound on the gap to the target. now = At(D(-1)) = 1.980; At(D(-8)) walks past the hole to D(-24) = 1.760; w1 = (1.980-1.760)*100 = +22.0 over 23 calendar days (:89-90). The sigma is built from the same holed series: 34 daily diffs, 33 of them zero and one of +22.0, so sd = sqrt((33*0.647^2 + (22-0.647)^2)/33) = 3.77bp, sigmaEst = 3.77*sqrt(5) = 8.44bp (:99-101); the strict weekly sigma is unavailable because the marks walk breaks at k=11 against MinWeeklyObs 40 (:116-127), so z = 22.0/8.44 = 2.61 and ZIsEst = true. The despike makes it worse, not better: HistoryFilter is INDEX-based, not date-aware, so at the D(-2) point the +-5-index window spans 22 calendar days, mad = 0, thr = 4.45bp and dev = 22bp > 2.5*thr, so the reopening print is rewritten to 1.760 (HistoryFilter.cs:36, 44) and the whole 22bp lands in the single last diff. Same page, same run: the desk's email says "no 1w for SEK", the public hub says "biggest DM mover of the week, 2.6 sigma".

**Against the existing suite.** No existing scenario touches MoverScan's anchors (the catalogue's dashboard group, 51-53/59, covers the per-currency strips only). Adjacent to 24 in mechanism - the same cap that 24 proves exists on the email - asked of the surface that never received it, and where the answer is amplified by being divided by a sigma.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Every step verifies: MoverScan.cs:79 caps only h[^1]'s age, At() at :83-86 walks back unbounded, :116-127 breaks the strict-sigma loop long before MinWeeklyObs 40, and HistoryFilter.cs:28 iterates 1..Length-2 so the D(-2) reopening print is rewritten to the median while D(-1) survives — dev 22bp > 2.5×4.448bp at :44. A public landing page printing "+22.0bp" and "2.6 sigma" for a 23-day gap, then ranking on it, is a wrong number on a published surface, not a missing note — the same fault class the author scored P0 in 122.

### 128. A genuine 5bp one-day round trip on a snapless rung is erased, and the 1w anchor loses it

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | moderate confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** The Hampel filter runs on every stored close before any anchor reads it. On a dead-flat contract its effective ceiling is 4.45bp. What happens when a real one-day move of 5bp lands exactly on the 1w anchor day and the rung has no intraday bars to overwrite it?

**Set-up.** RBA (ADSF{N}A on NABZ - a contributor price page, which typically publishes no TRADE bars, so GetLondonSnaps returns nothing for it). No announcement or period start in D(-40)..D(+10). Store closes for the rung carrying the second published contract: 2.000 on every business day D(-40)..D(-1), EXCEPT the business day that D(-7) resolves to, which closed at 2.050 - a real one-day repricing on an Australian CPI print that fully retraced the next day. No snaps are seeded for that rung at all. Live mid today 2.000, PrevClose 2.000. Neighbouring rungs are 15bp apart so a mis-rung read would be unmistakable.

**What must publish.** Δ1w = -5.0. The desk's own reconciliation source is the 16:15 mark on that day, which was 2.050; live 2.000 against it is (2.000 - 2.050)*100 = -5.0. Δ1d = 0.0 (yesterday closed 2.000), Δ1m = 0.0.

**How it breaks.** The blast prints RBA Δ1w 0.0 - "unchanged on the week" - while the incumbent sheet, which stores the raw 16:15 print, shows -5.0, and the desk's reconciliation is 5bp out with nothing to look at. Branch: the family's close series is served through Hist(tkr, full:true) -> HistStats -> HistoryFilter.Despike with the defaults window 5, k 6, madFloorPct 0.005 (PricingServiceAnalytics.cs:42-59, HistoryFilter.cs:17-18). At the spike index the +-5 window holds ten neighbours all at 2.000, so med = 2.000 and mad = 0; thr = 6 * 1.4826 * max(0, 0.005) = 0.044478 percent = 4.4478bp (HistoryFilter.cs:36). dev = |2.050-2.000| = 5.0bp > thr, and neighbourGap = |2.000-2.000| = 0 < thr, so the classic-spike arm fires and the point is overwritten with the median 2.000 (HistoryFilter.cs:43-47). ChangeToBp then differences 2.000 against 2.000 and prints 0.0 (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287, 535). The filter is only reachable here because the rung has no snap: the merge at PricingServiceBoards.cs:1645-1656 overwrites despiked closes with 16:15 snaps for the last 50 days, and the `if (snaps.Count == 0)` branch at 1651 hands back the despiked closes wholesale. That happens for a contributor page with no TRADE bars, for a thin far rung with no trades that day, and - crucially - for EVERY family in a run where the IntradayBarRequest throws, since RefDataClient.cs:500-503 swallows the exception, returns an empty array and does not cache it. So two runs ten minutes apart, one with the bar service responding and one throttled, publish different Δ1w for every bank, with no note either way. The harness's Hygiene scan refuses to seed this shape (SpikeDangerBp 4.4, Market.cs:265-278) precisely because it usually indicates an authoring error - so pinning it needs an explicit opt-out for scenarios that are ABOUT the filter, plus a snaps-absent seed.

**Against the existing suite.** Nothing in the catalogue tests the despike filter against the code under test - the harness's stated policy is to AVOID triggering it (README, "Two data hazards the harness refuses outright"). Adjacent to 25, which proves snapped and un-snapped boundary days behave differently; this asks what the un-snapped case costs on an ORDINARY day, where the snap's absence silently hands the anchor to a filter with a 4.45bp ceiling. Also the OIS-side counterpart of the 2026-08-28 FINDINGS conclusion that "Despiking does NOT fix this" for inflation - here it actively breaks a good print.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Arithmetic and path both check out: HistoryFilter.cs:36 gives thr = 6×1.4826×0.005 = 4.4478bp on a zero-MAD window, :43-48's classic-spike arm fires on dev 5.0bp with neighbourGap 0, and PricingServiceBoards.cs:1651 hands the despiked closes back wholesale when GetLondonSnaps returns nothing. But the filter's 4.45bp ceiling on a dead-flat series is a deliberate calibrated tradeoff the desk is actively re-litigating in FINDINGS ("Despiking does NOT fix this"), so whether a lone 5bp print should survive is undecided rather than defective. **Change before writing it:** The expected justifies -5.0 off "the 16:15 mark on that day, which was 2.050" while the setup deliberately seeds no snaps for that rung — reword it to anchor on the CLOSE, which is the only mark that exists here.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/Analytics/HistoryFilter.cs:17` | `src/RateDesk.Core/Analytics/HistoryFilter.cs:36` | `src/RateDesk.Core/Analytics/HistoryFilter.cs:43` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceAnalytics.cs:31` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceAnalytics.cs:49` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1634` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1645` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1651` | `src/RateDesk.Bloomberg/RefDataClient.cs:488` | `src/RateDesk.Bloomberg/RefDataClient.cs:500` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287` | `tests/RateDesk.Scenarios/Harness/Market.cs:233`

## Unscheduled and inter-meeting moves

*Everything the calendar did not know about.*

### 129. FOMC cuts 100bp at 15:00 London, config says the Fed announces at 19:00 — board reads -400% of 25bp

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** An unscheduled decision arrives at an hour meetings.json does not know for that bank. Does the board roll and re-base off the marks it actually holds, or does a per-bank announcement time freeze it in the pre-decision state for the rest of the day?

**Set-up.** Two banks in emergency the same day (13-15 Mar-2020 shape). FOMC: 100bp cut announced TODAY at 15:00 London, target 1.00-1.25 -> 0-0.25. decisionTimeLondon stays as shipped, 19:00 (config/meetings.json:16-23) — nobody edits it, and the app has no per-meeting override. The desk correctly adds decisionDates += today and dates += StE = today (FOMC lag 0). Daily run pressed 16:30, so SnapDiscipline pins the marks and MarksAsOfLondon = today 16:15 (DailyBuilder.cs:224-230; harness spec.MarksAsOfLondon = 16:15). FEDL01 = 1.080. Rungs: 0 eff D-45 mat today mid 1.080; 1 eff today mat D+45 mid 0.080; 2 eff D+45 mat D+94 mid 0.060; 3 mid 0.055. Store: 45d of pre-cut closes, rung 1 = 1.060 (the D+45 contract), rung 2 = 1.040, records maintained. CONTROL ARM, BOC: 50bp cut announced today at 14:45 — its shipped decisionTimeLondon — CAONREPO 1.750, StE = D+1, rungs 0/1/2/3 = 1.750/1.250/1.230/1.220, config fully updated. Faked: Bloomberg only.

**What must publish.** BOC (control) must read: gate rolls (DecisionFor pairs today's announcement with StE=D+1, gap 1<=10; Announced(today,14:45,16:15)=true), re-base step 1 finds the quote whose Effective==D+1 and takes 1.250, so front = D+43, Mid 1.230, Fixing 1.250 dagger, Priced = (1.230-1.250)x100 = -2.0, %25bp = -2/25x100 = -8%. FOMC must read the same shape: the 100bp is delivered, so the today-starting period leaves the front, the base becomes that period's own OIS 0.080, front = D+45, Mid 0.060, Priced = (0.060-0.080)x100 = -2.0, %25bp = -8%. The front row's Delta1d must be BLANK for the today-starting period (it did not exist yesterday, no rung held it).

**How it breaks.** Announced(today,"19:00",16:15) is false (DecisionClock.cs:35-40), so the gate never shifts (PricingServiceBoards.cs:859-864) and lastDec falls back to the previous scheduled FOMC, whose window closed weeks ago (:895-905), so no re-base fires. The blast/xlsx/email publish FOMC front row dated TODAY, Mid 0.080, Fixing 1.080 (no dagger), Priced -100.0, % 25bp -400% — the whole delivered cut sold to the desk and to clients as still priced in, 98bp from the truth, sitting next to a BOC line that is right. Worse, LateAnnouncementNotes (DailyBuilder.cs:340-353) fires and states 'FOMC announces at 19:00 London, after this run's 16:15 marks — the board is NOT rolled past today's decision, because every price in this run was taken before it' — false: the marks are 16:15, 75 minutes AFTER the statement, and they carry the cut. The one note the operator would read tells them not to look. Second wrong number: the front row's Delta1d prints -98.0, because MeetingRungMap's offset calibration computes n1=0 ('no rung held it') and then falls through to the naive boundary count instead of returning null (MeetingRungMap.cs:151-168, against its own rule at :25-27).

**Against the existing suite.** 58 (16:15 close precedes the 19:00 statement) is the mirror image and asserts the OPPOSITE — there the marks legitimately predate the statement and must not roll. This scenario is the case 58's rule cannot distinguish: an off-schedule announcement BEFORE the snap, where the same branch produces the wrong answer and 58's own note becomes a false statement. Also touches 55 (two banks cutting together) but as a control-vs-treatment pair.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** PricingServiceBoards.cs:858 takes nowLdn from MarksAsOfLondon, so Announced(today,"19:00",16:15) is false at DecisionClock.cs:39 and neither the gate (:861-864) nor the re-base (:900) fires, while DailyBuilder.cs:347-351 emits exactly the false note quoted. The Δ1d half also checks out: with records maintained the offset is -1, n1=0 so MeetingRungMap.cs:151 skips its return and the naive count at :165-168 answers rung 1 instead of null (only slip: the 19:00 time is meetings.json:68, not :23, which is fixingLagDays).

### 130. BOC meeting postponed, config date left standing: a live period vanishes and CAONREPO is replaced by a swap mid

`P0 - wrong number published | needs a desk call | moderate confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** The calendar says a decision was announced today; the bank did not meet and the feed never re-pointed. What does the board publish, and does anything at all warn?

**Set-up.** BOC (CDSF{N}A, source BMOD, decisionTimeLondon 14:45, fixing CAONREPO). config carries Dec1 = TODAY with St1 = D+1, then Dec2 = D+42/St2 = D+43, Dec3 = D+84/St3 = D+85, Dec4 = D+126/St4 = D+127. Reality: the meeting was postponed (or the config date is simply wrong by a fortnight) — no statement, no renumber, CAONREPO prints 1.750... use 2.000 unchanged. Quiet week: fixing 2.000; rungs 0/1/2/3/4 = eff D-40 mat D+1 mid 2.000, eff D+1 mat D+43 mid 2.250, eff D+43 mat D+85 mid 2.400, eff D+85 mat D+127 mid 2.500, eff D+127 mid 2.550. PrevClose = the same values; 45 days of flat contract-level closes on those rungs, maturity records maintained, no renumber anywhere in the window. Run at 17:00 London (after 14:45), live marks.

**What must publish.** Nothing was decided, so the front published row is the period starting D+1: Mid 2.250, Fixing 2.000 (no dagger), Priced = (2.250-2.000)x100 = +25.0, Step blank, % 25bp = +25/25x100 = +100%. Then D+43 Mid 2.400 Priced +40.0 Step +15.0; D+85 2.500 Priced +50.0 Step +10.0; D+127 2.550 Priced +55.0 Step +5.0. Delta columns 0.0 throughout (flat tape).

**How it breaks.** DecisionFor pairs today's phantom announcement with St1 (gap 1 <= 10) and Announced is true, so gateShift = 1 and the D+1 row is DELETED from every surface (PricingServiceBoards.cs:859-870). The re-base then fires on the same phantom: effStart = D+1, gap 1 <= 10, today <= windowEnd, and step 1 finds the quote whose Effective == D+1 and takes 2.250 as the base (:905, :925, :932-935). Blast/xlsx/email publish: 'BOC | Decision D+42 | Start D+43 | Mid 2.400 | Fixing 2.250 dagger | Priced +15.0 | +60%' — the desk's next-meeting row is 42 days out instead of tomorrow, every Priced is 25bp too low (+60% where the market prices +160%), and the Fixing column shows a swap mid under the footnote 'dagger fixing re-based onto the just-decided period's OIS — the new rate has not printed yet' while CAONREPO is on the terminal at 2.000 and no decision occurred. NOTHING warns: CalendarHealth check 1 passes (every live eff is in the grid), check 2's runway is covered, check 3 only fires on an OBSERVED re-point and there was none, decisionTimeLondon parses. The asymmetry is the finding — an unexpected renumber is flagged, an expected renumber that never happens is not.

**Against the existing suite.** 5 (decision today, feed already re-pointed) and 4 (before the announcement time) exercise the gate when a decision is REAL. None of 1-64 exercises the gate when the calendar is right about the date and wrong about the event. 35 (nobody prices the decided rung) is about the re-base's fallbacks, not about the re-base firing with no decision behind it.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The mechanics are right — DecisionClock.cs:50 pairs the phantom decision with D+1, gateShift fires at PricingServiceBoards.cs:865-869, the re-base takes quotes[0]=2.250 at :933-935, and CalendarHealth.cs:44/91 stays silent — but :846-857 is the desk's explicit 2026-08-20 ruling that the calendar overrides a feed that has not re-pointed, and nothing in the data separates a postponement from a slow feed. Reachability is also thinner than stated: it needs Bloomberg's own generics to keep eff D+1 for a meeting that never happened.

### 131. ECB cuts 50bp inter-meeting, calendar never updated: the delivered cut prints as -200% of 25bp for weeks

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** An inter-meeting decision that nobody ever puts in config — the calendar is simply wrong until someone notices. What do the daily boards publish every day until then, and does the mail carry any marker?

**Set-up.** ECB (EESF{N}A, composite, decisionTimeLondon 13:15, fixing ESTRON). 50bp emergency cut announced YESTERDAY at 13:15, effective StE = D+5. config untouched: dates = [.., S1 = D-20, St1 = D+25, St2 = D+67, St3 = D+109], decisionDates = [D-26 (settled), D+19, D+61, D+103]. The feed re-pointed at yesterday's announcement (documented: EESF jumped between the 24-Jul and 27-Jul closes around the 23-Jul-26 ECB, PricingServiceBoards.cs:1157). Live: ESTRON 2.000; rung 0 eff S1 mat StE mid 2.000; rung 1 eff StE mat St1 mid 1.500; rung 2 eff St1 mat St2 mid 1.400; rung 3 eff St2 mat St3 mid 1.300. Store: yesterday's closes post-renumber (rung1 1.500, rung2 1.400, rung3 1.300); D-2 back 45 days pre-renumber at contract level (the St1 contract 1.930, St2 1.890, St3 1.850), maturity records maintained (yesterday's daily run wrote rung1 -> eff StE).

**What must publish.** The announcement is 24h old, so the StE period is decided, not priced: it leaves the front and the base re-bases onto its own OIS 1.500. Front table: 'ECB | Decision D+19 | Start D+25 | Mid 1.400 | Fixing 1.500 dagger | Priced -10.0 | -40%' since (1.400-1.500)x100 = -10.0 and -10/25x100 = -40%. Rows: D+25 Priced -10.0; D+67 Mid 1.300 Priced -20.0 Step -10.0; D+109 Mid 1.250 Priced -25.0 Step -5.0. Delta1d on the D+25 row = (1.400-1.400)x100 = 0.0 (its own yesterday close sat on rung 2), Delta1w = (1.400-1.930)x100 = -53.0.

**How it breaks.** DecisionFor(decisionDates, StE=D+5) finds nothing (D-26 is 31 days early, D+19 is later than the start) so gateShift stays 0 and the DECIDED period stays on the front; lastDec resolves to D-26 whose window closed at D-20, so no re-base. Published every day until someone edits the file: front row Start D+5, Mid 1.500, Fixing 2.000, Priced -50.0, % 25bp -200%, and because no decisionDate pairs with D+5 the Decision Date cell prints '05-Sep-26 *' under the footnote '* swap-period start shown (no decision calendar for this bank)' — untrue of the ECB. Every Priced below it is 50bp too negative (-60.0, -70.0, -75.0) while the Steps are right, which is exactly what makes it look correct. REGRESSION-LOCK HALF: the change columns survive — the store's per-day records re-point the window (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1724-1737) and publish Delta1d 0.0 with the note 'the tickers were not numbered the way the meeting calendar implies' — and CalendarHealth check 1 does say 'ticker period starting 05-Sep-26 is not in config\meetings.json'. Both are CHECK-lane notes that never reach the mail, so the -200% goes out unmarked.

**Against the existing suite.** 21 (ECB inter-meeting emergency, 12 days before the scheduled meeting) assumes the desk DID update both lists the same day and asserts the change columns. This attacks the same event from the config-never-updated side, where the failure is Priced and the front table rather than the lookbacks, and it pins 21's untested premise (that the calendar work happens at all).

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** DecisionFor (DecisionClock.cs:50) finds nothing within 10 days of StE=D+5 so gateShift stays 0, and lastDec=D-26 gives effStart=D-20 with today>windowEnd at PricingServiceBoards.cs:925, so the front publishes 1.500 against ESTRON 2.000 = -50.0/-200% under the "start *" footnote at WeeklyEmail.cs:182; the regression-lock half also holds (RungFor's exact scan returns rung 1 for D-8, so :1748 reads 1.930 and -53.0). One wording slip that cuts the scenario's way: CalendarHealth.cs:51 carries no CHECK prefix, so it never even reaches the gate at MainWindow.xaml.cs:334.

### 132. Operator types the emergency announcement into decisionDates and never adds its period start: -240% of 25bp, no dagger

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** The half-update every desk makes under pressure — the announcement is easy to type, the maintenance-period start needs working out. Does the re-base survive it, and can the desk tell that the base is stale?

**Set-up.** OPERATOR ERROR. ECB, 50bp emergency cut announced TODAY at 13:15, effective StE = D+5, run at 17:00. During the afternoon the operator edits config\meetings.json and adds decisionDates += today; dates is left alone: [.., S1 = D-20, St1 = D+25, St2 = D+67, St3 = D+109], decisionDates = [D-26, TODAY, D+19, D+61]. Feed re-pointed at 13:15. Live: ESTRON 2.000; rung 0 eff S1 mat StE mid 2.000; rung 1 eff StE mat St1 mid 1.500; rung 2 eff St1 mat St2 mid 1.400; rung 3 eff St2 mat St3 mid 1.300; rung 4 mid 1.250. 45 days of contract-level closes, records maintained.

**What must publish.** The half-update is enough for the gate: gateShift = 1 (today pairs with StE, gap 5 <= 10) so the front becomes St1 = D+25, which is right. The base must then re-base onto the decided period's own live OIS — it is sitting on post-shift quotes[0] with Effective == StE, mid 1.500 — giving front table 'ECB | D+19 | D+25 | Mid 1.400 | Fixing 1.500 dagger | Priced -10.0 | -40%', then D+67 1.300 Priced -20.0 Step -10.0, D+109 1.250 Priced -25.0 Step -5.0.

**How it breaks.** effStart is taken as the first dates entry >= the decision, and with StE missing that is St1 = D+25, so (eff - dec) = 25 > 10 and the whole re-base block is skipped (PricingServiceBoards.cs:905, :925). RefPct stays ESTRON 2.000 and the mail reads 'ECB | D+19 | D+25 | 1.400 | 2.000 | -60.0 | -240%' with NO dagger and no re-based footnote — the one visual cue that the base is a stand-in is absent precisely because the code declined to stand anything in. Every Priced is 50bp too negative. SIBLING BRANCH, same missing line: had the next scheduled start been <= 10 days out (St1 = D+8), the same block would have fired onto the WRONG period — the front published row's own mid — printing Priced exactly +0.0 on the front row and measuring every other row off it, which reads as 'nothing priced' and is even harder to spot. Only signal either way: CalendarHealth check 1's 'ticker period starting D+5 is not in config\meetings.json — grid drifted or missing (phantom-meeting risk)', a CHECK-lane note.

**Against the existing suite.** 21 has both lists correct; 61 (re-base fallback after a surprise) exercises the fallback CHAIN once the block is entered. Nothing tests the entry condition failing because the period grid and the decision list disagree — the commonest half-edit there is.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** PricingServiceBoards.cs:905-906 takes effStart from sched.Dates, so with StE unentered it resolves to St1=D+25 and (eff-dec)=25 fails the ≤10 test at :925 — RefPct stays 2.000, no †, Priced -60.0/-240% — while the gate at :861-864 rolled correctly off the ticker-derived date. The sibling branch is right too: with St1=D+8 the scan at :933-935 matches post-shift quotes[1], the front row's own mid, giving Priced +0.0.

### 133. Riksbank cuts 100bp inter-meeting: the 14-day cluster deletes the new period start and every strip row is one contract out

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** For a rollsAtPeriodStart family the renumber boundaries ARE the period starts. When an emergency start lands within 14 days of the previous one, the cluster keeps only the earlier — what do the surfaces that count boundaries publish once the emergency period actually begins?

**Set-up.** RIKSBANK (SKSF{N}A, rollsAtPeriodStart, markTurnPeriods, trustConfigDates, decisionTimeLondon 08:30, fixing SWESTR). Scheduled meeting D-11 with period start S1 = D-5. Emergency 100bp cut announced D0 at 08:30, period start StE = D+6 (the Riksbank's documented 6-7 day lag). Config correctly and fully updated: dates = [S0 = D-40, S1 = D-5, StE = D+6, St1 = D+30, St2 = D+72, St3 = D+114], decisionDates = [D-46, D-11, D0, D+24, D+66]. RUN THE PRODUCT ON D+7, the first day after the emergency period starts and the family renumbers. Store: pre-cut closes to D-1 (the D+30 contract 1.980, D+72 1.900, D+114 1.860); on D+6, post-cut closes rung1 1.000 (StE), rung2 0.880 (St1), rung3 0.800 (St2), rung4 0.760 (St3). Strip asOf = store.LatestDate() = D+6 (Surfaces.cs:155-161, MainWindow's own rule).

**What must publish.** MeetingRungMap for SKSF takes starts only (no announcements). Sorted candidates D-40, D-5, D+6, D+30, D+72: D+6 is 11 days after D-5, so the cluster (>14 to keep) DROPS it. The boundary list must instead contain D+6, and with it the D+30 dashboard row must publish its own contract: Mid 0.880, 1w level = rung 1's close on D-1 = 1.980, rendered 1w = (0.880-1.980)x100 = -110.0. The D+72 row must publish 0.800, the D+114 row 0.760. The email side, ticker-dated with SWESTR having caught up on D+7, correctly publishes front = D+30 Mid 0.880 base 1.000 Priced -12.0.

**How it breaks.** With D+6 clustered away, RungAt counts boundaries in (asOf, contract] one short (RollingStrip.cs:60-75): the D+30 row resolves to rung 1 and publishes 1.000 — the just-started emergency period's rate under the next meeting's date, 12bp too high — the D+72 row publishes 0.880 (8bp too high), the D+114 row 0.800. Its 1w level is still right (1.980, pre-emergency rung 1), so the rendered 1w change reads -98.0 against a truth of -110.0 on a page with no sign-in. Same missing boundary mis-rungs every record-less lookback on the boards' stitched history (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1603, :1664-1668). Frequency is not marginal: with ~35-day gaps between Riksbank starts, an emergency start avoids both 14-day windows only if it lands in a 7-day band, so ~80% of Riksbank inter-meeting decisions delete a boundary. The invariant the cluster rests on is stated in the code — 'no two real meetings of one bank sit within 14 days, a tested config invariant' — and an inter-meeting move is the definition of breaking it; nothing checks it at runtime.

**Against the existing suite.** 21 observed the cluster dropping an ECB ANNOUNCEMENT and accepted it because the store's records defend the numbering. This is the start-vs-start flavour that only a rollsAtPeriodStart family can have, it damages published LEVELS (not just history) from the emergency start day onward, and it hits the dashboard derivation, which is built with no records at all. Distinct from 28-33, which are scheduled Riksbank rolls and the Y/E turn.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** MeetingRungMap.cs:53-55 does drop StE=D+6 (11 days after D-5) for this rollsAtPeriodStart family, and ForMeetings builds its map with no records and no ticker dates (RollingStrip.cs:223), so RungAt/RolledValue (:71-75, :159-165) read rung 1's D+6 close 1.000 under the D+30 label with the 1w level still 1.980. Citation slip: meetings.json:376 is SNB's fixingLagDays; the RIKSBANK entry is :473-535, rollsAtPeriodStart at :518. **Change before writing it:** Seed closes for D0..D+5 as well: once D+6 is a boundary, RolledValue steps `then` back off it (RollingStrip.cs:153-154), so the expected Mid 0.880 must be readable from D+5, not only D+6.

### 134. Emergency day run on a machine with no maturity records: the price scan cannot fire and the change columns are 12bp out

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** On a machine whose store carries the 45-day price seed but no historical SW_EFF_DT records, the strip's own prices are the only defence against an inserted meeting. Can RungShiftScan actually see the insertion on the day it happens?

**Set-up.** OPERATOR/OPS. The desk runs the emergency-day daily on the second machine (fresh install, or a store restored from a StoreBackup snapshot older than the records): 45 days of prices, maturity records only for TODAY, stamped by the live quotes exactly as production does — harness BankSpec.RecordMaturities = false (Spec.cs:56-59). ECB, 50bp cut announced TODAY 13:15, run 17:00, config FULLY and correctly updated: dates = [S2 = D-62, S1 = D-20, StE = D+6, St1 = D+30, St2 = D+72, St3 = D+114], decisionDates = [D-26, TODAY, D+24, D+66]. Live (feed re-pointed): ESTRON 2.000; rung 0 eff S1 mat StE mid 2.000; rung 1 eff StE mat St1 mid 1.500; rung 2 eff St1 mat St2 mid 1.380; rung 3 eff St2 mid 1.260; rung 4 mid 1.140 — 12bp between contracts so a mis-rung is unmistakable. Store, contract-level under the PRE-emergency boundary list [D-68, D-26, D+24, D+66, D+108]: the St1 contract flat 1.980 for D-45..D-1, St2 1.860, St3 1.740, St4 1.620.

**What must publish.** Priced is fine here (re-base step 1 finds eff == StE and takes 1.500, front = D+30 Mid 1.380 Priced -12.0, % -48%). The change columns must each measure the D+30 contract against itself: Delta1d = Delta1w = Delta1m = (1.380 - 1.980)x100 = -60.0 on the front row, and -60.0 on the rows below by the same construction. Flipping RecordMaturities to true on the identical market must produce the same -60.0 — that is the A/B.

**How it breaks.** Boundaries today are [D-68, D-26, D0, D+24, D+66, D+114], so the stitched window (D-26, D0] is read at idx 2 and (D-68, D-26] at idx 3 — one rung out for every pre-announcement day, because an inserted meeting renumbers history under a count the market never had. The rescue never arrives: no record for those days, so the code falls to ShiftSince, and today's judgement is guaranteed Unknown — hypothesis 0 misses by 48bp, +1 by 36bp, +2 by 24bp, -1 by 60bp, all far outside FitToleranceBp = 1.5 — and one unconfirmed day voids the entire chain (RungShiftScan.cs:167-175, :192-202). trueIdx is null, the point is kept at the calendar's idx, and the blast/xlsx/email publish Delta1d/1w/1m = (1.380-1.860)x100 = -48.0 on every row where the truth is -60.0. No note fires (trueIdx was null, not different, so PricingServiceBoards.cs:1754 stays silent), and the CHECK notes that DO appear are the absolute bars tripping on a 50bp day — identical to what a correct board would produce, so they cannot discriminate. The liveMid parameter added expressly so 'the scan is not blind to a renumbering that happened TODAY - which is exactly when an unscheduled decision shows up' (RungShiftScan.cs:90-93) cannot work by construction: an insertion is always accompanied by the repricing that caused it.

**Against the existing suite.** 21 names this as its own residual ('the residual exposure is history OLDER than the store's maturity records') and then seeds full records, so it never tests it; 63/64 exercise price-only rungs with no maturity records but no inserted boundary. This scenario is 21 with the records removed, and it shows the price-scan fallback built for the case cannot cover it. If the desk prefers BLANK to -60.0 on an unattributable window, say so — but -48.0 is not defensible either way.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** All four hypotheses miss by 24-60bp so Judge returns Unknown (RungShiftScan.cs:167-170), ShiftSince voids the chain at :198, and with no record HasRecordFor is false so PricingServiceBoards.cs:1745 leaves the point at the calendar's idx — windows (D-26,D0] at idx 2 and (D-68,D-26] at idx 3 both read 1.860, giving -48.0 where the contract's own mark is 1.980 and the truth -60.0. Heavy overlap with catalogue 114 (same lines, same conclusion); the distinct kernel — the liveMid arm at :104 being dead by construction on an insertion day — justifies keeping it (minor: today's last boundary is D+108, not D+114).

### 135. Public per-currency dashboards read one contract too near after an unrecorded inter-meeting cut, while the email is right

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** The dashboards are a second derivation — config grid plus stored closes, with no maturity records in the map at all. On the day after an inter-meeting cut the calendar does not know about, do the 28 public pages agree with the email they are mailed beside?

**Set-up.** ECB, exactly the market of the never-added scenario: 50bp cut announced YESTERDAY 13:15, effective StE = D+5, config untouched (dates [.., S1 = D-20, St1 = D+25, St2 = D+67, St3 = D+109], decisionDates [D-26, D+19, D+61, D+103]). Feed re-pointed yesterday, so the store's D-1 closes are post-renumber: rung 1 1.500 (StE), rung 2 1.400 (St1), rung 3 1.300 (St2), rung 4 1.200 (St3); D-8 and back are pre-renumber (rung 1 = the St1 contract 1.930, rung 2 = St2 1.890). Strip asOf = store.LatestDate() = D-1. RollingStrip.ForMeetings builds its own MeetingRungMap with no tickerDates and no recordedEffective (RollingStrip.cs:223) — the derivation is 100% calendar.

**What must publish.** Boundaries clustered from config = [D-68, D-26, D+19, D+61, D+103]. The strip's contracts are St1/St2/St3, and each row must publish that contract's own newest stored close: D+25 -> 1.400, D+67 -> 1.300, D+109 -> 1.200, with the D+25 row's 1w level = the St1 contract on D-8 = 1.930, i.e. a rendered 1w of (1.400-1.930)x100 = -53.0. That is what the email publishes for the same bank in the same minute (ticker-dated), so the two surfaces must agree to the tick.

**How it breaks.** RungAt counts boundaries in (D-1, contract]: {D+19} = 1 for St1, so RolledValue reads rung 1's D-1 close and the row dated D+25 publishes 1.500 — the emergency period's rate, 10bp too high. D+67 publishes 1.400, D+109 publishes 1.300: every row carries its nearer neighbour's rate, and the last real contract is silently represented by the previous one. The 1w column still reads rung 1 on D-8 = 1.930, so the page renders a 1w change of (1.500-1.930)x100 = -43.0 where the truth is -53.0. Net effect: the public site tells external readers the market prices 10bp less easing at every horizon than the email attached to the same run, all week, with no note (the strip's own notes only count rolls inside the 1m window and neighbour-guard substitutions), and nothing reconciles the two surfaces before upload.

**Against the existing suite.** 51-53 and 59 test the strip on and around a decision day the calendar KNOWS about, and 50 checks one report across every surface. None tests the strip when a renumber boundary is absent from config, which is the state every inter-meeting move creates until someone edits the file — and it is the only surface with no access to the store's records to defend itself.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** ForMeetings builds MeetingRungMap(sched) with no tickerDates and no recordedEffective (RollingStrip.cs:223), so with D+5 absent from config RungAt counts {D+19}=1 for the D+25 row (:73) and RolledValue reads rung 1's D-1 close 1.500 (:159-165) while the 1w still reads 1.930 at D-8 — -43.0 published against a truth of -53.0, and 1.400 in the email beside it. Nothing gates the pages either: MainWindow.xaml.cs:334 fires before the blast/workbook, not before the dashboard upload.
