### 136. MPC cuts 50bp then 15bp eight days apart (Mar-2020): cluster eats the second boundary, and MPC has no fixingLagDays

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | moderate confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** Two inter-meeting cuts by one bank inside the 14-day cluster window, with non-25bp increments. What does the day AFTER each cut measure Priced against, and what happens to the seven days between them?

**Set-up.** MPC (GPSF{N}A, decisionTimeLondon 12:00, fixing SONIA, lag 0 — config dates 17-Sep == decisionDates 17-Sep). March-2020 shape: cut 1 announced D-8, Bank Rate 0.75 -> 0.25 (-50bp); cut 2 announced D0, 0.25 -> 0.10 (-15bp). Both correctly added: dates gains D-8 and D0, decisionDates gains D-8 and D0; next scheduled Dec1 = St1 = D+38, then D+80. RUN THE PRODUCT ON D+1. Live: SONIA prints 0.250 (it reports D0, the day of the 12:00 cut, and the new Bank Rate applies from the day after). Rungs: 0 eff D0 mat D+38 mid 0.100; 1 eff D+38 mat D+80 mid 0.080; 2 eff D+80 mid 0.070. 45 days of contract-level closes, records maintained.

**What must publish.** The 0.10 Bank Rate is in force from D+1, and the D0 period's own OIS prints it at 0.100, so the base on D+1 must be 0.100 and the front row must read: 'MPC | D+38 | D+38 | Mid 0.080 | Fixing 0.100 dagger | Priced -2.0 | -8%' since (0.080-0.100)x100 = -2.0 and -2/25x100 = -8%. Boundaries must contain BOTH D-8 and D0, and no day between them may be masked (a lag-0 family has no announcement-to-start gap at all).

**How it breaks.** (1) The re-base window is [dec, eff + fixingLagDays] and MPC's fixingLagDays is absent from config, i.e. 0, while eff == dec == D0 — so on D+1 the window is already shut, RefPct stays SONIA 0.250, and the mail prints Priced -17.0 / % 25bp -68% where the truth is -2.0 / -8%. The code's own comment states the opposite of the config: 'FOMC and MPC are the exception and carry 1 in config' (PricingServiceBoards.cs:179-181) — the flag is on FOMC (config/meetings.json:23) and on SNB, which BuildWeekly excludes from the report, and NOT on MPC. Same mechanism on cut 1's day-after gives a 50bp error. (2) Sorted candidates .., D-50, D-8, D0, D+38: D0 is 8 days after D-8, so MeetingRungMap's cluster DELETES today's boundary, and every rung count is one short from D0 onward. (3) The surviving boundary D-8 pairs with the next start within 14 days — D0 — so the mixed-state loop masks D-7..D-1 as unattributable, seven clean days on which nothing may anchor: on a records-less machine the window empties, Delta1d blanks (5-day cap) and Delta1w silently anchors on the D-8 snap, an 8-day change under a 1w label.

**Against the existing suite.** 54 is the same day-after mechanism for the FOMC, which passes because it carries fixingLagDays = 1; nobody wrote the MPC twin, and the config makes it fail. 15 (two FOMC decisions inside the 1m window) has them 6 weeks apart, outside the cluster. The 8-day pair is what makes one bank break the 14-day config invariant on its own.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Break (1) is real — MPC's entry (meetings.json:147-199) has no fixingLagDays unlike FOMC's :23, so windowEnd=eff=D0 and the ≤windowEnd test at PricingServiceBoards.cs:925 fails on D+1, publishing -17.0/-68% — but it is catalogue 108's finding with a different premise. Break (3) also holds: MeetingRungMap.cs:84-92 masks D-7..D-1 because D0 counts as a "start within 14 days" of the D-8 boundary, which is spurious for a lag-0 family. **Change before writing it:** Strike break (2). With lag 0 the inserted D0 period becomes the run-down, so rungs 1+ never renumber; dropping D0 in the cluster is the CORRECT count for pre-D0 anchors, and Δ1d anchors cleanly on D0 rather than blanking. Keep (1) and (3).

## The guards and the gate

*OutlierGuard, FuturesGuard, staleness, and what the CHECK modal actually protects.*

### 137. Day after a 25bp cut, the re-base does not fire: every Priced is 25bp wrong and every guard reports clean

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** Priced = (Mid - fixing) x 100 is the desk's headline column and the front table's "% of 25bp". Does ANY guard in the app look at it? If a re-base fails and the base is stale by a whole policy step, is there a single note anywhere?

**Set-up.** Same geometry and arithmetic, but drop the pruning. Use MPC instead of FOMC: the shipped MPC entry (config/meetings.json:147-198) sets NO fixingLagDays, so the default is 0 (PricingServiceBoards.cs:182) and windowEnd = eff = the decision day; MPC's period starts ON the decision date (lag 0), so on the morning after, today <= windowEnd at PricingServiceBoards.cs:925 is FALSE and the whole re-base block is skipped while SONIA still prints the pre-cut rate. No config edit, no operator error. Alternative premise if the author wants to keep FOMC's trivial FF monthavg blend: a HOLE in decisionDates — the shipped ECB list deliberately omits the decision for the 2027-05-06 start ('left for the official calendar', config/meetings.json:107-112), and a period whose decision is absent gets no gate roll and no re-base. Either way state the caveat that a missing decisionDate DOES put a '{start} *' marker on the front line (WeeklyEmail.cs:165) — the assertion must be that no note or marker names the BASE, not that the row is unmarked.

**What must publish.** Unchanged except: front Priced is 0.0, not +0.0. If MPC is used, re-derive the guard half — MPC's guard is SFI imm3m (config/meetings.json:187-188), so the window is 3rd-Wed-Sep to 3rd-Wed-Dec and the published rows must straddle it (rows[0] <= 16-Sep-26, rows[^1] >= 16-Dec-26) for the guard to run at all instead of reporting 'skipped'.

**How it breaks.** Unchanged. Add the note-inventory assertion explicitly: assert that NONE of the eight CHECK families in the repo fires — the run has rows (so CompoundedFixing.cs:108 is silent), RefPct is non-null (so PricingServiceWeekly.cs:251 is silent), no row is rejected (:270), decisionDates is non-empty (so CalendarHealth.cs:78/:82 are silent) — and that the futures guard prints its 'ok' line.

**Against the existing suite.** 54, 61 and 35 test whether the re-base MECHANISM produces the right base. This attacks the same mechanism from the opposite side: it assumes the re-base has already failed and asks what the GUARD LAYER does. New assertion - that no note of any kind names a Priced-only error - which none of 54/61/35 makes.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The guard-blindness claim is exactly right and I verified all nine sites. OutlierGuard's only three selectors are m.D1Bp/m.W1Bp/m.M1Bp (OutlierGuard.cs:33-34) — Priced and Step are read by nothing in src/. FuturesGuard blends MeetingRow.MidPct (FuturesGuard.cs:70, :128), so it is structurally blind to RefPct and will print the ok branch (FuturesGuard.cs:79-80). I grepped every CHECK producer in the repo (FuturesGuard.cs:85, PricingServiceWeekly.cs:251, :270, CalendarHealth.cs:78, :82, CompoundedFixing.cs:108, :114, InflHistory.cs:580, SnapDiscipline.cs:42) and confirmed the only Priced-related one is gated on RefPct being NULL (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:250) — with RefPct = 4.000 it cannot fire. Arithmetic checks out (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1102; WeeklyEmail.cs:162 -25.0/25*100 = -100%; StoreBooks.cs:95 priced/25.0). TWO fixes. (1) The premise is pure operator error and unnecessary: FOMC's shipped fixingLagDays is 1 (config/meetings.json:23), so windowEnd = dec + 1 business day and on the morning after a Bd(-1) cut the re-base DOES fire (PricingServiceBoards.cs:918-925) — the scenario only works if the decision is pruned. There are two shipped states that produce the identical dead base with no operator error, and one of them should be the premise instead. (2) The harness invariant forbids '+0.0' on a flat cell (README 'a flat change prints 0.0 and never +0.0'), so the front Priced expectation must be written 0.0.

### 138. Terminal data feed frozen since 09:00; ECB cuts 25bp at 13:15; the 16:30 board publishes 09:00 prices with no STALE line

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** Staleness is judged as (age - the snapshot's own 10th-percentile baseline) so a per-machine clock offset cancels. What happens when the fault is not an offset but a UNIFORM freeze - every quote in the snapshot equally old?

**Set-up.** Drop the 16:30/SnapDiscipline/PRE-CLOSE framing entirely (README: SnapDiscipline is not applied in the harness; use spec.MarksAsOfLondon, Spec.cs:259, only if a pinned-marks clock is needed for the decision gates). Seed ESTRON at the PRE-cut 2.250 (T+1, consistent with the scenario's own reasoning). Keep DefaultAgeMinutes = 450 on every bank with no per-rung override. Split the SOURCES '*>1h' half out as a manual check — MainWindow.xaml.cs:427 is unreachable from the harness.

**What must publish.** BaselineAgeMinutes = 450 over ~30 identical ages -> per-rung test 450-450 = 0, not > 60, so staleRungs is empty and PricingServiceBoards.cs:1130 emits nothing. The gate shift drops 16-Sep; the re-base reads quotes[0].Effective == 16-Sep and sets RefPct = 2.250 (rebased, daggered). Published rows: 4-Nov 2.200 Priced -5.0; 23-Dec 2.170 Priced -8.0 Step -3.0; 10-Feb 2.150 Priced -10.0 Step -2.0. Note the trap: because mid AND base are frozen by the same 25bp, the Priced column is UNCHANGED by the freeze — the correct-world Priced is also -5.0/-8.0/-10.0. What is wrong is (i) every published Mid, 25bp high (2.200 vs ~1.950); (ii) the fixing cell, 2.250 with a dagger asserting it is the decided period's own OIS, against a market that just repriced it to ~2.000; (iii) the change columns, which read the seeded flat +1.0 where truth is about -24.0 on the day; (iv) no STALE line for four rungs quiet 7.5 hours. Front table: Priced -5.0, -20% of 25bp — plausible-looking, which is worse than an absurd number.

**How it breaks.** The blast, workbook and sheet email publish three ECB mids 25bp above the market on the afternoon the ECB cut 25bp by surprise, a daggered 'rebased' fixing that is 25bp stale, and Δ1d/Δ1w/Δ1m that show a 1bp week — with no STALE note, because the relative staleness test's denominator is the fault itself (RatesSnapshot.cs:107-115 has no absolute bound and never compares LAST_UPDATE_DT against an earlier calendar day; RefDataClient.cs:522 documents the relative-only rule). OutlierGuard is silent because the freeze is uniform (MAD 0, every |Δ| under 12/30/50). The futures guard blends the frozen mids and will agree with a frozen TKY, so it reports ok. Separately, and manually: the operator who opens SOURCES to find a fresher contributor is told every contributor is fresh, because MainWindow.xaml.cs:427-428 takes the MIN of the probed ages as its baseline.

**Against the existing suite.** 37 attacks the same STALE watch but seeds ONE aged rung against a 5m baseline - i.e. it tests the relative test working. This is the complement: the relative test's denominator is itself the fault. Also adjacent to 45, which uses age:95.0 on one rung. Neither varies DefaultAgeMinutes globally.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The mechanism is real and new. BaselineAgeMinutes takes ages[min(n-1, n/10)] over the whole snapshot (RatesSnapshot.cs:107-115) with no absolute bound, and the per-rung test is age0 - ageBase > 60 (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1073) against ageBase = the snapshot's own percentile (:1044), so a uniform 450m freeze gives 450 - 450 = 0 and staleRungs is empty — no STALE note at :1130. The harness can drive it: ScenarioSpec.DefaultAgeMinutes exists and defaults to 5.0 (Spec.cs:255) and Market.cs:75/104/118 stamp it on every rung, the fixing and every Extra. Genuinely complementary to 37, which seeds one 200m rung against a 5m baseline and asserts the 195m arithmetic. The SOURCES min-baseline second failure is also real (MainWindow.xaml.cs:427-428 uses .Min(), not a percentile). BUT the published numbers in expected/break_mode are wrong, and two setup elements are unreachable. (a) With the ECB deciding today at 13:15 TimePassed and the decision in decisionDates, the announced gate shift FIRES (PricingServiceBoards.cs:861-870), so the 16-Sep row leaves the board — there is no 'front Mid 2.250' row. (b) quotes[0] after the shift IS the 16-Sep contract, so the re-base finds it by effective date (PricingServiceBoards.cs:933-935) and RefPct becomes 2.250 rebased — Priced is never measured against ESTRON. (c) ESTRON seeded at 2.000 contradicts the scenario's own T+1 argument; on a frozen pre-announcement feed it must be the pre-cut 2.250. (d) 'Run pressed at 16:30, SnapDiscipline in Snap1615 mode' and 'no PRE-CLOSE flag' are not expressible: the harness never applies SnapDiscipline (README, Known harness limits) so PRE-CLOSE cannot fire for either reason, and time of day is the machine's.

### 139. A scheduled RatesWeeklyCli daily writes the blast, the workbook and the shared-drive mirror through a triggered futures guard — the gate exists only in the WPF window

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | live-or-manual-only | critic-authored`

**The question.** The pre-publish gate is a method on MainWindow. The product also ships a headless CLI that runs the same Build and Render. Which CHECK notes stop the CLI, and what does a scheduled task publish on a day the futures guard breaks by a full policy step?

**Set-up.** Take scenario 43's market unchanged (FOMC decision today, FF priced a full 25bp away from the meeting blend, so FuturesGuard.cs:85 emits a CHECK-prefixed FUTURES GUARD TRIGGERED line). Drive it through src/RateDesk.Weekly.Cli/Program.cs 'daily' rather than the DAILY RUN button: the CLI calls DailyBuilder.Build(store, Console.WriteLine, appData) then DailyBuilder.Render(rep, store, outDir, appData, Console.WriteLine) back to back (Program.cs:186-187). publish.json has a dailyDir and a save-down root configured, as it does on the desk box.

**What must publish.** Build produces the note and logs it as one line among ~30: '  daily note: CHECK: FUTURES GUARD TRIGGERED — FOMC: FFV6 Oct-26 implies 4.500 but the meeting rows blend to 4.250 (Δ+25.0bp > 2.5bp tolerance)' (DailyBuilder.cs:239, :250). Then Render writes blast.txt and its table flavour, the runs workbook, the email fragment/plaintext/preview, mirrors to the shared drive and syncs OIS_Runs_*.xlsm and Inflation_Runs_*.xlsm into the save-down folders (DailyBuilder.cs:383-448, :419-424). Process exits 0. A correct product either refuses to publish on a CHECK from a non-interactive path, or requires an explicit --force/--ignore-checks and says in the exit text what it overrode.

**How it breaks.** Every CHECK family in the repo — the futures-guard breach, a rejected mid publishing n/a, a run with no rows, an unquoted fixing, the calendar-health warnings, the inflation coherence notes — is advisory on the CLI path, because ConfirmChecks is a private method on MainWindow (MainWindow.xaml.cs:332) invoked only at :564 and :788 and grep finds no other caller. The CLI's own header markets it for exactly this use ('scriptable history maintenance ... from a scheduled task without anyone sitting in front of the GUI', Program.cs:7-9). So the mechanism the desk decided must 'demand eyes, not just a log line' (CompoundedFixing.cs:99-102) is bypassed by the product's own automation entry point, and a 25bp-broken board reaches the chat paste, the shared drive and the clients' workbook with nobody having seen the gate.

**Against the existing suite.** 43 and 44 establish that a breach must reach the gate; 45 proves note text stays out of content. None asks which entry points the gate governs. Adjacent to the batch's check-gate-unreadable-and-blocking, whose claim that no headless path exists this scenario refutes.

**Critic (CRITIC-AUTHORED).** proposed by the domain critic

### 140. The investor-facing currency page still publishes a fabricated neighbour midpoint, marked with a bare asterisk, and throws its own warning away

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | critic-authored`

**The question.** The desk decided on 2026-08-27 that a rejected print WITHHOLDS rather than substitutes — 'we should never have to invent mids'. That fix landed on the desk path. What does the public dashboard path do with the same print?

**Set-up.** EUR, no decision in the window, quiet tape. One INTERIOR contract's stored close on the render as-of is mis-posted 40bp below both neighbours, which sit 5bp apart (persistent level via Level/LevelStep, so the Hampel despike leaves it alone). Nothing else unusual. Compare the same print on the two paths in one scenario: the meeting run's own row and Surfaces.Strips for the same bank (Surfaces.cs:159-161, dumped to strips.txt).

**What must publish.** Desk path: PricingServiceBoards.cs:1077-1080 sets off, the row publishes MidSource 'rejected (ticker -40.0bp off its neighbours)', every renderer substitutes the n/a label, the step chain steps over it, and PricingServiceWeekly.cs:270 raises a CHECK that gates distribution. Dashboard path: RollingStrip.Guard finds |m0 - midpoint| = 40bp > 25 with neighbours inside 25bp and RETURNS the midpoint (RollingStrip.cs:130-134); the row is published at that invented level with '*' appended to its label (:96); the warning '1 interior row(s) replaced by the neighbour midpoint — the quoted print was implausible against both neighbours' goes into StripTable.Notes (:107-109) and dies there, because CurrencyPage.cs:48 consumes only Panels.From(RollingStrip.ForMeetings(...)) and never reads Notes. So: two paths, one print, one publishes a label and stops the press, the other publishes a number nobody quoted.

**How it breaks.** eur.html on the zero-sign-in public site, the movers hub and the single-file pack attached to the weekly email carry a rate the market never quoted, distinguishable only by an asterisk with no legend on the page, and the note that would have explained it is discarded before rendering. Nothing gates it: RenderAll builds its own PricingService on an empty snapshot (MainWindow.xaml.cs:627) and runs before ConfirmChecks (:548 vs :564), and FuturesGuard/OutlierGuard/the completeness gate run only inside EmailBuilder.Build (EmailBuilder.cs:189-192). The doc comment at RollingStrip.cs:124-126 still states the superseded rule ('the midpoint replaces it and the row is labelled'), so the divergence is visible in the source. Assert: the strip row's Mid equals (neighbour a + neighbour b)/2 to 1e-9, the label ends in '*', StripTable.Notes carries the substitution line, and rep.Notes does NOT.

**Against the existing suite.** 38 and 62 attack the DESK path's misprint guard (and finding 8 was closed by making it withhold). 51/52/53/59 check strip CONTENT around a decision. None compares the two guards' policies or notices that the dashboard copy still substitutes and drops its note. New.

### 141. Flat market, whole strip's change columns read -10.0 in all 15 cells: uniform by construction, under every bar, futures guard says ok

`P1 - missing gate or note | regression lock | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** OutlierGuard is purely cross-sectional plus three absolute bars. FuturesGuard compares LEVELS. So which mechanism catches a change-column error that hits every row by the same amount - a strip whose history is off by one rung after an un-noticed renumber?

**Set-up.** Unchanged mechanically, but say in the header what the seed is FOR: 'the store is seeded one rung out by hand as a STAND-IN for the finding-2/3/7 error class (an inter-meeting decision, a renumber-day as-of, an unstable settlement lag), each of which puts the same uniform inter-contract gap on every row and every horizon. Nothing in this synthetic world tells the app the data is wrong, so no product can recover it; the question is whether anything DOWNSTREAM would notice if it did happen.' Drop the futures-guard geometry fussiness — with rows[1] at Cal.D(58) rather than 70 the Oct blend is (27x3.700 + 4x3.800)/31 = 3.712903 against an implied 3.700, gap -1.3bp, still inside the 2.5bp tolerance and still an ok line, so either grid works.

**What must publish.** Keep the hand arithmetic (flat tape -> truth 0.0; 3.700 - 3.800 = -10.0 on every row at every horizon). Delete 'a correct app must recover the shift or refuse the change cells'. The assertion becomes: fifteen coherent -10.0 cells publish, and NO note of any kind is produced — no cross-sectional flag (MAD collapses to 0, deviations 0), no absolute flag (10 < 12), no futures-guard breach (levels are right), no STALE, no completeness note — plus the positive statement that the futures guard prints an ok line while the change columns are wrong.

**How it breaks.** Same published numbers, but the claim is scoped: the guard layer cannot see a change-column error at all, by construction, because OutlierGuard's only three selectors are D1Bp/W1Bp/M1Bp within one run's cross-section and FuturesGuard reads MidPct only. The strip-level uniformity means the cross-sectional arm has nothing to compare against and 10bp is deliberately chosen to sit in the gap between MAD = 0 and the 12bp d1 bar.

**Against the existing suite.** 22 (phantom-step trap) and 23 (renumber-day correction) attack the same rung-mapping mechanism, but ask whether the STITCHER gets it right. This attacks the second line of defence: given a mis-rung strip, does anything downstream notice? Deliberately uniform, and deliberately 10bp so it lands in the gap between MAD=0 (no cross-sectional signal) and the 12bp d1 bar.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The guard-blindness half is unconditionally correct and I confirmed every line. With all five rows at -10.0 the front is excluded (OutlierGuard.cs:51-52), the body of four is all -10.0, so med = -10.0, every |x - med| = 0.0, mad = 0.0, thresh = max(4.0, 0.0) = 4.0 and the test at :58 is 0.0 > 4.0 = false; the absolute bars at :42 need |v| > 12/30/50 and -10.0 clears none. FuturesGuard consumes MidPct only (FuturesGuard.cs:97-102, :125-129) so it cannot see a change column, and with correct levels it legitimately reports ok. Level/LevelStep exist for raw per-rung seeding (Spec.cs:117-120). The FIX is the framing, and it matters. There is no renumber anywhere in this scenario's lookback (no decision in the window, by construction), so rung numbering is CONSTANT over the 45 days and there is no shift for the stitcher, MeetingRungMap or RungShiftScan to recover — the setup does not model 'a strip whose history is off by one rung after an un-noticed renumber', it models a store that simply holds wrong data. Demanding that the app 'recover the shift or refuse the change cells' asks it to detect an error that is information-theoretically absent from the synthetic world; that half of the expectation is unsound and would be recorded as a false finding. Reframe as what it actually is: a lock on the guard layer's structural blind spot, with the fabricated store standing in for the finding-2/3/7 class of defects, which produce exactly this shape (FINDINGS #2 measured one inter-contract gap on EVERY row and EVERY horizon: -42.0/-45.0/-47.5).

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:42` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:54` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:56` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:58` | `src/RateDesk.Core/FuturesGuard.cs:97` | `src/RateDesk.Core/FuturesGuard.cs:125` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:283`

### 142. Two adjacent ECB rungs mis-priced the same way: MAD collapses to zero, the median moves to the fault, and CHECK names the one correct row

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** With only three body rows, the median is one of them. If TWO rows carry the same fault, does OutlierGuard flag the fault - or does it flag the row that is right, and send the desk to verify the wrong cell before it clicks Yes?

**Set-up.** ECB, no decision in the window. EXACTLY four ticker-dated published rows (so vals.Count = 4 clears the >=4 test at OutlierGuard.cs:46 and body.Count = 3 clears :53). Starts 16-Sep-26 / 4-Nov-26 / 23-Dec-26 / 10-Feb-27; live mids 2.000 / 1.950 / 1.900 / 1.870; ESTRON 2.000. The contributor page has mis-posted the two BACK rungs by 10bp for one day only in the sense of the change column: seed the histories so Δ1d comes out front -2.0, then body [0.0, +10.0, +10.0] - persistent levels either side of the step so the despike filter is not involved. Δ1w and Δ1m seeded uniform (+1.0 everywhere) so exactly one column can speak. Note the neighbour misprint guard cannot help here: RejectedBy needs the print >25bp from the neighbour midpoint (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1034) and a 10bp level error is less than half that; and with two ADJACENT rows wrong the neighbours no longer agree within 25bp anyway (:1031).

**What must publish.** Truth: the market was flat; the +10.0 pair is the fault and 0.0 is the honest cell. Hand arithmetic on the guard: body = [0.0, +10.0, +10.0]; sorted [0,10,10], odd count -> median = +10.0. Absolute deviations [10.0, 0.0, 0.0]; sorted [0,0,10] -> MAD = 0.0. Threshold = max(4.0, 4 x 0.0) = 4.0bp. Row 23-Dec and 10-Feb: |10.0 - 10.0| = 0.0, not > 4.0 -> silent, and |10.0| > 12.0 is false so no absolute flag either. Row 4-Nov: |0.0 - 10.0| = 10.0 > 4.0 and |0.0| <= 12.0 -> FLAGGED. The correct outcome is either both +10.0 rows flagged or nothing flagged; flagging exactly the good row is the failure.

**How it breaks.** The pre-publish popup shows one line: "CHECK: ECB 04-Nov-26 Δ1d 0.0bp vs run median +10.0bp — verify before distribution". The desk checks 4-Nov, finds a perfectly good print, concludes the gate is noise, clicks Yes - and the two genuinely wrong cells (+10.0 on 23-Dec and 10-Feb, on a flat day) publish to the blast, the workbook, the sheet email and clients, endorsed by an investigation that looked at the wrong row. Branch: OutlierGuard.cs:54 takes the median of the body itself, so a fault present in a majority of a three-element body BECOMES the reference; :55 then collapses MAD to 0 and :56 pins the threshold to the 4bp floor, which is exactly what makes the good row clear it. The structure is guaranteed for any run with 4 published rows - the shipped MPC/RBNZ/RIKSBANK runs routinely publish 3-5.

**Against the existing suite.** 41 attacks the same cross-sectional arm with FIVE rows and ONE outlier - the case the estimator is built for, where the median is clean. This is the minimum-body case (3) with the fault in the majority, so the estimator's own breakdown point (a 3-element median tolerates one contaminated point, not two) is the target. New.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Best scenario in the batch. I re-derived it line by line and it is exact. Four ticker-dated rows clear OutlierGuard.cs:46 (vals.Count < 4); the front is dropped at :51-52 leaving body.Count = 3, clearing :53. body = [0.0, +10.0, +10.0]: Median (:86-89) on an odd count returns s[1] = +10.0; deviations [10.0, 0.0, 0.0] -> Median returns 0.0; thresh = max(4.0, 4x0.0) = 4.0 (:56). The two faulty rows: |10.0 - 10.0| = 0.0, not > 4.0 -> silent, and |10.0| > 12.0 false so no absolute flag either. The honest row: |0.0 - 10.0| = 10.0 > 4.0 and |0.0| <= 12.0 -> flagged at :58-61. Exactly the good row is named. The neighbour-misprint guard genuinely cannot help: RejectedBy needs |m0 - midpoint| > 25bp with neighbours agreeing within 25bp (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1029-1034), and a 10bp error clears neither leg. This is the estimator's breakdown point (a 3-element median tolerates one contaminated point, not two) and it is guaranteed for any run publishing exactly 4 rows — which the hard-data rule at PricingServiceBoards.cs:1052 produces routinely (scenario 36 already truncates RBNZ to 2 and BOC to 3). Genuinely distinct from 41, whose body is [+1,+1,+9,+2] with a clean median of +1.5 and MAD 0.5 (verified at Group08_Guards.cs:258-261). Severity P1 is right if anything understated: the note actively misdirects the investigation.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:46` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:51` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:53` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:54` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:58` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:86` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1027` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1031`

### 143. A SOURCES pick left on a thin contributor truncates the RBA to two rows: the futures guard silently switches itself off and "skipped" never reaches the gate

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** OPERATOR ERROR. Someone picked a contributor in the SOURCES dialog three weeks ago while chasing a quote and never reset it. The pick persists in sources.json and applies to every run. Does anything tell the desk that the independent exchange-settled cross-check for that bank is now disabled?

**Set-up.** Same, plus: leave every rung on the harness default 5m age (Spec.cs:255) and describe the fault as a MIS-KEYED live contributor page, not a stuck one — the page quotes actively, so no STALE note fires and the scenario stays about the guard. Keep DisableGuardFutures = false and the twelve IB contracts in Extras.

**What must publish.** Corrected walk from FuturesGuard.cs:56-64 with rows[0].Date = 15-Sep-26, rows[^1].Date = 27-Oct-26. Pass 1: a = 1-Sep-26, b = 1-Oct-26; a > today so it survives :63, then rows[0].Date (15-Sep) > a (1-Sep) -> continue. Pass 2: a = 1-Oct, b = 1-Nov; rows[0] 15-Sep <= 1-Oct passes but rows[^1] 27-Oct < 1-Nov -> continue. Passes 3-12: a moves past 15-Sep and the rows[0] leg fails from 1-Nov onward, because coverage needs rows[0].Date <= a AND rows[^1].Date >= b and only b <= 27-Oct can satisfy the second, which forces probe = 1-Sep. So no window exists FOR THESE DATES (state it that way; two-row runs can cover a window in general). Loop exits to :90 -> the un-prefixed skip note. OutlierGuard: vals.Count = 2 < 4 at :46, absolute bars see 0.0. Completeness silent. Front misprint unjudgeable (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1029).

**How it breaks.** Unchanged, and the assertions are right as written: assert no CHECK note mentions the RBA, assert 'skipped' appears in the notes with no CHECK prefix, and assert the front row publishes Mid 3.350 / Priced -25.0 / -100% of 25bp.

**Against the existing suite.** 36 truncates a run (far rung unquoted on a decision day) and 34 exercises contributor-vs-composite pricing; 42/43 exercise the futures guard when it CAN find a window. None asserts what the guard does when the window search finds nothing, nor that the skip note misses the CHECK gate. New angle on the same guard.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The finding stands and the angle is new — 36 truncates a run but leaves DisableGuardFutures at its default true (Spec.cs:54), so it says nothing about what the guard does when its window search finds nothing, and 42/43 only exercise the case where it does. I verified the silent-off paths: FuturesGuard.cs:49 ('run too short'), :90 ('no covered, quoted contract window found — skipped') and :39 (any exception) are all UN-prefixed, so ConfirmChecks's StartsWith(OutlierGuard.Prefix + ":") filter at MainWindow.xaml.cs:334 drops every one of them. OutlierGuard's cross-sectional arm is off at :46 with 2 rows and the absolute bars see 0.0. The completeness gate is silent because rows exist (CompoundedFixing.cs:103-109). The front misprint cannot be caught: RejectedBy's lo >= 1 test (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1029) never judges rung 1. Guard tolerance default 2.5 confirmed at PricingServiceBoards.cs:131; RBA guardFutures 'IB{MY} Comdty'/monthavg confirmed at config/meetings.json:303-304. THREE fixes. (1) The window walk is off by one month: probe starts at the 1st of the CURRENT month and the loop's first statement is probe = probe.AddMonths(1) (FuturesGuard.cs:56-58), so candidate 1 is a = 1-Sep-26 / b = 1-Oct-26 and it fails on rows[0].Date (15-Sep) > a, not on the coverage leg. (2) The generalisation 'a two-row run can never span two consecutive month boundaries when meetings are ~6 weeks apart' is false — rows at 25-Sep and 10-Nov would pass both legs for a = 1-Oct / b = 1-Nov. The chosen dates do fail, so the conclusion holds; the reasoning must be replaced with the specific walk. (3) A contributor page frozen for a week would trip the STALE watch (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1073) and pre-empt the finding; the honest fault is a page that ticks but at the wrong level.

### 144. Declining the weekly CHECK gate does not un-publish the 28 investor-facing dashboards, and nothing ever guarded them anyway

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | high confidence | live-or-manual-only | corrected`

**The question.** OPERATOR ERROR. The desk presses WEEKLY RUN, the futures guard triggers on the ECB, and the desk clicks No at the gate. What is already on disk, what is unlocked, and which of the three guards ever looked at the pages the external clients read?

**Set-up.** Split in two. (A) IMPLEMENTABLE, and the real finding: any store defect that reaches a strip — seed the mis-rung strip via Level/LevelStep — and assert that the strip row carries the wrong level while rep.Notes contains no note naming that currency, that bank or that row. Cite Surfaces.cs:159 for the harness path and MainWindow.xaml.cs:627 / EmailBuilder.cs:189-192 for why no guard can see it. (B) MANUAL, and a desk call rather than a defect: press WEEKLY, decline the gate on a real futures-guard breach, and record that out\*.html timestamps are newer than the run start, that OPEN OUTPUT is enabled, and that the status/log text (:596-599) names the dashboards but says nothing about them being unguarded. Acknowledge the :331 comment in the write-up.

**What must publish.** For (A): the strip publishes a wrong level and no note anywhere mentions it — CurrencyPage.cs:48 consumes only Panels.From(RollingStrip.ForMeetings(...)) and discards StripTable.Notes entirely, and none of the three guards runs on that path. For (B): drop 'the desk believes nothing went out'; the log DOES say dashboards were rebuilt and OPEN OUTPUT unlocked. The residual question is only whether 'rebuilt' should also say 'and were never guarded'.

**How it breaks.** The 28 per-currency pages, the movers hub, movers.json and the emailed single-file pack are the only investor-facing surfaces in the product, and no OutlierGuard bar, no cross-sectional flag, no futures cross-check, no completeness count and no STALE line ever touches them — on a clean day as much as on a declined one. Drop the un-publish claim; keep the unguarded-surface claim.

**Against the existing suite.** 51, 52, 53 and 59 check the CONTENT of the published dashboard strips around a decision. None asks whether any guard or gate governs that surface, and the harness cannot reach MainWindow at all. 43/44 establish that a breach must reach the CHECK gate; this asks what the gate actually protects.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The core is confirmed and important, but two claims are wrong and the harness assessment is too pessimistic. Confirmed: RenderAll runs inside the Task.Run at MainWindow.xaml.cs:548 and ConfirmChecks only at :564, so the 28 currency pages, index.html, movers.json and the single-file pack (:640-657) are on disk before the gate; the No path sets only emailErr and SetOutputButtons(email: false, output: pages > 0) at :592 leaves OPEN OUTPUT lit. Confirmed and the strongest half: RenderAll builds its own PricingService on an EMPTY RatesSnapshot from store data at :627, and FuturesGuard.Check / OutlierGuard.Check / the completeness gate run only inside EmailBuilder.Build (EmailBuilder.cs:189-192), so the dashboard surfaces have zero guard coverage. WRONG (1): 'The desk reads CANCELLED and believes nothing went out'. The log immediately says otherwise — :565-566 logs 'WEEKLY EMAIL CANCELLED', then :596-599 logs 'WEEKLY PARTIAL — dashboards rebuilt (OPEN OUTPUT unlocked) but the email FAILED'. The desk IS told the dashboards were rebuilt; what it is not told is that they were never guarded. WRONG (2): framing the gate ORDERING as a suspected defect. The author documents it deliberately at :331 — '(data-store upkeep and dashboards from the build phase have already run)' — so that half is a desk call, not a bug. Also the harness CAN reach the strip content: Surfaces.Strips is built for every bank via RollingStrip.ForMeetings at Surfaces.cs:159-161 and dumped to strips.txt. Cited lines are one or two off (:563 is :564, :616 is :618).

### 145. Global repricing day: 40+ CHECK lines join into one un-scrollable MessageBox, and an unattended run stops dead on it

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** OPERATOR ERROR. The gate is one Win32 MessageBox with every CHECK note concatenated, no cap, no ordering, no severity. On the day it matters most it fires most. Is it readable, and can a run ever complete without a human at the keyboard?

**Set-up.** Unchanged. Assert note count >= 44 and joined length >= ~5,000 characters in a Custom lambda; note that the exact count depends on how many rows also clear the cross-sectional test, and pin the ORDER assertion instead: the FUTURES GUARD TRIGGERED line's index in rep.Notes is lower than the outlier notes' but higher than the completeness and coherence notes (EmailBuilder.cs:183-192).

**What must publish.** Unchanged for the volume half. Delete the 'no non-interactive entry point' derivation. The modal geometry (no scroll, sized to the work area, clipped tail) stays a manual look.

**How it breaks.** Keep the first paragraph verbatim — a FUTURES GUARD TRIGGERED line indistinguishable from 44 lines that are just a market that moved, in a box the desk cannot read, training the learned Yes the :327-331 comment warns about. Replace the second paragraph: the unattended failure mode is NOT a hang. src/RateDesk.Weekly.Cli/Program.cs:175-202 runs DailyBuilder.Build then DailyBuilder.Render with no gate, so a scheduled run writes the blast, the workbook, the email fragments, the shared-drive mirror and the save-down books (DailyBuilder.cs:419-424) and exits 0 with the CHECK notes reduced to '  daily note: ...' lines on stdout.

**Against the existing suite.** 45 proves note text never leaks into client surfaces, with four notes firing. This is the volume and triage question - forty-four notes, no cap, no severity order, no scroll, no timeout - which 45 does not touch. The note-count and joined-length assertions are pure Custom-lambda work; only the modal's geometry needs eyes.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The volume/triage half is sound. ConfirmChecks does string.Join("\n", checks) into one MessageBox.Show with no cap, no sort and no scroll (MainWindow.xaml.cs:334-342); the absolute bars fire per row per horizon at 12/30/50 (OutlierGuard.cs:23, :41-45) with no decision-day or repricing-cycle exemption; note order is insertion order and FuturesGuard lands BEFORE the outlier flood (EmailBuilder.cs:189 then :192), so the one line that is almost never benign is buried; 8 rows per bank is exactly the report's cap (Surfaces.cs:87 / BuildWeekly meetingsPerRun: 8); the author's own :327-331 comment is the right thing to quote against it. The SECOND HALF IS FALSE and must go. There IS a non-interactive entry point, in a project the candidate did not look in: src/RateDesk.Weekly.Cli/Program.cs exposes 'daily' (:175-202) and 'email' (:147-173), which call DailyBuilder.Build/Render and EmailBuilder.Build/Render with NO ConfirmChecks anywhere — so a scheduled run does not hang on a modal, it publishes straight through every CHECK note. That is a bigger finding than the one claimed and it inverts the conclusion; I have raised it separately as cli-daily-publishes-through-every-check. Minor: '44 CHECK notes' is a floor, not an exact count — rows whose |Δ1w| <= 30 can still earn cross-sectional notes (OutlierGuard.cs:58 only suppresses a row that already cleared the absolute bar), so the assertion should be a lower bound plus a joined-length bound.

### 146. The inflation lone-mover note says NON-BLOCKING in its own doc and is CHECK-prefixed, so one bad RPI tenor cancels the whole daily OIS product

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | derived by hand`

**The question.** The desk's standing rule is that CHECK blocks and STALE does not. Which of those did the v0.17.0 lone-mover test actually become?

**Set-up.** An ordinary daily run, OIS side entirely clean, nine banks, no decision. On the RPI strip one tenor prints a lone mover — the measured base rate is roughly 8 tenor-days per three months per family, and BPSWIF3 on 27-Aug-26 (415.000 against neighbours at 429-435) is the worked example already in FINDINGS.md. Press DAILY at 16:20.

**What must publish.** By the note's own doc comment — 'NON-BLOCKING and never suppressed: the number still publishes, the desk is just told which one to look at' — the run completes, the blast, workbook and shared-drive mirror are written, and the desk sees an informational panel naming the tenor. That is also the treatment InflHistory.StaleNotes gets, which emits a 'STALE:' prefix and lands in the non-blocking ShowStaleNotes path.

**How it breaks.** CoherenceNotes emits RateDesk.Core.OutlierGuard.Prefix, i.e. the literal string 'CHECK' (InflHistory.cs:580). DailyBuilder.cs:317 adds it to rep.Notes, and MainWindow.ConfirmChecks (:334) selects every note starting 'CHECK:' except PRE-CLOSE into a modal MessageBox whose No answer aborts the render: 'daily CANCELLED at the outlier check — nothing written.' So a bad quote on a single UK inflation tenor now gates the entire daily OIS product — a blast, a workbook and an email about nine central banks that have nothing to do with RPI — and does it roughly weekly. The desk learns to answer Yes without reading, which is precisely the training effect the PRE-CLOSE exclusion was written to avoid ('a modal that fires every morning trains the desk to click through the gate that matters', MainWindow.xaml.cs:327-329). Either the doc comment is wrong or the prefix is; they cannot both stand.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 45 (notes are not content) and to the 40-CHECK-lines scenario, but the defect here is the ROUTING of one specific note family, not the volume.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** InflHistory.cs:580 emits OutlierGuard.Prefix = "CHECK" (OutlierGuard.cs:16), DailyBuilder.cs:317 puts it in rep.Notes, and MainWindow.xaml.cs:334 selects every "CHECK:" note except PRE-CLOSE into the blocking modal at :788 — so the doc comment at InflHistory.cs:555 ("NON-BLOCKING and never suppressed") is contradicted by the code, and one RPI tenor aborts the whole daily. Nothing is written when it fires, so P1 is right.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Infl/InflHistory.cs:554` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Infl/InflHistory.cs:580` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Infl/InflHistory.cs:623` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:317` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:334` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:788`

### 147. The 27-Aug bad RPI close still publishes on the public GBP dashboard while the email publishes the arbitrated mark

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** Did the 2026-08-31 close-vs-snap arbitration actually reach every client-facing surface, or only the email/xlsx? Two zero-sign-in surfaces must not disagree about the same tenor on the same day.

**Set-up.** Replay the documented day: RPI, 27-Aug-2026. BPSWIF3 (Mar-27) close 415.000, 16:15 snap 435.500, live mid ~434.6, 20-Aug close 433.000; the other eleven tenors flat to +0.5bp (FINDINGS.md:106-119). Store seeded so the `daily` table holds the raw closes and `Maintain(bars)` has run so the `fixings` table holds the arbitrated 435.500 for (RPI, 2027-03, 27-Aug). Render BOTH the email fragment (InflEmail.WriteFragments) and the GBP currency page (CurrencyPage.Body) with asOf = 27-Aug. Nothing is faked but the bars provider.

**What must publish.** One number for Mar-27 on both surfaces. Dashboard value = 435.500/100 = 4.355%, and its 1w change against the 20-Aug mark 433.000 = (4.355-4.330)x100 = +2.5bp. Email Mid = 416.50 x (1+435.500/10000) = 434.60 (UKRPI Mar-26 base 416.50 per CpiFixings.cs:26).

**How it breaks.** The public GBP page prints "GBP RPI monthly fixings · Mar 27 · 4.150% · 1w -18.0" - a 20.5bp error with the sign flipped - while the email prints 4.346%/Mid 434.60 the same afternoon. CpiFixings.Build never touches the fixings table: it reads store.ValueAsOf(ticker, asOf) (CpiFixings.cs:87-89), which is a raw walk-back over the `daily` closes (HistoryStore.cs:422-433). The arbitration writes only into `fixings` (InflHistory.cs:264-293), so the entire 2026-08-31 fix is invisible to the 28 dashboards. Second-order: the dashboard's 1w/1m use WeeklyCurves.WeekDays/MonthAgo (7d walk-back, same-day-last-month) while the email uses exact -1bd/-7d/-28d, so the two surfaces cannot agree even on a clean day.

**Against the existing suite.** none — scenarios 51-53/59 cover the OIS meeting strips on the dashboards, never the inflation panels; InflMarkQualityTests covers Maintain only, never a renderer

**Critic (CORRECTED).** CpiFixings.cs:87 does read store.ValueAsOf (raw `daily` walk-back, HistoryStore.cs:422) while Maintain writes only to `fixings` (InflHistory.cs:292), and CurrencyPage.cs:75 publishes it with changeScale 100 — so the divergence is real. But the email card's Mid comes from CollectLiveMarks (InflHistory.cs:401), the live snapshot mark, not the arbitrated fixings row. **Change before writing it:** The email/dashboard contrast must be set on the Δ1d/1w/1m anchors (BuildDisplayRows reads `fixings` at InflHistory.cs:492-511) — the card's Mid never touches the arbitration. Also 416.50 x 1.04355 = 434.64, not 434.60 (434.60 is the live 434.6bp mark), and rendering CurrencyPage.Body needs a harness extension: README.md:197 puts dashboards and inflation out of scope.

### 148. Bloomberg serves a frozen RPI strip for two days: every Delta reads 0.00, both discriminators are themselves zero, no note fires

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** When the feed - not the market - is flat, does anything in the inflation path say so? The desk's standing rule is that a rung off a feed quiet >1h gets a STALE line.

**Set-up.** The documented 02-03 Mar-2026 case, re-dated: freeze Mon and Tue, run Wed. All twelve BPSWIF closes and 16:15 snaps on Mon and Tue are byte-identical to Friday's; LAST_UPDATE on every one of the twelve is 51h old; Wednesday's live snapshot still serves the same PX_LAST. Sep-26 yoy 430.00 throughout, base UKRPI Sep-25 = 410.00. The previous Wednesday's stored mark was 428.00. Meanwhile the real ILS strip repriced +15bp over the two frozen days.

**What must publish.** The marks still publish (nothing is invented) PLUS a STALE line naming the family - "RPI: all 12 fixings unchanged across the last 3 saves, quotes last updated 51h ago" - and, per the >1h rule the meeting boards already apply, the Delta1d cells marked or blanked. The 15bp the market actually moved = 410.00 x 0.0015 = 0.615 index points of understatement, which is what the STALE line is protecting.

**How it breaks.** The email/xlsx/save-down print Mid 427.63 (410.00 x 1.0430) for Sep-26 - Friday's number under Wednesday's date - with all eleven Delta1d cells at exactly 0.00 and Delta1w at +0.08, and NO note anywhere. Three separate discriminators degenerate together: StaleNotes returns at InflHistory.cs:611 because `moved(0) < live.Count/2(6)` (its comment asserts a family that did not move is "a quiet day, not staleness"); Maintain's strip median is 0.0 and close==snap so arbitration is a no-op (lines 255-273); CoherenceNotes sees every D1 = 0 so every residual is 0 (line 569-579). And nothing on this path ever reads q.AgeMinutes - Staleness.Assess is wired only at PricingServiceAnalytics.cs:202, and the >1h rung rule only at PricingServiceBoards.cs:1073. Three surfaces agreeing cell-for-cell makes it read as verified.

**Against the existing suite.** attacks the same MECHANISM as 24 (staleness cap) and 37 (published rung >1h quiet on decision day) from the inflation side, where the guard does not exist at all rather than being capped

**Critic (CORRECTED).** StaleNotes returns at InflHistory.cs:611 exactly as claimed (moved=0 < live.Count/2), and Staleness.Assess is wired only at PricingServiceAnalytics.cs:202 with the >1h rung rule only at PricingServiceBoards.cs:1073 — no quote age reaches this path. But the scenario's own `expected` says the marks still publish unchanged, so the defect is a missing note, not a wrong number.

### 149. MATURITY comes back empty on the Dec-26 RPI print day: the card publishes a phantom Dec-26 forecast and the public page prints the roll as -16.2bp in a week

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** What does the strip publish when Bloomberg serves the price but not the MATURITY field on the one day of the year that ticker re-points? Line 400 guards for exactly that hiccup - is the fallback safe?

**Set-up.** RPI, Dec-26 print day (RPI prints 16-22d after month end - the app should be reading it off ECO_RELEASE_DT, not a calendar). BPSWIF12 has re-pointed: it now means Dec-27 and trades 408.00 yoy bp; the snapshot returns Mid but no MATURITY this run. Store: yesterday's maturity record for BPSWIF12 = 01-Feb-2027 (Dec-26 + 2m GBP lag, CpiFixings.cs:13-17); yesterday's Dec-26 mark 424.20; 13-Jan close 424.20; 20-Dec close 425.00. Prints: UKRPI Dec-25 = 413.00, Jan-26 = 409.70, Dec-26 = 430.60 (released that morning). Jan-27 fixing yoy 420.00.

**What must publish.** Dec-27 is the furthest month, so it is DROPPED (InflEmail.cs:116) and no Dec-26 row exists at all - the front row is Jan-27, Mid = 409.70 x 1.0420 = 426.91, MoM anchored on the print just released: 426.91/430.60 - 1 = -0.857% -> "-0.86". On the dashboard the 1w/1m for that ticker must be BLANK with the "lookback(s) suppressed" note, because MaturityAsOf(-7d) != the live maturity.

**How it breaks.** CollectLiveMarks falls back to store.MaturityLatest (InflHistory.cs:400) = the stale 01-Feb-2027 record, RefMonth(01-Feb-2027,12) returns Dec-2026, and the card gains a PHANTOM front row: "Dec 26 | Base 413.00 | Mid 429.85 | YoY 4.08" - a published forecast for a month that printed at 430.60 that morning, wrong by 0.75 index pts (17bp). It is internally self-consistent, so nothing on the row looks odd. It also drags Jan-27's MoM off the phantom instead of the print: 426.91/429.85 - 1 = -0.68 vs -0.86. On the public GBP page the same stale record makes s.Mat == thenMat so CpiFixings.LookBack does NOT suppress (CpiFixings.cs:127-130), and the roll publishes as a market move: "Dec 26 · 4.080% · 1w -16.2 · 1m -17.0" on an already-fixed month. CoherenceNotes does fire (D1 = 429.85 - 430.51 = -0.66 vs tol 12/10000 x 413.00 = 0.4956), but it is non-blocking and it accuses a "bad mark" when the fault is a mis-identified contract.

**Against the existing suite.** same family of fault as 23 (renumber day: feed re-pointed, mid(N) vs PrevClose(N+1)) and 39 (fixing ticker unquoted on decision day), but on the annual CPI-fixing re-point, where the fallback is MaturityLatest rather than a rung map

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** InflHistory.cs:400 falls back to MaturityLatest and RefMonth(01-Feb-2027,12) returns Dec-2026 (line 635-643), so the phantom front row publishes through InflEmail.cs:116; CpiFixings.cs:66 uses MaturityLatest for slots and CpiFixings.cs:127-130 cannot suppress when thenMat equals it. Arithmetic checks: 413.00 x 1.0408 = 429.85, 409.70 x 1.0420 = 426.91, MoM -0.86 vs -0.68, dashboard 1w -16.2 / 1m -17.0.

### 150. An adopted old-basis CPTFEMU base beside a real one: the HICP card prints Mid 28% high and MoM -21.76 while YoY stays right

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | moderate confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** The hole-adoption path writes the external sheet's Base into the CPTFEMU print table. CPTFEMU exists on two bases 1.281085 apart. What stops a 2015-basis base sitting next to a 2026-basis one in the same strip?

**Set-up.** A desk machine restored from a StoreBackup where CPTFEMU was never deep-seeded (seed is 45d by design, CLAUDE.md), so `prints` holds only the newest one or two months. The desk ingests the incumbent HICP_History workbook, whose rows before Feb-2026 carry OLD-basis Base values (documented, InflHistory.cs:36-38). Sheet Base for Jan-2026 = 131.90 (old basis; new-basis equivalent 131.90/1.281085 = 102.96); Base for Feb-2026 = 103.15 (new basis). Live EUSWIF1 (Jan-27) 240.0 yoy bp, EUSWIF2 (Feb-27) 245.0 yoy bp. Sheet rows dated ~11 days ago, so 31-Jan-2026 < obs-45d and adoption fires.

**What must publish.** Either refuse to adopt a base whose basis cannot be established, or normalise it: Mid(Jan-27) = 102.96 x 1.0240 = 105.43, Mid(Feb-27) = 103.15 x 1.0245 = 105.68, MoM(Feb-27) = 105.68/105.43 - 1 = +0.237% -> "0.24". A monotone strip, MoM in tenths of a percent.

**How it breaks.** The HICP card, the DRAX Fixing Runs attachment and the save-down HICP_History page all print Jan-27 Base 131.90 / Mid 135.07 (131.90 x 1.0240) / YoY 2.40 - self-consistent and 28.1% high - then Feb-27 Base 103.15 / Mid 105.68 / MoM -21.76 (105.68/135.07 - 1). YoY is untouched because yoy = value/100 (InflHistory.cs:468), so a sane YoY column sits beside a Mid column with a 30-point cliff, and a client reading only the Jan-27 line gets a plausible-looking HICP forecast index that is 28% wrong. Nothing catches it: adoption at lines 127-134 records the base with no basis test; MatchesPrint DELIBERATELY accepts either basis (line 690-692) so validation cannot flag it; the internal-consistency gate at line 105 passes because the sheet row is old-basis throughout; PrintsOf prefers bbg but there is no bbg row to prefer. Delta columns use today's base on both sides (line 502-505), so a real 5bp yoy move on that row prints +0.066 instead of +0.051 - 28% too big, quietly.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Adoption at InflHistory.cs:127-135 records the sheet base with no basis test, and with the 45d seed prints lacks (1,2026) so `own is null` is the routine path, not an edge case; Mid = 131.90 x 1.024 = 135.07 via line 469 while yoy stays 2.40, and MoM 105.68/135.07-1 = -21.76 all check out. **Change before writing it:** Fix the citations in break_mode: MatchesPrint's either-basis acceptance is InflHistory.cs:658 (not 690-692), the internal-consistency gate is 101-102 (not 105), and yoy = value/100 is line 469 (not 468).

### 151. One month unquoted in the middle of the strip and the next row's MoM % silently becomes a two-month change (-0.52 vs -0.84)

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** MoM chains mid-over-PREVIOUS-ROW's-mid. When a month drops out of the strip - documented as routine for the thin front months - does the next row's MoM still mean one month?

**Set-up.** RPI, a Thursday in Jan-2027. BPSWIF12 (Dec-26) returns no Mid this run (documented: "BPSWIF8 skipped a day entirely", InflHistory.cs:585-588), so CollectLiveMarks skips it at line 401. Marks: Nov-26 426.0, [Dec-26 absent], Jan-27 420.0 yoy bp. Prints: UKRPI Nov-25 = 411.60, Dec-25 = 413.00, Jan-26 = 409.70.

**What must publish.** Either blank the Jan-27 MoM cell or anchor it on the actual previous calendar month. Mid(Nov-26) = 411.60 x 1.0426 = 429.13; Mid(Dec-26, had it quoted) = 413.00 x 1.0424 = 430.51; Mid(Jan-27) = 409.70 x 1.0420 = 426.91. True MoM(Jan-27) = 426.91/430.51 - 1 = -0.837% -> "-0.84" - the January sales effect the desk trades.

**How it breaks.** prevMid is "the previous ROW", not "the previous MONTH" (InflHistory.cs:470, chained at 489/496), and nothing checks RefMonth continuity. Jan-27's MoM prints 426.91/429.13 - 1 = -0.519% -> "-0.52": a two-month change labelled MoM %, the January seasonal shown as barely half its size, 0.32 percentage points wrong on the one column a linker desk reads for month-on-month. Completely silent - StaleNotes only inspects fixings PRESENT on the latest save (line 606, ContainsKey(days[0])), so an absent month is not in `live` at all, and the card just renders 10 rows where it usually renders 11. Same defect in the save-down HICP/RPI_History pages, which chain prevMid inside each day's block (StoreBooks.cs:305-317) where missing months are commoner still.

**Against the existing suite.** none — 36 truncates a run at an unquoted FAR rung; this is a hole in the MIDDLE of an inflation strip and it corrupts a neighbour rather than shortening the table

**Critic (CORRECTED).** DeriveRow's anchor is `prevMid ?? previous month's print` (InflHistory.cs:470) with prevMid assigned from the previous ROW at line 514, and StoreBooks.cs:305-320 chains the same way per day-block — the mechanism is exactly as described. The setup's months are impossible: in Jan-2027 Nov-26 has already printed, so BPSWIF11 points to Nov-2027 and cannot be in the strip. **Change before writing it:** Shift the trio one month forward: Dec-26 present, Jan-27 ABSENT, Feb-27 present (bases UKRPI Dec-25 413.00, Jan-26 409.70, Feb-26 410.90), and the corrupted cell is Feb-27's MoM. The hole must have a row before it — if the absent month is the front, prevMid is null and DeriveRow falls to the previous month's print, so MoM is right or blank and there is no bug.

### 152. The close-vs-snap arbitration measures its reference against yesterday's CHOSEN mark, not yesterday's close, exactly as its own comment says it must not

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | moderate confidence | needs-harness-extension | derived by hand`

**The question.** The v0.16.9 fix judges each tenor's two candidate marks against the strip's median move, 'measured on closes so the reference never depends on what is being chosen'. Does it?

**Set-up.** Two consecutive days on the RPI strip. Day 1 is the 02-03 Mar-2026 shape already documented in the repo: Bloomberg's CLOSE series is junk across the whole strip (stuck), the 16:15 snaps are good, so the arbitration correctly takes the snap for all twelve tenors — a systematic close-minus-snap gap of, say, 20bp. Day 2 the closes come back and the strip genuinely moves +4bp. Seed both series so the two candidate marks on Day 2 are 4bp apart on one tenor under test.

**What must publish.** On Day 2 the reference median must be the strip's true close-to-close move, +4bp, so the tenor takes whichever candidate lands nearest +4bp. The reference must be identical whether or not Day 1 chose snaps, because the comment promises it is built from closes alone.

**How it breaks.** strip.Add(cv - pv) uses cv = today's close but pv = prev[m], and prev[m] was assigned pick — the ARBITRATED mark — at the bottom of the previous day's loop (InflHistory.cs:257 and :275). So after any day on which snaps won, every tenor's contribution to the next day's median is displaced by that day's close-minus-snap gap; when snaps won across the whole strip the median itself moves by ~20bp, and the arbitration then prefers whichever Day-2 candidate is 20bp away from the truth. The reference is not independent of the choice — it is a one-day-lagged function of it, which is the one property the comment two lines above rules out. Two further holes in the same loop: a tenor with no mark on some day keeps its stale prev, so its next 'day-over-day' change silently spans two or more days and is compared against a one-day median (RPI's own day-to-day dispersion is 2-6bp, so a two-day change is systematically about 1.4x too large and pulls the wrong candidate); and when no tenor has both a close and a prev the median falls back to a hard 0.0, which on a sparse day biases every tenor toward whichever mark moved LESS — under-reporting real moves on exactly the thin days the test exists for.

**Against the existing suite.** none — no existing scenario reaches InflHistory at all; the harness README puts inflation out of scope.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** prev[m] = pick at InflHistory.cs:275 is fed straight back into strip.Add(cv - pv) at line 257, so the reference is a lagged function of the choices and the comment at 253-254 is false — worth locking. But the median is built from CLOSES, so a systematic close error defines it and the closes always win: on the stuck-strip Day 1 as specified med = 0 and every tenor takes its close, so 'snaps won across the whole strip' and the ~20bp median displacement cannot occur. **Change before writing it:** Recast the break as the reachable one: one or two tenors taking the snap on Day 1 (Mar-27 27-Aug, 20bp gap) makes their own next-day contribution mix a close against a snap, contaminating 1-2 of 12 samples — a rank shift in the median, not a 20bp shift. Keep the two sub-claims that do hold: a stale prev spanning multiple days, and the hard med = 0.0 fallback at line 260.

### 153. On BPSWIF12's re-point day the close/snap arbitration prefers whichever mark sits nearer the PRE-ROLL Dec-26 level

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** The 2026-08-31 arbitration judges each tenor against its own previous day. On the one day a year the ticker means a different contract, what does "its own previous day" mean?

**Set-up.** RPI, Dec-26 print day (~20-Jan-2027). BPSWIF12 re-points from Dec-26 to Dec-27: maturity 01-Feb-2027 -> 01-Feb-2028. Yesterday's stored mark 424.00 (Dec-26, effectively fixed). Today the new contract's CLOSE is 408.00, corroborated by Nov-27 at 407.50 and Jan-28 at 408.50; the 16:15 SNAP is 419.00 - a thin first print of the new contract, 11bp high. The other eleven tenors move -0.25bp, so the strip median med = -0.25. Prints: UKRPI Dec-26 = 430.60.

**What must publish.** Suspend arbitration on a tenor's own re-point day - the day-over-day change is undefined - and take the close, 408.00, or refuse the day entirely. This is exactly the rule CpiFixings.LookBack already implements for lookbacks (CpiFixings.cs:127-130: ask MaturityAsOf, suppress when the ticker rolled).

**How it breaks.** prev[12] = 424.00 is the OLD contract, so |419.00-424.00+0.25| = 4.75 beats |408.00-424.00+0.25| = 15.75 and the arbitration stores the BAD SNAP, 419.00, precisely because it is nearer the pre-roll level (InflHistory.cs:272). The maturity is only consulted afterwards, to key the row (line 284). Consequences: Hist_RPI in Inflation_Fixings_History.xlsx prints the Dec-27 Delta1w on 27-Jan as 409.50 - 419.00 = -9.5 y/y bp where the truth is +1.5 (walk-back anchors, InflBook.cs:60-65); and on 17-Feb, when Jan-27 prints and Dec-27 stops being the dropped furthest month, the card's Delta1m anchor is exactly the poisoned 20-Jan row: 430.60 x (410.00-419.00)/10000 = -0.39 index pts printed where the truth is 430.60 x (410.00-408.00)/10000 = +0.09. Sign flipped, 0.47 index pts = 11bp. CoherenceNotes cannot see it - it is Delta1d only, by deliberate design (InflHistory.cs:552-556).

**Against the existing suite.** the inflation analogue of 23/62 (a boundary day where the two arms of a guard disagree about which contract a mark belongs to); no existing scenario touches Maintain's arbitration

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** prev[12] is the old contract when line 272 evaluates |419-424+0.25| = 4.75 against |408-424+0.25| = 15.75, and the maturity is consulted only afterwards at line 284 to key the row — so the bad snap stores. InflBook.cs:42-48 confirms the walk-back At() that publishes 409.50 - 419.00 = -9.5, and 430.60 x (410-419)/10000 = -0.39 vs +0.09 is right; P1 is the correct call since 419.00 is itself a documented mark and the fault is the absent re-point gate.

### 154. "Days with no recorded maturity are skipped, not guessed" is false: a three-day run gap writes Aug-27 prices onto the fixed Aug-26 month

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** The class docstring says unrecorded days are skipped. MaturityAsOf returns the last record at-or-before the day, so no day after the first record is ever skipped. Which is it, and what does the gap cost?

**Set-up.** RPI. Desk offsite Tue-Thu: last run Mon 14-Sep-2026 16:20, next run Fri 18-Sep. Aug-26 RPI prints Wed 16-Sep at 07:00, BPSWIF8 re-points (maturity 01-Oct-2026 -> 01-Oct-2027). Maturity records exist for 14-Sep (old) and 18-Sep (new) only - both stamped DateTime.Today at run time (UpdateEngine.cs:93, DailyBuilder.cs:296-298). Closes: 15-Sep 302.00 (Aug-26, nearly fixed), 16-Sep 427.50, 17-Sep 428.90, 18-Sep 429.10 (all Aug-27; the documented BPSWIF6 roll was 295.75 -> 420.25, CpiFixings.cs:79). Base UKRPI Aug-25 = 404.10.

**What must publish.** 16 and 17-Sep are unattributable - no record of their own - so they are skipped, exactly as InflHistory.cs:20-23 promises, and Friday's run re-fetches nothing it cannot key. fixings(RPI,'2026-08') ends on 15-Sep at 302.00; fixings(RPI,'2027-08') starts 18-Sep at 429.10.

**How it breaks.** MaturityAsOf carries the 14-Sep record forward (HistoryStore.cs:302-313), so 16 and 17-Sep are keyed to ref month Aug-2026 - a month that FIXED on the morning of the 16th. Hist_RPI publishes Aug-26 Delta1d for 16-Sep = 427.50 - 302.00 = +125.50 y/y bp on an instrument that can no longer move, and the save-down History_RPI page renders it as Mid = 404.10 x 1.04275 = 421.37 against the true 404.10 x 1.0302 = 416.30. Meanwhile Aug-27 loses its first two days, so its Delta1w on 23-Sep and Delta1m on 14-Oct are BLANK - the display anchors are exact-date, not walk-back (InflHistory.cs:510-511). The store is the irreplaceable layer (CLAUDE.md), and FINDINGS.md:78-90 already established on the OIS side that a run-time record does not establish which contract a day's CLOSE belonged to; the inflation path leans on it anyway.

**Against the existing suite.** 57 walks the save-down OIS history across a decision and 13/15 cover anchors inside a mixed-state window; none covers an inflation ticker's annual re-point falling inside a run gap

**Critic (CORRECTED).** MaturityAsOf's `date<=@d ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT 1` (HistoryStore.cs:302-313) does carry the 14-Sep record onto 16/17-Sep, flatly contradicting the docstring at InflHistory.cs:20-23, and the exact-date anchors at 510-511 blank Aug-27's first lookbacks as claimed. The consequence is a genuinely wrong number on a published surface: Hist_RPI (InflBook.cs:52-65) prints Aug-26 Δ1d +125.50 and StoreBooks History_RPI prints Mid 404.10 x 1.04275 = 421.38 for a month that fixed on the 16th.

### 155. A 40bp Ofgem-cap STEP at Jan-27 fires the lone-mover note on both boundary months, one of which moved exactly 0.00

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** FINDINGS.md leaves the lone-mover test unsigned-off pending "confirm a genuine single-month event is not a real risk for UK RPI". Settle it: what shape of real event actually breaks the test, and at what size?

**Set-up.** RPI. Ofgem resets the January cap 9% lower (quarterly Jan/Apr/Jul/Oct; the announcement date needs desk sign-off, the quarterly structure and the fact a cap reset moves RPI are documented at FINDINGS.md:37,42). Energy's RPI weight puts -40bp of yoy on every fixing month FROM Jan-27 onward and nothing on Nov-26/Dec-26. Delta1d by month: Sep-26 0, Oct-26 0, Nov-26 0, Dec-26 0, Jan-27 -40, Feb-27 -40, ... Aug-27 -40. Bases: UKRPI Dec-25 = 413.00, Jan-26 = 409.70, Feb-26 = 410.90.

**What must publish.** No note. This is a coherent term-structure repricing, not a bad mark, and FINDINGS.md's own calibration says a real event's worst residual is 2.0-4.8bp. The generalisation that settles the caveat: for a clean step of size S at a boundary, the residual on EACH of the two boundary months is exactly S/2, so the test false-fires whenever S > 2 x LoneMoverBp = 24bp. The exposure is a STEP, not a lone genuine month, and not a parallel move (25-Aug's parallel -12bp passes, worst residual -4.75bp).

**How it breaks.** Two notes go into the run, both wrong. Dec-26 (i): neighbours are Oct-26 0.00, Nov-26 0.00, Jan-27 409.70x(-40)/10000 = -1.639, Feb-27 -1.644 -> median (-1.639+0)/2 = -0.819; own D1 = 0.00; tol = 12/10000 x 413.00 = 0.4956; |0 - (-0.819)| = 0.819 > 0.4956 -> "CHECK: RPI Dec-26 D1d 0.00 against -0.82 either side of it - that tenor moved ALONE...". A tenor that printed zero accused of moving alone. Jan-27 fires identically (own -1.639 vs median -0.819, residual 0.820). Because it is the fixing that moved 40bp correctly, the desk's instinct will be to raise LoneMoverBp - and at any threshold above 20bp the 18bp real bad marks the test exists for stop firing. The fix has to be structural (same-side neighbours, or a step-aware residual), not a bigger number: InflHistory.cs:571-579 takes the median of BOTH sides indiscriminately.

**Against the existing suite.** deliberately the same mechanism as 41 (cross-sectional outlier guard, front exempt) attacked from the inflation side, where the neighbour set is a curve rather than a meeting board and the front is NOT exempt

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** CoherenceNotes takes both sides indiscriminately (InflHistory.cs:571) and the even-count median at 575-577 gives (-1.639+0)/2 = -0.819 against tol 12/10000 x 413.00 = 0.4956, so both boundary months clear line 579 — the S/2 generalisation and the S > 24bp threshold are exact. Kind is right: FINDINGS.md:57-58 leaves the single-month-event caveat explicitly unsettled, and P1 holds because nothing wrong is published, the run is just gated.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Infl/InflHistory.cs:566` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Infl/InflHistory.cs:571` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Infl/InflHistory.cs:576` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Infl/InflHistory.cs:578` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Infl/InflHistory.cs:580` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Infl/InflHistory.cs:548` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:16`

## The compounded fixing

*Silent mechanics today, published numbers the moment the desk switches them on.*

### 156. Riksbank cut 50bp six days ago; the SWESTR tape stops the day before the new period, so the compounded fixing prints 2.0000 — the OLD policy rate

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** When the store holds no o/n fixing print dated on or after the current period start, does the compounded fixing publish nothing, or does it fill the PRE-decision rate forward across the boundary and report it as the realized floating leg of the post-decision period?

**Set-up.** Today Mon 31-Aug-26. RIKSBANK on the shipped entry (fixingDcc 360, SEK onFixingTicker = SWESTR Index, rollsAtPeriodStart). Decision Thu 20-Aug (decisionDates, decisionTimeLondon 08:30, Cal.TimePassed), rate applies from Wed 26-Aug (dates) — the real config pair, so the boundary is an EXACT grid date and ps.Derived is false. The Riksbank cut 50bp: SWESTR 2.000 -> 1.500 from 26-Aug. Seed the fixing series flat 2.000 from Cal.D(-70) through Tue 25-Aug and NOTHING on or after 26-Aug (b.FixingHistory ends 25-Aug) — the state after a run whose SWESTR gap-fill returned nothing (StoreBackedHistory.cs:51-65 swallows the failure and serves the store), or on a machine where SWESTR sat in the deferred tail of the 45-day seed (UpdateEngine.cs:26-34, MaxSeedPerRun 250). Live snap b.Fix(1.500) so the board's spot base is the post-cut rate. Rungs seeded normally so the published board is correct and unremarkable.

**What must publish.** BLANK. The window [26-Aug, 31-Aug) contains zero observations of the rate regime it is supposed to measure, so under the hard-data rule and the module's own claim ('fill-forward only across the publication lag — never guessed', CompoundedFixing.cs:23-24) there is nothing to publish. Had 26/27/28-Aug printed 1.500 (Fri 28 carrying 3 calendar days), the right answer is: growth = (1+0.015/360)^2 * (1+0.015*3/360); growth-1 = 2.08346e-4; x 360/5 x 100 = 1.5001. Assert run.CompoundedPct is null (or 1.5001 in the control arm where the prints exist).

**How it breaks.** run.CompoundedPct = 2.0000 exactly, CompoundedFrom = 26-Aug, Days = 5 — the pre-cut policy rate, 50.0bp wrong, frozen into daily_report.json. Path: line 44 tests staleness only against `to` ((31-Aug - 25-Aug) = 6 <= MaxStaleDays 7, PASSES); line 49 FindLastIndex(p.Date <= from) happily lands on the 25-Aug point; the loop at 51-65 finds no later point so `next` = to.Date (line 54), n = 5 <= 7 so the interior-hole guard at 61 never fires; one segment means the annualization at 66 inverts the compounding exactly, so it prints a suspiciously ROUND 2.0000 — indistinguishable from a quoted policy rate, which is precisely what makes it look right. There is no test anywhere that the series contains a point dated >= from. No note: Stamp (78-98) adds none, and its catch at 97 is empty.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 39 ('the o/n fixing ticker unquoted on a decision day') and 37 (published rung feed >1h quiet). Both attack the SPOT fixing on the board; neither touches the fixing HISTORY series or CompoundedFixing at all — no existing scenario asserts CompoundedPct.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Mechanism is real and untested: CompoundedFixing.cs:49 FindLastIndex lands on the 25-Aug point and :54 sets next=to, so 2.0000 prints, and the only existing coverage (CompoundedAndSourcesTests.cs:74-78) tests no fixing BEFORE the window, never none on/after it. But the value reaches no published surface — CompoundedPct is written at CompoundedFixing.cs:93 and read nowhere but tests — so P0 is wrong. **Change before writing it:** Re-anchor the setup to Cal.D offsets (period start Cal.D(-5), tape ending Cal.D(-6)) instead of the shipped 26-Aug/25-Aug pair: as written the staleness gate at CompoundedFixing.cs:44 kills the premise from 2-Sep-26 onward ((asOf - 25-Aug) > MaxStaleDays 7) and the scenario silently stops reproducing.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:44` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:49` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:51` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:54` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:61` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:66` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:23` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/StoreBackedHistory.cs:51` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/UpdateEngine.cs:26` | `config/meetings.json:477`

### 157. One bad NZOCRS print on a Friday weights three days and drags the compounded RBNZ fixing to 3.78 against a 2.50 OCR — the only history series in the app read without a despike

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** CompoundedFixing reads the o/n fixing history RAW while every other history consumer in the codebase despikes first. What does a single junk print — the exact fault the Hampel filter exists for — do to the compounded number, and does it also reach the shared-drive save-down workbook?

**Set-up.** Today Mon 31-Aug-26. RBNZ on the shipped entry (source NABZ, NZD onFixingTicker = NZOCRS Index, fixingDcc default 365). Its current period start is the DERIVED one the unit tests pin: Dates hold future starts only (2026-09-03 onward), PastDates hold the hand-entered decision 2026-07-08, median lag +1 => window start Thu 09-Jul-26, ps.Derived = true (CompoundedAndSourcesTests.cs:88 pins exactly this). RBNZ held at 2.50 all summer: seed NZOCRS flat 2.500 business-daily from Cal.D(-70) to Cal.D(-1) — then overwrite ONE point, Friday 24-Jul-26, with 25.00 (a decimal-shift junk print; 24-Jul is 15 days after the derived start, so it sits outside the +/-4-day derived guard). Second arm: overwrite the same Friday with 0.250 instead. Window [09-Jul, 31-Aug) = 53 calendar days; the Friday print carries Fri+Sat+Sun = 3 days.

**What must publish.** 2.5044. Flat 2.500 ACT/365 over 53 days: uplift ~ 2.500 * (0.025*53/730) = 0.0045, i.e. 2.5044 (weekend clustering trims the 4th decimal), consistent with the module's own validated reproduction (2.50 over 47 days -> 2.5039, CompoundedAndSourcesTests.cs:28-35). A despiked series returns 2.5044 in BOTH arms, because the tick is rewritten before the compounding sees it.

**How it breaks.** Arm A prints 3.7813. Hand check: sum of segment accruals = (0.025*50 + 0.25*3)/365 = 5.47945e-3; second-order cross terms 1.264e-5; growth-1 = 5.49212e-3; x 365/53 x 100 = 3.7813. That is 127.7bp above the OCR — a 'compounded NZOCRS 3.78 | OCR 2.50' line no client would forgive. Arm B prints 2.3767 (simple leg (2.500*50 + 0.250*3)/53 = 2.3726 plus uplift), i.e. -12.8bp — deliberately UNDER the 15bp bar the doc claims, so even the promised CHECK would have been silent. Path: CompoundedFixing.cs:133 calls history.GetDaily(refTicker, lookback) raw; every sibling consumer wraps the same call in HistoryFilter.Despike (PricingServiceAnalytics.cs:54, PricingServiceCorr.cs:107, PricingServiceRv.cs:97, PricingServiceBoards.cs:450). And the same raw series reaches a SAVE-DOWN BOOK: StoreBooks.cs:71 pulls it raw and RefAt at :74-76 walks back to it with no staleness cap, so Historical_NZ's Priced/Step/Percent columns for 24-26 Jul are computed against 25.00 and written into the macro-enabled .xlsm on the C+C share (StoreBooks.cs:87).

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 38 (neighbour-misprint guard) and 40/41 (OutlierGuard bars) — those guard PUBLISHED meeting rows against a misprint; nothing guards the fixing series, and the harness's own despike hazard note (README 'Seeding the market') applies to rung closes, not to FixingHistory, which Market.cs:108 upserts raw.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** CompoundedFixing.cs:133 does read GetDaily raw while PricingServiceAnalytics.cs:54 / Corr.cs:107 / Rv.cs:97 / Boards.cs:450 all wrap it in HistoryFilter.Despike, and Market.cs:108 upserts FixingHistory raw, so the 3.7813 arm is sound arithmetic on a reachable state. Severity is not P0 though: the compounded value is unrendered, and the .xlsm arm is not reachable from the harness — Surfaces.cs:132-147 only calls DailyBook.BankHistoryRows and never StoreBooks.WriteOis. **Change before writing it:** Drop the save-down claim from this scenario's assertions (StoreBooks.cs:71-87 is real but out of harness reach), and derive the window length from asOf at run time rather than pinning 53 days — the 09-Jul derived start is fixed by config/meetings.json RBNZ pastDates, so the expected number changes daily and Derived flips false once the 2026-09-03 grid date lands.

### 158. Desk hand-adds an ECB emergency cut to pastDates; the compounded window jumps to a date that never existed and prints 1.5001 instead of 1.8497

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** pastDates is documented as 'used only to stitch meeting-ticker HISTORY across rolls'. MeetingCalendar also derives period starts from it. What happens to the compounded window when the desk records an inter-meeting decision there — and does the +/-4-day derived guard catch a phantom boundary sitting six days after the real rate change?

**Set-up.** Today Mon 31-Aug-26. ECB on the shipped entry (fixingDcc 360, EUR onFixingTicker = ESTRON Index, no refTicker).
