DecisionDates = the real future list 2026-09-10 onward, so DecisionToStartLagDays returns exactly 6 for every configured pair. The ECB delivers an EMERGENCY 50bp cut announced Thu 20-Aug-26; ESTR goes 2.000 -> 1.500 from Fri 21-Aug. The maintenance period does NOT move: it is still [29-Jul, 16-Sep). The operator, following config/meetings.json:8, adds "2026-08-20" to ECB pastDates. Seed ESTRON business-daily 2.000 from Cal.D(-70) to Thu 20-Aug and 1.500 from Fri 21-Aug to Sun 30-Aug (FixHistStep). b.Fix(1.500).

**What must publish.** 1.8497. The window is the CURRENT maintenance period, [29-Jul, 31-Aug) = 33 days: 2.000 on 29-Jul..20-Aug (23 days), 1.500 on 21-Aug..30-Aug (10 days). Simple leg (2.000*23 + 1.500*10)/33 = 61.0/33 = 1.84848; sum of accruals 1.69444e-3, second-order 1.391e-6, growth-1 = 1.69584e-3, x 360/33 x 100 = 1.8497. **How it breaks.** CompoundedFrom = 26-Aug and CompoundedPct = 1.5001 — the post-cut rate reported as the whole period's realized leg, 35.0bp too low, describing a period start (26-Aug) that no ESTR maintenance period ever had. Path: MeetingCalendar.cs:43-46 maps the un-matched pastDate 20-Aug to 20-Aug + medianLag 6 = 26-Aug with Derived = true; :50-53 picks it as `best` because it is later than the real 29-Jul; CompoundedFixing.cs:129 takes it. The one guard written for exactly this (:139-145) then PASSES, because it only looks for a fixing CHANGE within +/-4 days of the phantom start and ESTR is dead flat at 1.500 across 22-30 Aug — the real change was six days earlier, just outside the radius. CompoundedFixing never consults MeetingCalendar.LagIsStable or LagRange (MeetingCalendar.cs:78-90), only ps.Derived. Silent: no note, and the board itself is unaffected because it re-bases off the market print, so every visible surface looks right.

No existing scenario writes a pastDate that is later than the last settled start.

Severity contradicts the scenario's own break_mode ("every visible surface looks right") — nothing published moves. **Change before writing it:** Expected value is 1.8500, not 1.8497: growth-1 = 1.695804e-3 x 360/33 x 100 = 1.84997, and the gap to 1.5001 is 35.0bp either way.

### 159. Stamp's doc promises a CHECK when the compounded value sits >15bp from spot; the body has no such comparison — and on the Riksbank's year-end period the bar as written would read 11bp and stay silent anyway

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** Is there ANY gate on the compounded fixing? And is the 15bp bar the doc describes even the right detector, on the one period the config already documents as legitimately dislocated?

**Set-up.** Two arms, same bank. RIKSBANK, shipped entry (fixingDcc 360, SWESTR, markTurnPeriods). Arm A is scenario 1's setup replayed: the compounded value comes out 2.0000 against a spot fixing of 1.500, a 50.0bp gap, and the run must carry a CHECK naming SEK. Arm B reproduces the shape of the period that spans a year end, which config/meetings.json:510-513 documents as a real SWESTR dislocation ('SWESTR drops hard on the last business day of the year ... the Dec-26 rung printed ~1.47 vs ~1.93 on Coremont'): period start Cal.Bd(-30) with the period grid written to match, SWESTR flat 1.750 for the first 26 calendar days, then a turn print of 0.900 as the last observation of the window carrying 4 calendar days (a Thursday print with the next print after `to`), and the live snap already recovered: b.Fix(1.750).

**What must publish.** Arm A: a CHECK-prefixed note naming SEK, and/or CompoundedPct withheld. Arm B: CompoundedPct = 1.6377 and NO false alarm. Hand check for B: 26 days at 1.750 and 4 at 0.900; simple leg (1.750*26 + 0.900*4)/30 = 49.1/30 = 1.63667; accrual sum 1.363889e-3, second-order 8.981e-7, growth-1 = 1.364787e-3, x 360/30 x 100 = 1.6377. The gap to the recovered spot is |1.6377 - 1.750| = 11.2bp.

**How it breaks.** Arm A: NO note of any kind. CompoundedFixing.cs:71-74 states 'A compounded value more than 15bp from the spot fixing earns a CHECK note'; the body at :78-98 sets run.CompoundedPct and run.CompoundedFrom and compares them to nothing, and the only notes Stamp adds (:103-116) are the completeness-gate ones. So a 50bp error is entirely unpoliced. Arm A also shows WHY the code has none: the desk's silence order (:85-87) and the unit test at CompoundedAndSourcesTests.cs:194-199 forbid the string 'compounded' on any surface, and notes reach every surface — so the CHECK as documented is unimplementable as written, and the code resolved the conflict silently in favour of silence rather than in favour of a gate. Arm B shows the bar is also mis-calibrated: on the year-end period it reads 11.2bp against the recovered spot and would stay quiet, but 73.8bp against the dislocated last print — the same period reads either side of the bar depending on which spot you pick, and the board's own Y/E Turn marking (markTurnPeriods, config:514) does not extend to the fixing window that spans the turn.

**Against the existing suite.** Uses the same Stamp entry point as 44 (completeness gate) and the same Y/E-turn period as 30/31/33, but from the opposite side: 44 asserts the notes Stamp DOES add; this asserts the one it documents and does not. 30/31/33 test the turn ROW on the board, never the compounded window that spans the turn. Desk call needed on the bar and on how a gate can exist under the silence order.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** The doc at CompoundedFixing.cs:71-74 promises a 15bp CHECK and the body at :78-98 compares CompoundedPct to nothing — only the completeness-gate notes at :103-116 are added — while CompoundedAndSourcesTests.cs:194-199 forbids the word on every surface, so the conflict is genuinely a desk call and P1 is right. **Change before writing it:** Arm B's period start must be Cal.D(-30), not Cal.Bd(-30): 30 business days is ~42 calendar days, which contradicts the 26+4 = 30-day window the 1.6377 derivation assumes.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:71` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:78` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:85` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:93` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:99` | `tests/RateDesk.Tests/CompoundedAndSourcesTests.cs:194` | `config/meetings.json:510` | `config/meetings.json:514`

### 160. NAB quotes the front three rungs only, so the 09-Dec RBA row on the public dashboard shows a +6.0 phantom month

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | moderate confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** SourceAwareTicker chooses the spelling PER RUNG. When a contract sits on a contributor-quoted rung today and a composite-only rung a month ago, does the change column difference two dealers?

**Set-up.** RBA on NABZ. Its last decision was 20 days ago, so one renumber boundary sits inside the 1m window. Contracts on the board today: 30-Sep (rung 1), 04-Nov (rung 2), 09-Dec (rung 3); a month ago the 09-Dec contract lived on rung 4. Store: NABZ closes/snaps exist for rungs 1-3 only (dealer pages quote the front of the strip and stop — supported by config/meetings.json:203-205 'meeting-only by nature' and the app's own composite ride-along at PricingServiceBoards.cs:581-585, but worth confirming on the terminal); rung 4 has no NABZ rows at all, composite rungs 1-6 complete. Quiet month: the 09-Dec contract was quoted 3.400 by NABZ then and now; composite rung 4 a month ago 3.340 (the same 6bp basis).

**What must publish.** Dashboard strip and movers series for 09-Dec-26: m1 = 0.0 (or blank). The contract did not move on the feed the desk selected; the spelling used must not change inside one contract's lookback window.

**How it breaks.** SourceAwareTicker resolves each rung independently on a 40-day probe — `hasSrc[n] = store.GetDaily(pat+" "+src, 40).Count > 0` (RollingStrip.cs:185-198) — so RolledValue reads rung 3 as "ADSF3A NABZ" today and rung 4 as "ADSF4A Curncy" a month ago (RollingStrip.cs:96-99, 170). m1 = (3.400 − 3.340)×100 = +6.0 on the 09-Dec row while the rows above it, whose whole window stayed inside NABZ-quoted rungs, read 0.0 — a strip publishing 0.0 / 0.0 / +6.0 on a dead month. MoverScan builds the same contract's series through the same func (MoverScan.cs:312-320), so the series carries a 6bp discontinuity at the 20-day-old boundary: both the 1w change and the est-σ are corrupted and the AUD 09-Dec row can take a DM hero card. The boards' OutlierGuard would CHECK-flag +6.0 against a median of 0.0 (OutlierGuard.cs:56-62), but the dashboards and the movers hub carry no guard and, by the desk's own presentation rule, no diagnostics at all — and they are the surfaces external readers see with no sign-in. The strip half is reachable the way 51-53 are; the movers half needs MoverScan wired into the harness.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 51-53/59 (dashboard strips on and after a decision day), which never vary the SPELLING; and to 22 (phantom step across a renumber), which is about the rung, not the contributor.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** THE BEST SCENARIO IN THE BATCH, and it is right where it matters. RollingStrip.cs:185-198 is verbatim a PER-RUNG 40-day probe (`hasSrc[n] = h = store.GetDaily(q, 40).Count > 0` at 194) with no coherence requirement across a contract's lookback; RolledValue reads `store.ValueAsOf(ticker(idxThen), then)` at 168-170 with idxThen computed at 158, so one contract's now-level and month-ago-level provably come off different spellings when the window crosses a boundary onto a rung the contributor does not carry. MoverScan.cs:306, 312, 318-320 builds the same contract's series through the same func. The RBA rung arithmetic checks against the shipped calendar (config/meetings.json dates 2026-08-12, 09-30, 11-04, 12-09): 09-Dec is rung 3 today and was rung 4 before the 12-Aug boundary.

THREE THINGS TO TIGHTEN (none fatal, so KEEP rather than KEEP_WITH_FIX — fold them in when you write it).
(1) HARNESS: the strip half is implementable-as-is, not needs-harness-extension. Surfaces.cs:159-161 already calls RollingStrip.ForMeetings with `source: svc.MeetingSrc(sched)`, and Spec.cs's Close/Snap take a `Spell` (Spec.cs:83, 101), so rungs 1-3 seed Both and rungs 4-6 Composite-only. Only the MoverScan half needs wiring.
(2) IT ALSO HITS THE EMAIL BOARD, which you understate by scoping this to the dashboards. FamilyHist walks per-idx (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1634-1640), so the 09-Dec contract's 1m leg reads idx=4, finds no NABZ series and takes the composite — the same cross-feed +6.0 lands on the emailed board. Two surfaces, one cause, and the boards half is assertable through the existing Expect machinery today. That makes the scenario stronger, not weaker.
(3) TWO CITATIONS DO NOT SUPPORT THE PREMISE. meetings.json:203-205 is the BOC comment block (RBA's identical one is 258-260) and it says NAB/BMO quote the MEETING families two-sided and live while not quoting the swap-curve families — nothing about dealer pages quoting only the front of the strip. PricingServiceBoards.cs:575-583 is about snapshotting the composite spelling so Resolve()'s DATE merge can work — also not evidence. Keep the premise, label it an assumption, and confirm on the terminal.

This one is also the batch's cleanest severity case: unlike candidates 3 and 6 the error is NON-UNIFORM, so at the real 1.7bp basis the boards' cross-sectional test (4bp floor, OutlierGuard.cs:56-62) stays quiet and the unguarded dashboards publish it — which is the finding, and worth saying in those words.

### 161. RBNZ front rung: NAB's page carries dates but no price, so the composite's mid prints as the NABZ mid and the cut reads fully priced

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** When the picked contributor stops quoting a rung, does the run refuse the row, or does it silently publish the COMPOSITE's price under the contributor label — and does anything say so?

**Set-up.** RBNZ on its shipped source NABZ (config/meetings.json:317-321), NDSF{N}A, fixing NZOCRS = 2.750. Decision in 2 days, period start in 3 days (RBNZ starts the next day). At the 16:15 London snap Sydney/Auckland is asleep: "NDSF1A NABZ Curncy" publishes SW_EFF_DT and MATURITY but NO bid/ask/last (harness: Quote(1, mid:null, eff, mat, sp:Spell.Active)); the composite "NDSF1A Curncy" is fresh at 2.500. NABZ prices the rest of the strip: NDSF2A 2.400, NDSF3A 2.330, NDSF4A 2.300, and would have shown 2.560 on the front. All ages within an hour of the snapshot baseline.

**What must publish.** The front row either publishes NO number and carries a label (like a rejected print, step chain stepping over it), or publishes 2.500 WITH a note naming NDSF1A and the feed substituted. Assert the label/note plus the note absence today — do not assert 2.560, which the setup does not put in the market.

**How it breaks.** Resolve() fills the price from the composite (PricingServiceBoards.cs:666) and merges it under the contributor's date fields (680-688), so the board publishes 2.500 → Priced −25.0, %25bp −100%, under a feed label the desk chose and that did not price this rung. Nothing marks it: MidSource is plain "ticker" (1079-1080); the no-mid CHECK (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:267-273) only fires when a row publishes NO mid; NZD has no guardFutures key; the front row is exempt from the cross-sectional test (OutlierGuard.cs:47-52). The doc comment at 645-648 states the rule the code breaks. The error size is the basis — 1.7bp measured (1631), not the 6bp assumed here.

**Against the existing suite.** Same mechanism as 34 (contributor price + composite dates) attacked from the OPPOSITE side: 34 proves the merge takes the price from the source page; this asks what happens when the source page has no price. Also adjacent to 37 (published rung >1h quiet) — here the rung is not quiet, it is PRICELESS, which is the case the stale watch cannot see.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** MECHANISM CONFIRMED. PricingServiceBoards.cs:666 is verbatim `priced ??= plain.Mid.HasValue ? plain : null;` and the merge at 680-688 takes Bid/Ask/Last/PrevClose/AgeMinutes from `priced` (the composite) and Maturity/Effective from `dated` (the contributor). The doc comment at 645-648 does state the opposite rule. MidSource is plain "ticker" (1079-1080), and the no-mid CHECK (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:267-273) fires only when a row publishes NO mid, so nothing labels it. Not a duplicate of 34 or 35.

THREE CORRECTIONS.
(1) STRIKE the stale-watch claim — it is the weakest sentence and it is wrong. merged.AgeMinutes = priced.AgeMinutes (line 684) and `priced` IS the composite, i.e. the very feed that produced the published number. The age matches the price. The watch is not blinded; it is correctly quiet. Claiming otherwise will get the scenario thrown out on first read.
(2) STRIKE the 2.560 arithmetic from `expected`. Your own setup gives the NABZ front quote mid:null — there is no 2.560 in the market, so nothing can assert against it, and 'NABZ's own front mark' is not an available outcome. The assertable expectation is the LABEL: withhold the row (MaskLabels.Rejected, the post-F8 'never invent mids' rule the remediation adopted) or publish the composite WITH a note naming the rung and the feed used.
(3) SEVERITY and KIND. The published mid is a real Bloomberg print of the SAME contract on a different feed. The error size IS the contributor-composite basis, and the only measured figure in this repo is 1.7bp (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1631). At 1.7bp the front reads −93% not −100%; 'the cut reads fully priced' is manufactured entirely by your chosen 6bp. Silent substitution may be what the desk wants; that nothing says so is what is certainly wrong.

CITATION: NZD's guard-future rejection is FuturesGuard.cs:21-22, not 19-21. Better authority: the RBNZ block in config/meetings.json has no guardFutures key at all.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:645` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:666` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:684` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1073` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1084` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:47` | `src/RateDesk.Core/FuturesGuard.cs:19`

### 162. Quiet month on the RBA board, every row reading w1 +6.0 and m1 +6.0 because the anchors are composite closes and the mids are NABZ

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | moderate confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** When the store/BDH has no bars under the active contributor spelling, do the 1w/1m columns blank — or do they publish a contributor-minus-composite difference, i.e. two dealers' marks subtracted from each other?

**Set-up.** Same, but delete the aud.json:319 citation (it is the swap-curve note at line 320, and meetings.json:258-260 contradicts it for the meeting family). State the precondition as an ASSUMPTION requiring one live check: does this terminal serve BDH bars under 'ADSF1A NABZ'? DailyBuilder.cs:265-268 + StoreBackedHistory.cs:53-54 backfill 70 days on first read, so if it does, the scenario cannot arise.

**What must publish.** w1 = m1 = 0.0 (or blank), Priced −25.0. State the point of the scenario explicitly: the assertion that matters is that a UNIFORM anchor bias is invisible to every defence, so if it publishes, nothing will ever catch it.

**How it breaks.** FamilyHist falls back per rung with no label (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1636-1640) and ChangeToBp differences a NABZ mid against a composite snap (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:528-537), giving a standing offset in w1 and m1 on every row, every day. Magnitude = the contributor-composite basis, measured at 1.7bp and episodic (1631) — not the 6bp assumed here. No defence can see it: the cross-sectional test is median-then-MAD so a uniform bias has zero residual (OutlierGuard.cs:52-62), the absolute bars are 12/30/50bp (:23), and FuturesGuard blends MIDS only (FuturesGuard.cs:63-77). The comment at 1630-1633 names this poison and the fallback re-creates it.

**Against the existing suite.** 34 proves the LIVE merge; this attacks the HISTORY side of the same source split, which no scenario touches. 24 covers a staleness cap with a feed hole; here nothing is stale — the series is complete, just a different dealer's.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** CODE CONFIRMED. PricingServiceBoards.cs:1636-1640 is verbatim the unlabelled per-rung fallback, and the comment at 1630-1633 does name the exact poison the fallback re-creates. ChangeToBp (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:528-537) will difference a contributor mid against a composite snap. The defences are genuinely blind to a UNIFORM bias: OutlierGuard.cs:52-62 takes median then MAD (median +6.0, MAD 0, residual 0), the absolute bars at :23 are 12/30/50, and FuturesGuard.cs:63-77 blends MIDS only — it never sees a change column. That blind-to-uniform property is this scenario's real contribution and should be its stated point.

FOUR CORRECTIONS.
(1) THE PRECONDITION CITE IS WRONG AND POINTS THE OTHER WAY. config/currencies/aud.json line 320 (not 319) is the AUD SWAP-CURVE note; meetings.json:258-260 says the opposite about the family this scenario is about — 'NAB/BMO quote these MEETING families two-sided and live (verified 2026-08-06)'. Worse, DailyBuilder.cs:265-268 pulls 70 days of contributor BDH every run and StoreBackedHistory.cs:53-54 backfills the FULL lookback for a ticker with no store rows, so on any terminal that serves contributor BDH at all the fallback never fires. Mark the whole scenario CONDITIONAL on the one live check and keep guardrail 4.
(2) KIND. The fallback is deliberate and commented. The desk question is 'prefer a complete composite series over an EMPTY contributor one — yes; over a HOLED one — ?'. That is a desk call, not a defect the code got wrong by accident.
(3) SEVERITY. The magnitude is the basis, measured at 1.7bp, and the repo says it is EPISODIC ('prints off NABZ episodically'), so the live symptom is intermittent 1-2bp noise in Δ1w/Δ1m, not a standing +6.0.
(4) THE MOVERSCAN CLAIM IS OVERREACH. At the real basis there is no hero card, and Heroes() caps one per (ccy, kind) — at most ONE AUD meeting hero, never 'the DM hero cards'.

OVERLAP: this is the degenerate all-rungs case of candidate 7, which is the stronger scenario. Keep it only for the blind-to-uniform property; if the batch must lose one, lose this and keep 7.

### 163. The desk picks a dealer that does not price the family at all, the status bar confirms the switch, and every number is the composite's

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | critic-authored`

**The question.** When a picked contributor prices NO rung of a family, does anything tell the desk the pick was inert — or does the board publish an unchanged composite table under a feed label nobody can see?

**Set-up.** RIKSBANK, SKSF{N}A, quiet week, no decision. The desk picks a contributor from the SOURCES list that resolves as a mnemonic but quotes no SKSF rung — the dialog's own probe is best-effort and the pick is saved regardless (MainWindow.xaml.cs:479-490). sources.json = {"RIKSBANK":"<mnemonic>"}. Snapshot: NO source-qualified SKSF quotes at all; composite SKSF1A..4A quote 2.000/1.900/1.850/1.800 with dates; store holds composite closes and snaps flat for 45 days. Run twice — once with the override, once without — and diff.

**What must publish.** The desk is told the pick did nothing. Either the run refuses the source and says so in a note that gates (the SOURCES pick decides which feed a traded number comes from — SourceStore.cs:34-36 calls a torn write of this file a data hazard for exactly that reason), or it raises a CHECK naming the family and the rungs the contributor did not price.

**How it breaks.** Resolve() falls through to the plain spelling at every rung (PricingServiceBoards.cs:662-670) and the merge is skipped entirely because `dated` and `priced` are the same composite object (676-678), so the two runs are CELL-IDENTICAL on every surface. Nothing anywhere says the pick was inert: SourceStore.cs:73-77 logs 'RIKSBANK priced from <mnemonic> (override)' — asserting the opposite of what happened; the completeness gate does not fire because rows exist (CompoundedFixing.cs:99-108); MidSource is plain 'ticker' (1079-1080); no renderer prints run.Source at all. The desk now believes, and can tell a client, that the SEK board is on a named dealer's page when every number on it is the Bloomberg composite — and the next operator, seeing the feed 'already switched', will not switch it. The failure is an identity assertion, so it needs no invented basis and no arithmetic to argue about.

**Against the existing suite.** The whole-family complement of candidate 2, which is the one-rung case: 2 must invent a contributor-composite basis to make its wrong number visible, this one asserts an IDENTITY (two runs, byte-identical output, one log line claiming otherwise) and so cannot be argued down on magnitude. Needs MeetingSourceOverrides driveable from the harness.

**Critic (CRITIC-AUTHORED).** proposed by the domain critic

*SQLite history, maturity records, watermarks and the irreplaceable rows.*

### 164. Daily run from a machine east of London: every Δ1d on every bank prints 0.0 on a decision day

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** The app pins its published marks to the 16:15 LONDON snap but keys every store read, store write and change-column anchor to DateTime.Today, the MACHINE's local date. What does the desk read when those two dates disagree?

**Set-up.** Same setup, plus: seed a Fri 4-Sep-26 16:15 snap of 1.985 on the 4-Nov-26 row as well as the 3-Sep 1.975. Without a 4-Sep point the Δ1w arm produces no visible error (see fixed_expected). Reachability: this fires only on a machine whose LOCAL date is ahead of London's at press time — Asia/Middle-East coverage pressing the London 16:15/17:00 run, or the UTC+3 dev box after 22:00 London. It never fires on the London desk box, so the desk-frequency argument should be stated honestly rather than as a daily hazard.

**What must publish.** Δ1d on the 4-Nov-26 row = (1.735 − 1.980) × 100 = −24.5 bp. Δ1w must anchor Thu 3-Sep = (1.735 − 1.975) × 100 = −24.0 bp; with the 4-Sep 1.985 seeded, the London-correct target (10-Sep − 7 = 3-Sep) still gives −24.0 while the machine-date target (11-Sep − 7 = 4-Sep) gives (1.735 − 1.985) × 100 = −25.0 — a 6-day change under a Δ1w label. Store expectations unchanged: no close booked for 10-Sep, and the 10-Sep SW_EFF_DT stamped under 10-Sep.

**How it breaks.** Δ1d prints 0.0 on every PINNED row of every bank on the day the ECB cut 25bp — not literally every row: SnapDiscipline.cs:70-76 leaves barless tickers on the live mid (locked by SaveDownTests.cs:110 `Snap1615Mode_PinsQuotedTickersToTheSnap_LeavesBarlessOnesLive`), and those print a small non-zero, which makes the picture worse rather than better because the zeros and the non-zeros sit side by side. Δ1w prints −25.0 where the label promises a 7-day change measured to 3-Sep. Remainder of the break_mode stands as written (HistoryStore.cs:387 admits 10-Sep's unsettled BDH value via StoreBackedHistory.cs:59; DailyBuilder.cs:279 stamps SW_EFF_DT under 11-Sep so EffectiveOn(EESF*, 10-Sep) is NULL and MeetingRungMap.cs:120-123 is blind on the decision day). Cite DailyBuilder.cs:233 for the Prefetch skip, not :232.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 58 (16:15 close vs the 19:00 FOMC statement) and 51-53 (dashboard strips on a decision day), which attack the LONDON clock against the announcement. This attacks the MACHINE clock against the London calendar — the harness README names the hazard for decision gating only and never follows it into the store or the change columns.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Mechanism confirmed end to end. PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286 is exactly `wm.D1Bp = ChangeToBp(s, row.MidPct, DateTime.Today.AddDays(-1), 5)`; the merge that puts today's 16:15 bar into the series is PricingServiceBoards.cs:1652-1656 (`snaps.AddRange(GetLondonSnaps(tkr, snapDays, snapAtNew).Where(sp => sp.Date.Date >= snapCutover))` then `merged[sp.Date] = sp.Value`), fed by RefDataClient.cs:464 `var endUtc = DateTime.UtcNow`; the boundary-day rule admits it because it is snapped (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1706 `p.Date == hi && snapped.Contains(p.Date)`); and SnapDiscipline.cs:66 pins the published mid to the same bar. Δ1d is therefore exactly 0.0 and the 5-day cap at PricingServiceWeekly.cs:534 passes. SnapDiscipline.cs:61-64 does carry the comment 'a machine east of London is already on tomorrow's LOCAL date at 16:15 London, which silently unpinned every mark (audit 2026-08-26)' — the author fixed this class in the pinning path only. HistoryStore.cs:387 and DailyBuilder.cs:279 both key on DateTime.Today as claimed. Two corrections and a reachability caveat below.

### 165. EXPORT XLS during an outage overwrites the dated books on the share, destroying the desk's own outage marks

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** EXPORT XLS rebuilds the save-down books from the LAST STORED report and names them after that report's AsOf. What happens to the genuine book of that date already sitting on the share, and to the manual rows inside it?

**Set-up.** Bloomberg API down Thu 27-Aug-26 through Mon 31-Aug-26. Because no DAILY RUN succeeds, the app produces NO 27-Aug or 28-Aug book, so the newest dated book on the share is OIS_Runs_26August26.xlsm — that is the one the desk opens and stores into via the incumbent macro on Fri 28-Aug: (CurrentDate 28-Aug, StartDate 16-Sep-26, Rate 2.048) into Historical_EU. Store holds the bbg close 2.008 for the 16-Sep-26 contract on 26-Aug and nothing after. On Mon 31-Aug the desk presses EXPORT XLS. out\daily_report.json is the 26-Aug report, rep.AsOf = 26-Aug 16:20.

**What must publish.** The 28-Aug manual mark must survive in OIS_Runs_26August26.xlsm and reach the store as source='xls' on the next successful run (IngestNewestSaved's minDate for that book is 26-Aug, DailyBuilder.cs:407-409, so a 28-Aug row qualifies). On Fri 4-Sep with the strip at 1.955, Δ1w's target is 28-Aug and the anchor is the 28-Aug mark: (1.955 − 2.048) × 100 = −9.3 bp. No rebuild may write over a dated book on the share it has not first read.

**How it breaks.** ExportBook writes out\OIS_Runs_26August26.xlsm from rep.AsOf (StoreBooks.cs:43, ExtractTemplate File.Create at :348) and SaveDown.Sync copies it over the share's copy (SaveDown.cs:118-120) — the desk's 28-Aug row in that book is gone, and it was never read: ExportBook DOES run FallbackIngest, but only over the appdata `fallbackBook` (DailyBuilder.cs:111-112), never IngestNewestSaved over the share's dated books. Drop the 'repeat the click and the 28-Aug book goes too' sentence: ExportBook only ever writes files named for rep.AsOf, and a desk-created 28-Aug book was never in out\ so Sync cannot touch it. The published symptom is a WRONG NUMBER, not a blank: on 4-Sep the last usable close is 26-Aug and ChangeToBp measures (target − anchor) = 28-Aug − 26-Aug = 2 days, well inside the 7-day W1 cap (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287, :534), so the ECB front row publishes Δ1w = (1.955 − 2.008) × 100 = −5.3 bp against a truth of −9.3 — a 4.0bp error on the blast, the workbook, both email bodies and the strip, with Historical_EU showing a hole across 27-Aug…31-Aug. Also narrow the app-derived-promotion arm: StoreBooks.cs:298-323 is the INFLATION book's {fam}_History tabs; the OIS book's app-written Historical_* rows are excluded from re-ingest by FallbackIngest.cs:108's minDate test, and the inflation path is the one that DOES log an override (InflHistory.cs:163-166).

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 57 (save-down history tables across a decision), which checks the CONTENT of the Historical_* tables. This attacks the FILE lifecycle around them — naming, mirroring, re-ingest — which no existing scenario touches.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The file-lifecycle defect is real and cleanly cited: StoreBooks.cs:43/:274 name both books off rep.AsOf, ExtractTemplate at StoreBooks.cs:341-351 does `using var f = File.Create(destPath)` so the local file is unconditionally replaced from the embedded template, and SaveDown.cs:118-120 then copies over the share copy because the local mtime is newer. ExportBook genuinely never runs IngestNewestSaved (only Render does, DailyBuilder.cs:407-410), and FallbackIngest's notes are discarded at DailyBuilder.cs:371/:112 (the Result is dropped, notes go only to `log`), so the 'never reaches rep.Notes → never reaches ConfirmChecks (MainWindow.xaml.cs:788)' claim is right. Three things are wrong: the ingest claim is overstated, the second-click claim cannot happen, and the Δ1w arithmetic is wrong in a way that changes the finding.

### 166. New machine: the restore is never offered, and two runs delete both generations of the only irreplaceable snapshot

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** A desk laptop is replaced. Every path by which the operator can reach WEEKLY/DAILY RUN before accepting a restore ends with the share's snapshot being overwritten by a fresh 45-day store. Does anything stop it?

**Set-up.** Same, with the Path B claim narrowed: AskSaveDownChoice's 'Locate C+C' branch skips OfferStoreRestore in THAT session (MainWindow.xaml.cs:253). The next launch does reach it via SetupSaveDown's config branch (:181) — but by then the operator has run something and DbPath exists, so :197 exits. The correct claim is 'never offered before a store exists', not 'never offered'.

**What must publish.** Either path must offer the restore before any store is created, and no snapshot may be replaced by one that holds fewer source='xls' rows or a shorter maturity span. Post-run the share must still hold 612 xls rows and maturity back to 26-Aug-26.

**How it breaks.** Same as written for Path A. For Path B, replace 'AskSaveDownChoice never calls OfferStoreRestore at all' with 'AskSaveDownChoice never calls OfferStoreRestore, so the offer is deferred to the next launch (:181) — by which time :197 has a store to find and exits permanently'. The load-bearing defect is the combination at the end, and it should be the headline: StoreBackup.AfterRun rotates unconditionally (:40-45) so two runs consume both generations; FindBackup (:57) cannot see a surviving _prev; Restore (:67) refuses to merge into an existing store. 612 xls rows, 3 adopted CPI prints (InflHistory.cs:130-132) and ten months of SW_EFF_DT are then unrecoverable, with every surface looking normal on 45 days of composite closes.

**Against the existing suite.** none — tests/RateDesk.Tests/CrossSurfaceTests.cs:176 asserts the snapshot round-trips and that Restore refuses an existing store, which is exactly the guarantee that makes this unrecoverable; nothing tests the rotation's willingness to overwrite a richer snapshot with a poorer one.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Both paths verified. Path A: MainWindow.xaml.cs:542 `using var store = new HistoryStore(DbPath)` creates the file (HistoryStore.cs:21-27 Directory.CreateDirectory + Open), and OfferStoreRestore's guard is MainWindow.xaml.cs:197 `if (File.Exists(DbPath)) return`; UpdateBtn at MainWindow.xaml:33-35 carries no IsEnabled="False" while every other button does (:42-50), and nothing gates it on SetupSaveDown, which is fired from `Loaded` at :129. Path B confirmed: AskSaveDownChoice's locate branch returns at :253 and the Save-Locally tail at :259-268 both end without calling OfferStoreRestore. The terminal part is confirmed too: StoreBackup.cs:40-45 rotates with no comparison of any kind, :46-47 logs MB only, FindBackup at :57 reads only LatestName so a good _prev is unreachable, and Restore at :67 refuses any machine that has a store — which CrossSurfaceTests.cs:199 locks as a feature. One overstatement to fix.

### 167. One derivation, three call sites: a manual outage mark is filed on rung 2 while every reader reads rung 1

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** MeetingRungMap's docstring says it is THE one derivation for all five consumers. The boards construct it WITH the store's SW_EFF_DT evidence, DailyBook WITHOUT it, and FallbackIngest without evidence AND without ticker-derived dates. On a day the store has records, do the three agree?

**Set-up.** Retitle to 'one derivation, six constructions, one with evidence' and run two arms. ARM A (mixed state, P1): ECB — announcement Thu 20-Aug-26, period start Wed 26-Aug-26, decisionDates carrying 20-Aug. The API is down 24-Aug, the desk hand-stores (CurrentDate 24-Aug, StartDate 4-Nov-26, Rate 1.980) into Historical_EU of the newest dated book. 24-Aug is NOT a boundary (so FallbackIngest.cs:118 lets it through) but IS mixed-state by MeetingRungMap.cs:84-92, and FallbackIngest checks IsBoundary only — it never asks IsMixedState. ARM B (ticker-date drift, P0): use a family whose config period start is drifted from the ticker's own SW_EFF_DT — the case MeetingRungMap.cs:11-13 records ('BOJ's 2027 entries sat 8-11 days late'). Config start 20-Jan-27, ticker-derived start 29-Jan-27; the manual row is dated 23-Jan (between the two). FallbackIngest's map has the boundary at 20-Jan and counts it as already passed; the boards' map (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1551, tickerDates unioned in) has it at 29-Jan and does not. Neighbouring rungs 10.0bp apart.

**What must publish.** ARM A: the 24-Aug mark must either be refused with a note (the same treatment the boundary day gets at FallbackIngest.cs:120-122) or filed under a rung every reader will actually use. ARM B: the 23-Jan mark must land under the rung the ticker's own SW_EFF_DT says carried the 4-Nov-27 contract that day, and the next day's Δ1d on that row must be the same contract's own move, ≈ −0.6 bp.

**How it breaks.** ARM A: the mark is written under a boundary-counted rung, and then no reader will touch it — MoverScan.cs:186 and RollingStrip.cs:168 drop mixed-state days outright, DailyBook.cs:169 walks past them, and PricingServiceBoards.cs:1705 admits one only when the store has a maturity record for that day (which an outage day does not). The failsafe's whole purpose — 'history is continuous across an outage and every change column computes normally' (FallbackIngest.cs:8-11) — is silently not delivered, and the rung's raw series now carries the neighbouring contract's level on that date for anything that reads it raw. ARM B: writer and reader differ by one boundary, so the mark lands on rung N+1 and the next day's Δ1d on the 4-Nov-27 row publishes ≈ +10.0 bp against a truth near 0.0, on all five surfaces, internally consistent and invisible to every cross-surface check. Keep the DailyBook.cs:133 observation (tickerDates but no `recorded`) as the third answer.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 28-33 (Riksbank period-start renumbering) and 35 (re-base fallbacks), which exercise the READER's rung mapping. This attacks the WRITER — the ingest that decides which ticker a manual mark is filed under — from the opposite side of the same mechanism. Belongs in tests/RateDesk.Tests alongside DailyTests.cs:111.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The mechanism is real and BIGGER than claimed, but the concrete setup does not break — I worked the arithmetic. SKSF's boundaries are the period starts only (config/meetings.json:481-494: 2026-08-26, 2026-09-30, 2026-11-11…). FallbackIngest.cs:74 builds `new MeetingRungMap(sched)` and RungFor(30-Sep-26, 27-Aug-26) reaches MeetingRungMap.cs:165-168 with 27-Aug not in _set, counting b > 27-Aug && b <= 30-Sep = {30-Sep} = 1 — the CORRECT rung. The mark is filed under SKSF1A and every reader agrees. CrossSurfaceTests.cs:170 pins the adjacent case (`Assert.Equal(2, sekMap.RungFor(new(2026,9,30), new(2026,8,24)))`). The −10.6-vs-−0.6 case in MeetingRungMap.cs:114-119 is onDay = 26-Aug, the BOUNDARY day — and FallbackIngest.cs:118-124 explicitly refuses a manual row dated on a boundary, with a note. So as written this is a false alarm. Two arms DO break, and the enumeration is understated: there are six constructions of MeetingRungMap in src/ and exactly one passes `recorded` (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1551); PricingServiceWeekly.cs:257, DailyBook.cs:133, FallbackIngest.cs:74, MoverScan.cs:310 and RollingStrip.cs:223 all go without.

### 168. Two desk machines, one C+C share: the second run of the afternoon leaves both snapshot generations poor

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | critic-authored`

**The question.** StoreBackup rotates latest→prev on every successful run with no comparison and no origin stamp. When two machines with very different stores back up to the same share within ten minutes, what does a third machine inherit?

**Set-up.** Two operators, one C+C root. Machine A holds the desk's real store: 41,200 daily rows of which 612 are source='xls' (incumbent marks back to Nov-25), 3 adopted CPI index prints (InflHistory.cs:130-132) and maturity records from 26-Aug-26. Machine B was rebuilt yesterday, declined the restore, and holds a fresh 45-day seed: ~44,000 rows, 0 xls rows, maturity from yesterday only. Thu 3-Sep-26: B presses DAILY RUN at 16:40, A presses DAILY RUN at 16:52. Fri 4-Sep: a third laptop is provisioned and offered the restore.

**What must publish.** The share must not replace a snapshot holding 612 xls rows and ten months of SW_EFF_DT with one holding none, or at minimum must stamp each snapshot with origin machine, row count and xls-row count so the restoring operator can choose. After both runs the share must still be able to hand a new machine the 612 xls rows.

**How it breaks.** StoreBackup.cs:40-45 rotates blind: B's fresh store becomes history_backup.db and A's rich snapshot moves to _prev; A's run ten minutes later moves B's fresh copy to _prev (deleting A's rich one at :42) and writes A's store as latest. Reverse the click order — A at 16:40, B at 16:52 — and both generations are B's fresh store within twelve minutes, with the log at :46-47 reporting only '(45.1 MB, previous rotated)' both times. Friday's laptop then reads FindBackup (:57, LatestName only) and is shown the dialog at MainWindow.xaml.cs:202-208, which tells the operator they are inheriting 'the full history — incumbent-sheet marks, manual entries and maturity records included'. That sentence is now false. Desk-visible: Historical_EU/UK/SEK print 'bbg' in the source column on every row where the incumbent's Nov-25 marks read 'xls' (DailyBook.cs:146-149 GetDailyWithSource), and every |z| on the DM and EM movers tables carries the 'est' marker because no ticker on the new machine has 400 days (MoverScan.cs:121, :375). Restore then refuses to merge (StoreBackup.cs:67) so there is no way back.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to the batch's fresh-machine-never-offered-restore, which needs a mutex bug or a mis-sequenced first run to consume both generations on ONE machine. This needs neither: two ordinary Windows 11 boxes running their ordinary daily runs do it. Belongs in tests/RateDesk.Tests beside CrossSurfaceTests.cs:176 — two HistoryStores, one temp share dir, assert the rotation cannot demote.

**Critic (CRITIC-AUTHORED).** proposed by the domain critic

### 169. The rung identity is recorded at 17:00 while the marks are the 16:15 bar, and that 45-minute-newer record outranks everything

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | critic-authored`

**The question.** MeetingRungMap treats a recorded SW_EFF_DT as evidence that beats the boundary count and even the boundary-day step-back. The daily run reads that field from the press-time snapshot but publishes 16:15 marks. On a decision day, which numbering does the record describe?

**Set-up.** ECB cuts 25bp Thu 10-Sep-26, announced 13:15 London, period starting 16-Sep-26. The run is pressed at 17:05 London. At 16:15 the EESF composite had NOT yet re-pointed (rung 1 still the run-down to 16-Sep, eff 05-Aug); by 17:00 it has (rung 1 eff 16-Sep). SnapDiscipline resolves Snap1615 and pins every published mid to the 16:15 bar. Contemporaneous levels 10bp apart: EESF1A 16:15 = 1.980, EESF2A 16:15 = 1.735. Fri 11-Sep the run is pressed at 16:40 with the 4-Nov-26 row at 1.729.

**What must publish.** The store's 10-Sep record must describe the numbering the published 10-Sep marks belong to — i.e. it must either be stamped at the snap the marks came from, or be refused as evidence for a boundary day exactly as DailyBook.cs:160-168 already refuses it. Friday's Δ1d on the 4-Nov-26 row is then the same contract's own move: (1.729 − 1.735) × 100 = −0.6 bp.

**How it breaks.** DailyBuilder.cs:277-279 stamps `store.SetMaturity(tk, DateTime.Today, mm, q0.Effective)` from the snapshot taken at press time (DailyBuilder.cs:224 runs SnapDiscipline AFTER the snapshot, and SnapDiscipline's `snap.Update(t, sv, sv, sv)` writes bid/ask/last only — RatesSnapshot.cs:51-63 never touches Effective or Maturity). So the 10-Sep record says 'rung 1 = the 16-Sep period', which is the 17:00 truth, while the published 10-Sep close is the 16:15 pre-re-point number. Friday's board then builds `recorded` (PricingServiceBoards.cs:1541-1551) and MeetingRungMap.cs:120-123 returns that rung FIRST — ahead of the offset calibration, ahead of the boundary-day step-back, and the comment at :109-112 says so explicitly. PricingServiceBoards.cs:1705 also uses HasRecordFor to LIFT the mixed-state exclusion for 10-Sep. The 4-Nov-26 row's Δ1d therefore anchors the neighbouring contract and publishes (1.729 − 1.980) × 100 = −25.1 bp against a truth of −0.6 — a whole inter-meeting step on the morning after a cut, on all five surfaces, internally consistent. What makes this a defect rather than a judgement call is that the repo already ruled: DailyBook.cs:160-168 records the same lift as TRIED AND REVERTED on 2026-08-28 — 'a record is stamped when the RUN looked, and a boundary day's feed re-points intraday, so it does not settle which contract that day's CLOSE belonged to. Scenario 57 derives the same conclusion independently and rejected the change on twelve rows. Do not re-add without desk sign-off.' The books obey it; the boards never did.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 58 (16:15 close vs the 19:00 FOMC statement) and 23 (renumber day, mid(N) vs PrevClose(N+1)), which attack the PRICE side of the snap/announcement gap. This attacks the DATE FIELD side — the store's rung-identity record — and it is the one axis where a 45-minute discrepancy is promoted to evidence that overrides every other derivation. No existing scenario writes a maturity record at a different clock from the marks.

**Critic (CRITIC-AUTHORED).** proposed by the domain critic

## Save-down and the share

*The xlsm archive-of-record, the C+C mirror, and Excel.*

### 170. SOURCES override on RBA: the save-down book's Blast/Vandit pages quote the stale NABZ feed the desk just abandoned

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** Does the SOURCES selection carry to every page of the macro-enabled save-down book, or only to the Runs sheet and the report?

**Set-up.** Same market, but rung1 = 09-Sep-26 -> 01-Oct-26 (a future RBA period, so it is published row 0) and rung2 = 01-Oct-26 -> 05-Nov-26 (published row 1). No RBA decision inside the window. ADSF1A composite 3.585 / NABZ 3.590; ADSF2A composite 3.355 / NABZ 3.410, NABZ's ADSF2A last ticked 09:12 London. Composite 28-Aug close for the 01-Oct contract 3.335. RBACOR 3.600. SOURCES: RBA -> composite (an OVERRIDE, i.e. svc.MeetingSourceOverrides["RBA"] = "", NOT a config edit - the harness must gain an override hook, since BankSpec.Source writes config source at ConfigWriter.cs:53 and would hide the bug).

**What must publish.** Every surface, INCLUDING both live pages of OIS_Runs_31August26.xlsm, must be on the composite. Runs sheet / blast / sheet email row 2: Mid 3.355, Priced = (3.355-3.600)*100 = -24.5, Step = -24.5 - (3.585-3.600)*100 = -24.5-(-1.5) = -23.0, d1 = (3.355-3.335)*100 = +2.0. The Blast and Vandit pages must build their BDP tickers as 'ADSF2A Curncy' (active source = composite), so a recalculation reproduces -24.5 / +2.0.

**How it breaks.** StoreBooks.cs:160 builds the live-page ticker from sched.Source (config default NABZ), not run.Source / svc.MeetingSrc. So Blast!C reads _xll.BDP("ADSF2A NABZ Curncy","LAST_PRICE") = 3.410, Priced (StoreBooks.cs:219) = (3.410-3.600)*100 = -19.0, Step = (3.410-3.590)*100 = -18.0, and d1 (StoreBooks.cs:242-249, anchor literal 3.335000 taken from the COMPOSITE run) = (3.410-3.335)*100 = +7.5. One workbook, two pages, 5.5bp apart on Priced and 5.5bp on d1 - and the page the desk pastes from is quoting the exact stale feed the STALE note told them to leave. Whichever page they use, one of them is not what went out on the blast.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 34 (RBA contributor vs composite) and 37 (published rung >1h quiet) - both attack the BOARDS' source handling. New angle: the SOURCES selection reaching the SAVE-DOWN BOOK's own pages. No existing scenario builds StoreBooks at all.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Mechanism CONFIRMED at source. SourceStore.Apply writes the desk's pick into svc.MeetingSourceOverrides (SourceStore.cs:73), never into sched.Source; the active source is read through PricingService.MeetingSrc (PricingServiceBoards.cs:275-276) and the history writer follows it (DailyBook.cs:128 `run.Source ?? sched.Source ?? ""`). StoreBooks.cs:160 reads sched.Source alone, so the Blast/Vandit ticker suffix is the CONFIG DEFAULT while the Runs sheet and history_au on the same page of the same file are on the override. STALE note text verified verbatim at PricingServiceBoards.cs:1130-1134. Also genuinely new: the harness's own BankSpec.Source writes the config `source` field (ConfigWriter.cs:53), so no existing scenario can even express an override — 34 sets the default, which is why the divergence has never been reachable. FIX REQUIRED: the setup is internally inconsistent. Rung1 is given a start of 13-Aug (18 days in the past), which makes it the run-down quotes[0] — not a published row — yet the expected Step (-24.5 - (3.585-3.600)*100) requires ADSF1A to BE published row 0. Push rung1's start into the future so the RBA publishes two rows with no decision in the window; then the arithmetic (-24.5 / -23.0 / +2.0 correct, -19.0 / -18.0 / +7.5 broken) holds exactly as written. Also drop the STALE note from the load-bearing chain: it motivates the operator's action but nothing in the break depends on it, and requiring an age-baseline seed makes the scenario fragile for no gain.

### 171. Decision day: the save-down book's live pages read one rung nearer than the board publishes

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | critic-authored`

**The question.** WriteLivePage maps published row i to rung i+1. On a decision day the announced-gate shift has already dropped the decided period out of the published list. Does the live page's ticker still name the contract its row shows?

**Set-up.** Reuse scenario 34's market verbatim (tests/RateDesk.Scenarios/Catalogue/Group07_SourcesAndGaps.cs:113-163): RBA cuts TODAY, statement out, ADSF{N}A on NABZ, RBACOR 3.850 (still the pre-cut cash rate). Source-page mids ADSF1A 3.600 (the just-decided D(+1)->D(+43) period), ADSF2A 3.500, ADSF3A 3.400, ADSF4A 3.350; the composite prints 8bp above on every rung and carries the SW_EFF_DT/MATURITY fields the source page lacks. Every contract stepped +4bp on the decision, flat at mid-0.040 at D(-1)/D(-7)/D(-31). Then call StoreBooks.WriteOis and read the Blast and Vandit pages. Nothing extra faked.

**What must publish.** The live page's first RBA data row must show the contract the attachment's first row shows: Start D(+43), Mid 3.500, Priced -10.0, Step blank, Delta-1d +4.0 (the hand derivation is already in the repo at Group07_SourcesAndGaps.cs:177-191, and scenario 34 proves the board gets it right).

**How it breaks.** StoreBooks.cs:190 hard-codes `Tick(i + 1)` - 'row i is the i-th rung, as Current does' - which is the PRE-shift numbering. Published row 0 is rung 2 on an announcement day, so row 0 is given _xll.BDP("ADSF1A NABZ Curncy","LAST_PRICE") = 3.600, the just-decided period. Column A is `IFERROR(BDP(...,"SW_EFF_DT"),DATE(D+43))` (StoreBooks.cs:199-200), and on this market the composite carries the field, so the row RELABELS itself to D(+1) and its own label agrees with the wrong contract - nothing on the page contradicts it. Priced (StoreBooks.cs:219) = (3.600 - $B$fixRow)*100 = (3.600-3.850)*100 = -25.0 against a published -10.0. Delta-1d (247-249) uses the literal anchor 3.500 - 4.0/100 = 3.460, so the cell reads (3.600-3.460)*100 = +14.0 against a published +4.0. Every row in the block shifts one full inter-meeting step (10bp on this strip), and the deepest row's ticker names a rung the run never published. The app's own history writer is immune - BankHistoryRows resolves the rung from the contract's start date (DailyBook.cs:133, 171), not the row index - so the same workbook is right on Historical_AU and wrong on Blast.

**Against the existing suite.** Reuses scenario 34's market, which asserts the BOARD and passes; this asserts the workbook's own live pages, which nothing in the 64-scenario catalogue opens. Sibling of candidate 1 (same function): candidate 1 is the wrong SOURCE spelling, this is the wrong RUNG on the right spelling, and unlike candidate 1 this one fires with no SOURCES override at all, on every bank, every decision day.

**Critic (CRITIC-AUTHORED).** proposed by the domain critic

### 172. Live pages compute Priced off the raw o/n fixing, never the run's re-based one

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | critic-authored`

**The question.** On the days the board re-bases Priced onto the just-decided period, does the save-down book's own live page use the same base - and does it carry the dagger?

**Set-up.** Same scenario-34 market (RBA cuts today, statement out; source-page mids 3.600/3.500/3.400/3.350, RBACOR 3.850). The board re-bases: run.RefPct = 3.600 with RefRebased = true (PricingServiceBoards.cs:975-977), and the attachment prints '(rebased)' beside it. RBACOR itself still prints 3.850 all day - RBA's cash-rate fixing is published in arrears. Call WriteOis, read Blast/Vandit. Alternatively run the ECB arm of scenario 16/17 for the six-day lagged window instead of one day.

**What must publish.** The live page's Priced column must reproduce the attachment's: row 0 -10.0, row 1 -20.0 (Step -10.0), row 2 -25.0 (Step -5.0) (Group07_SourcesAndGaps.cs:182-184), and the page must carry the same re-based marker the attachment and blast carry ('(rebased)').

**How it breaks.** StoreBooks.cs:174 writes the fixing cell as a bare `_xll.BDP("RBACOR Index","LAST_PRICE")` and never looks at b.FixingPct or b.Rebased, which RunsTable.Build fills from run.RefPct / run.RefRebased (RunsTable.cs:62, 86) and which PricingServiceWeekly.cs:81-85 documents in as many words: 'RefPct is not the printed fixing: it was re-based onto the just-decided...'. So $B$fixRow = 3.850 and Priced (StoreBooks.cs:219) reads row 0 (3.500-3.850)*100 = -35.0 against -10.0, row 1 -45.0 against -20.0, row 2 -50.0 against -25.0: the whole column 25bp low, the size of the move, with no dagger and no '(rebased)' anywhere on the page. Reaches all eight lagged families for their entire announcement-to-start window (ECB/BOJ/SNB/Riksbank ~six days a meeting, RBA/RBNZ/BOC/Norges one), plus any run on a MeetingRefOverrides manual base, which also sets RefRebased (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:223). Independent of the rung fault above: fix one and the page is still wrong by the other.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 54, 55 and 61 (what Priced is measured against) and to closed finding 1 - all three test the BOARD's base. Nothing tests whether the workbook's own live page shares it. In the scenario-34 market this compounds with the rung fault above to give row 0 a Priced of -25.0 against a published -10.0; keep them separate because the two fixes are in different lines.

### 173. Last Tuesday's saved book reopened today reads Riksbank front d1 as -25.0bp; the true one-day move is -0.5

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** When a dated save-down book is reopened after the family has renumbered, do its change columns still describe the contract their row is labelled with?

**Set-up.** Add the missing marks: 28-Aug-26 closes SKSF1A (the 30-Sep contract) 1.7150 and SKSF2A (the 11-Nov contract) 1.6390 - these are the anchors the honest d1 is measured from. Keep 25-Aug row1 mid 1.9500 / d1 -1.0 and row2 mid 1.7237 / d1 -0.9 as the frozen literals.

**What must publish.** On reopen 31-Aug with live SKSF1A 1.7100 / SKSF2A 1.6350, the honest one-day changes for the contracts those tickers now carry are (1.7100-1.7150)*100 = -0.5 and (1.6350-1.6390)*100 = -0.4. A dated book must either show its own 25-Aug numbers verbatim or pin each row to the contract it names; it must not pair a live price with a 25-Aug anchor under a '1d Chg' header.

**How it breaks.** StoreBooks.cs:190 fixes the ticker to rung i+1 for ever, StoreBooks.cs:199-205 lets BDP SW_EFF_DT win so the row silently RELABELS itself 30-Sep-26, and StoreBooks.cs:242-249 froze the anchor as a literal (Mid - d/100 = 1.960000). On reopen the d1 cell computes (1.7100 - 1.960000)*100 = -25.0 and row2 gives (1.6350 - 1.7327)*100 = -9.8. The desk reads the Riksbank front rung down a full policy step in a day, in a workbook whose A1 says 'DRAX OIS Runs 25Aug26', while the Runs sheet of the SAME file (static) still shows the honest 25-Aug numbers. Note the docstring at StoreBooks.cs:132-141 still claims the change columns 'look up the Historical_ sheet on the contract's own START DATE, never on the ticker number... that is what makes them roll-proof' - the two failed attempts recorded at 227-241 removed exactly that property and the comment was left standing.

**Against the existing suite.** Same file as scenario 1 above (both attack WriteLivePage) but a different arm: 1 is the wrong TICKER FAMILY, this is the right ticker carrying a different CONTRACT. Adjacent to 28/29 (Riksbank period-start renumber) and 22/23 (phantom step across a renumber), which test the boards, never the written workbook.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** All three code claims CONFIRMED: StoreBooks.cs:190 fixes the ticker to rung i+1 with a comment that admits the assumption; 199-200 lets BDP SW_EFF_DT win over the run's own date, so the row relabels itself to whatever contract the ticker now carries; 242-249 freezes the anchor as `Mid - dbp/100` written into the formula as a literal. The stale-docstring catch is real and the best part of the submission: StoreBooks.cs:133-137 still asserts the change columns 'look up the Historical_ sheet on the contract's own START DATE, never on the ticker number... that is what makes them roll-proof', which is precisely the property the two reverted attempts at 227-241 removed. Arithmetic checks: 1.95+0.01=1.96 -> (1.7100-1.96)*100 = -25.0; 1.7237+0.009=1.7327 -> (1.6350-1.7327)*100 = -9.77. FIXES: (a) the expected column is wrong - it calls (1.7100-1.7150)*100 a '1d' change, but 1.7150 is the 26-Aug print and the reopen is on 31-Aug; and 1.6390 is never seeded anywhere in the setup. Seed 28-Aug closes explicitly and derive against those. (b) PricingServiceBoards.cs:1045 is the `for (int n = 1; n <= maxRows; n++)` row loop and says nothing about this - drop it. (c) Severity: at publish time on 25-Aug the file was right; the fault is a live page carrying a frozen anchor under a '1d Chg' header, which degrades later. That is P1, and the distinction matters because two of my extras ARE wrong on the day of writing and deserve the P0 slot. (d) Say out loud that this is the sibling of candidate 4 (frozen change literal + live price) so the desk sees one mechanism, two sheets.

### 174. API down: desk types the day's FOMC marks into the saved book, presses EXPORT XLS, and the marks are overwritten before anything ingests them

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** Does the offline EXPORT XLS path protect desk-entered rows in the book it is about to rewrite?

**Set-up.** As written, plus: after pressing Store the desk CLOSES Excel (an open write lock makes ExtractTemplate throw and the file survives - that is a different scenario). publish.json has a dailyDir and a savedown root but NO `fallbackBook` key, or a fallbackBook that is not this file, so the only copy of the typed marks is OIS_Runs_28August26.xlsm.

**What must publish.** The 4.080 mark survives: it is ingested into the store as source='xls' before any rewrite, or the rewrite refuses. Tue 1-Sep (live USSOFED2 4.070) Historical_US must then read 31-Aug = 4.080 with d1 = (4.080-4.155)*100 = -7.5, and 1-Sep's board d1 = (4.070-4.080)*100 = -1.0.

**How it breaks.** ExportBook (DailyBuilder.cs:103-136) never calls IngestNewestSaved - only Render does (399-413). It goes straight to StoreBooks.WriteOis, whose ExtractTemplate does File.Create on the dated path (StoreBooks.cs:341-350), TRUNCATING the local 28-Aug book, then SaveDownConfig.Sync (DailyBuilder.cs:127-130, SaveDown.cs:117-120) copies it over the share's copy because the local mtime is now newer. The typed rows are gone from the file and were never in the store: FallbackIngest.cs:107 refuses rows dated >= today, so even a same-day ingest could not have taken them. Tue 1-Sep the desk reads Historical_US 31-Aug = 4.155 (the 28-Aug close stamped with 31-Aug's date, 7.5bp wrong) with d1 = 0.0, and 1-Sep's d1 = (4.070-4.155)*100 = -8.5 against a truth of -1.0 - a 7.5bp phantom one-day move, on a day the answer was sitting in the file the app destroyed.

**Against the existing suite.** The wrong-number half is the OPEN FINDINGS.md fault (BankHistoryRows.ValueAt stamping a walked-back close with the day it asked about) - I am not re-reporting that. The NEW fault is the loss: EXPORT XLS truncates and mirrors a book carrying desk-entered rows that nothing has read. Adjacent to 57 (save-down history across a decision), which never exercises the ingest/rewrite order.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Path CONFIRMED line by line. ExportBook (DailyBuilder.cs:103-136) ingests only the configured `fallbackBook` (111-112) and never calls IngestNewestSaved - that call exists solely on the Render path (DailyBuilder.cs:407-413). It then goes to WriteOis (123), whose ExtractTemplate does File.Create on the dated path (StoreBooks.cs:341-350), and SaveDownConfig.Sync (127-130 -> SaveDown.cs:115-121) copies local over the share on a bare mtime comparison. FallbackIngest.cs:107 `if (d.Value >= DateTime.Today) continue;` confirmed, so the typed rows were unreachable on the day and only tomorrow's run could have taken them - from a file this run destroys. Break arithmetic verified against DailyBook.cs:204-216: cur=ValueAt(31-Aug)=4.155, prev=ValueAt(PrevBd(31-Aug)=28-Aug)=4.155 -> d1 0.0; 1-Sep gives (4.070-4.155)*100 = -8.5 against a truth of -1.0. Discipline noted and respected: the author correctly ring-fences the ValueAt stamping fault as the OPEN item in the root FINDINGS.md:66-90 and claims only the loss. FIXES: (1) Severity. Once the wrong-number half is excluded by the author's own choice, what remains is destruction-without-a-gate: P1, not P0. (2) The setup must state that Excel is CLOSED when EXPORT XLS is pressed - if the desk still has the book open, File.Create throws IOException, DailyBuilder.cs:133 swallows it, and the scenario collapses into candidate 7 instead. (3) Scope the claim: ExportBook DOES ingest the incumbent `fallbackBook` first (111-112), so the sentence must be 'the SAVE-DOWN books are never ingested on this path', not 'nothing ingests'.

### 175. Every save-down book ever written ships a Current page dated 19-Aug-26 with 19-Aug change literals under today's filename

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** Is the incumbent Current entry page safe to ship untouched inside a workbook named for today?

**Set-up.** No market setup needed - this is true of every file the app writes. Run DAILY today 31-Aug-26 and open out\OIS_Runs_31August26.xlsm. The embedded template (templates/OIS_Store_Template.xlsm, EmbeddedResource per RateDesk.Weekly.Core.csproj:20) carries, in the shipped bytes: Current!D7 = Current!D8 = Current!D23 = 46253 = 19-Aug-2026 under the header 'CurrentDate'; Current!L7 = -0.1000000000000334, M7 = +0.1499999999999, N7 = -5.5200000000000138 under headers L6/M6/N6 = '1d Chg' / '1w Chg' / '1m Chg' for the RBA block; Current!L23/M23/N23 = +0.34 / +1.74 / +1.69 for the ESTR block. C7/C23 are live BDPs, so prices refresh and the change columns cannot.

**What must publish.** With no market seeded, assert template properties only: after WriteOis, out\OIS_Runs_31August26.xlsm must contain no cell on Current holding the date serial 46253 (19-Aug-26), and Current!L7:N7, L23:N23 and AB7:AD7 must be blank rather than literals, since the price cells beside them (C7, C23) are live BDPs. Separately assert Historical_SZ is empty - it currently ships 152 rows dated 30-Oct-2023 to 13-Dec-2023, and 'Coremont Riskbank' 6 rows. If a number is wanted, seed an RBA market and assert Current's '1m Chg' equals the Runs sheet's Delta-1m for the same contract, or is blank.

**How it breaks.** WriteOis rewrites only Runs, the nine Historical_ tables and Vandit/Blast (StoreBooks.cs:47-49, 99, 103-105); Current is deliberately left alone (StoreBooks.cs:51-54). So the desk's entry page - the one they read during an outage, when the app is not there to contradict it - shows CurrentDate 19-Aug-26 and 1m Chg -5.52 against the app's -13.0 (7.5bp), a '1d Chg' of -0.10 on every day for ever, and RBA/RBNZ zeros wherever the incumbent's IFERROR wrapped the literal. This is the same defect diagnosed at StoreBooks.cs:118-125 ('a stale zero reads as "the market did not move"'); the 2026-08-28 fix rewrote Vandit and Blast and left the page the fault was found on. The drift only grows: 12 days today, six months by year end. Two further template tabs ship stale for the same reason - Historical_SZ (154 rows of SNB history, no OisTags entry) and 'Coremont Riskbank'.

**Against the existing suite.** none - no scenario in the catalogue opens a written .xlsm. Related in spirit to 45 (notes are not content): a template leftover is not content either.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Verified directly in templates/OIS_Store_Template.xlsm by unzipping it. The freeze is far worse than claimed: 106 cells on the Current sheet carry the serial 46253 = 19-Aug-2026 (D7, D23, D34, D40, D41, T7/T8/T23/T40/T41, R9, R17, O10-O46, AE*, AG*), and the tables current_au..current_sek (D6:N17, T6:AE17, D22:N34, ... D73:N85) are all literal. L6/M6/N6 = '1d Chg'/'1w Chg'/'1m Chg' with L7/M7/N7 = -0.1000000000000334 / 0.14999999999991687 / -5.5200000000000138 and L23/M23/N23 = 0.34 / 1.74 / 1.69, exactly as claimed; AB7/AC7/AD7 = 0.25/0.25/0.75. C7 = _xll.BDP("RBACOR Index","LAST_PRICE"), C23 = _xll.BDP("ESTRON Index","LAST_PRICE") - live, so the price/change pairing does drift. WriteOis touches only Runs, the nine Historical_ tables and Vandit/Blast (StoreBooks.cs:47-49, 99, 103-105) and leaves Current alone by explicit design (51-54). The SZ point is stronger than stated: history_sz is A1:K154 with 152 dated rows running 30-Oct-2023 to 13-Dec-2023 - nearly three years stale in every book ever written. FIXES: (1) D8 is EMPTY - the claim 'D7 = D8 = D23 = 46253' is factually wrong; D34/D40/D41 are the other block anchors. Say 106 cells and name the blocks, not three cells. (2) Severity down to P1: Current is the incumbent's break-glass ENTRY page, no client-facing surface reads it, and the app not writing it is a documented decision (StoreBooks.cs:51-54); the fault is that the literal change columns were left behind when the same defect was fixed for Vandit/Blast. (3) The expected field invents an app output (RBA 1m = -13.0) for a scenario that declares 'No market setup needed'. Either seed a market that produces -13.0 or assert only template properties.

## Email and surface parity

*What the desk and its clients actually read.*

### 176. A tenth or renamed bank: the front table publishes it, the blast, workbook and sheet email drop it silently

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | moderate confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** When config/meetings.json carries a meeting run whose name is not in DailyBlast.Blocks, does anything on any surface say the run was dropped — and does the front table still advertise it?

**Set-up.** The desk asks for Switzerland on the run. A developer removes the SNB exclusion in PricingServiceWeekly.cs:211-212 (config/meetings.json already ships SNB in full: ccy CHF, tickers SFSF{N}A, fixingLagDays 1, fixingDcc 360, decisionDates 24-Sep-26 with decisionTimeLondon 08:30, period start 25-Sep-26). Nothing else changes. SARON fixes 0.000; the Sep-26 rung prints 0.005, the Dec-26 rung (start 11-Dec-26) prints −0.120, the Mar-27 rung −0.185. The other nine banks quote normally. Equivalent second trigger, same mechanism: the name "MPC" is edited to "BOE" to match the label the desk uses, leaving GBP (GPSF{N}A, SONIA) off the list.

**What must publish.** Either every surface carries the run, or the run is refused loudly. With SNB restored the blast, the Runs sheet and the sheet-style email body must each show a tenth block: "SNB closing run", "SARON fixing 0.000", rows 25-Sep-26 0.005 Priced +0.5, 11-Dec-26 −0.120 Priced −12.0 Step −12.5, 19-Mar-27 −0.185 Priced −18.5 Step −6.5. If the surfaces cannot carry it, the run must produce a CHECK note naming the bank and the front table must not print a line for a bank with no run block.

**How it breaks.** DailyBlast.Find returns null for any name not in the nine-entry Blocks array, and both the blast (DailyBlast.cs:45-48) and RunsTable.Build (RunsTable.cs:74-77) just `continue` — no note, no log line, no gap. So the chat blast, the DRAX OIS Runs workbook and the sheet-style email body carry nine blocks and no CHF anywhere, while SheetEmail.FrontTable iterates rep.Fronts unfiltered (SheetEmail.cs:152) and publishes a tenth headline line "SNB CHF | 24-Sep-26 | 25-Sep-26 | 0.005 | 0.000 | +0.5 | +2%", and the card email (WeeklyEmail.cs:243) shows the full SNB card. The desk sends a front table listing a bank and a run section that has never heard of it; with the BOE rename the missing block is the entire SONIA run, −18.5bp of MPC pricing gone from the blast. The suite stays green: Invariants.CrossSurface filters `expected` to banks present in DailyBlast.Blocks (Invariants.cs:172-177), so the dropped bank is exempt from the cell-for-cell check, and FrontTable only compares line count against rep.Fronts (Invariants.cs:287-291), which also contains it.

**Against the existing suite.** None of the 64 can express it: harness rule 4 (README "Bank names must be one of the nine") forbids the off-list bank this scenario needs, and the README states the drop as a fact of life rather than testing it. Adjacent to 46 (nine banks, one decides) and 44 (completeness gate: a bank publishes nothing on its decision day) — 44 covers a bank with no ROWS, this covers a bank with rows that no run surface can address. The harness needs its rule-4 guard relaxed for one scenario and an assertion that the drop is noted.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Cited lines hold — DailyBlast.cs:45-48 and RunsTable.cs:75-76 both bare-`continue` on an off-list name while SheetEmail.cs:152 iterates rep.Fronts unfiltered — but nothing wrong is published: the SNB front line and its +2% (0.5/25) are arithmetically correct, so this is a silent omission, not a wrong number. Reachability is also weaker than claimed: the SNB arm needs a source edit at PricingServiceWeekly.cs:211-212, and only the rename arm is desk-reachable, via the exe-side override at PricingServiceBoards.cs:228.

### 177. Inflation section threw during the run: yesterday's fixing table is appended under today's subject, two dates in one email

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** When the inflation section fails mid-run, does the click-time composer notice that the frozen fragment on disk belongs to a different as-of?

**Set-up.** Mon 31-Aug-26. DAILY RUN completes: OIS marks fine, nine blocks, subject-worthy. Inside DailyBuilder.Render the inflation section throws — an IOException writing into %APPDATA%\RatesWeekly\out (a roaming, sync-touched path) is the concrete trigger, and the log carries "! daily: inflation email section failed". Friday 28-Aug's out\daily_infl_sheet.html is still on disk from the previous successful run: UKRPI base 434.6, Mar-27 (BPSWIF3) mid 433.75, Δ1d +0.21. Over the weekend and on Monday the RPI strip repriced 14bp lower: Monday's true Mar-27 mid is 427.10. The desk clicks DAILY EMAIL with the inflation tickbox on (default).

**What must publish.** Either the email carries Monday's inflation table (Mar-27 427.10) or it carries none at all — matching out\daily_email.html, the frozen fragment the run actually wrote, which correctly ends after the OIS blocks because inflHtml is "". A composer that appends a fragment must check its as-of, exactly as InflHistory.LoadPersistedMarks refuses marks stamped on a different day.

**How it breaks.** DailyBuilder.Render swallows the failure (DailyBuilder.cs:437) and leaves the previous fragment untouched; the frozen daily_email.html is written without an inflation section (DailyBuilder.cs:443). But DAILY EMAIL does not use that file — it recomposes from daily_report.json and appends InflFragment(DailySheetHtmlFile, ticked), whose only test is File.Exists (MainWindow.xaml.cs:703-707, 864-868). So the outgoing draft is subject "DRAX Swaps Closing Runs - 31 Aug 2026", body title row "DRAX OIS Runs 31Aug26" for the OIS half and title row "DRAX Fixing Runs 28Aug26" for the inflation half (SheetEmail.cs:321 → InflRunsXlsx.Title), publishing Mar-27 at 433.75 with a Δ1d of +0.21 when the tenor is 6.65 index points (≈15bp of implied YoY) lower and unchanged on the day. The attached DRAX Fixing Runs 31Aug26.xlsx is also absent, so the status line says "1 workbook(s) attached (no inflation workbook found)" — the only clue, on a status bar, next to a body that looks complete. The asymmetry is deliberate elsewhere: marks persisted on a different day are refused (InflHistory.cs:381), fragments are not.

**Against the existing suite.** None — the README puts inflation out of scope ("the report is built meetingsOnly") and no scenario drives the click-time composer at all. Adjacent in spirit to 50 (one report, every surface): this is the case where one surface is NOT from that report. The harness would need to write out\ fragments, force the failure and call the composition path, which today lives only in MainWindow.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Mechanism is real: DailyBuilder.cs:437 swallows the failure and MainWindow.xaml.cs:703-707 appends the frozen fragment on File.Exists alone with no as-of test, against InflHistory.cs:381 which does refuse cross-day marks. But SheetEmail.cs:321 stamps the fragment with InflRunsXlsx.Title(asOf), so the stale section self-dates "DRAX Fixing Runs 28Aug26" and every value in it is a real 28-Aug close — a missing gate, not a wrong number.

### 178. Weekly CREATE EMAIL attaches last week's dashboards pack and says "Dashboards pack attached"

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | live-or-manual-only | corrected`

**The question.** Can the weekly email's investor-facing attachment be older than the body it is attached to, and does the app claim otherwise?

**Set-up.** Fri 28-Aug-26 weekly run: 989 tickers pulled, 28 currency pages written, then MoverScan/SiteFile throws (a single dead ladder pillar is enough) — the log carries "! movers/pack failed", pages = 28 so the run still reports WEEKLY COMPLETE, the email fragments are written and CREATE EMAIL unlocks. out\RatesWeekly_Dashboards.html is still 21-Aug's build: USD 1y1y 3.420, EUR 1y1y 1.905. This week's frozen report has USD 1y1y 3.610 and EUR 1y1y 1.845. publish.json carries siteBase, so the body's currency headers hyperlink to the live site. The desk clicks CREATE EMAIL and sends to clients.

**What must publish.** Either an attachment built from the same report as the body, or no attachment and a status line that says so. The body's Forward Rates Summary and the attached dashboards must agree on USD 1y1y to the decimal: 3.610 in both.

**How it breaks.** RenderAll swallows the pack failure (MainWindow.xaml.cs:659) and CREATE EMAIL attaches SiteFile.FileName on File.Exists alone (MainWindow.xaml.cs:731-733) — and that name is the constant "RatesWeekly_Dashboards.html" (Render/SiteFile.cs:16), so the exact-name hardening the daily attachments got ("never the newest file matching a glob", MainWindow.xaml.cs:910-925 using DailyBook.FileName(rep.AsOf)) is impossible here by construction. The client opens the pack and reads USD 1y1y 3.420 against a body saying 3.610 — 19bp apart, two weeks of curve in one email — while the app's own status line asserts "draft opened in Outlook — add recipients and send. Dashboards pack attached." (MainWindow.xaml.cs:735-736). The subject is correctly dated 28 Aug 2026 from the frozen report (MainWindow.xaml.cs:725-727), which makes the stale attachment look authoritative. Second arm of the same setup: the body is frozen, the hyperlinked site is not, so a client following the EUR header link at any later point reads different numbers from the table they clicked out of, with no as-of on the page to reconcile them.

**Against the existing suite.** None — 51-53 and 59 test the dashboard STRIPS' content, never the packaging, and the README puts dashboards out of scope for the report build. No existing scenario touches CREATE EMAIL. Testing this needs the WPF click path plus Outlook COM, so it is a manual pre-release check unless the attachment resolution is lifted into a testable function.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** MainWindow.xaml.cs:659 does swallow the pack failure and :731-736 attaches SiteFile.FileName on File.Exists alone while asserting "Dashboards pack attached.", but SiteFile.cs:39-40 stamps the pack's own title/header "week to 21 Aug 2026", so the attachment is self-dated and no wrong number is published. Downgrade to a missing freshness gate. **Change before writing it:** Drop the "no as-of on the page to reconcile them" claim for the attachment arm — the pack titles itself from its own asOf (SiteFile.cs:39-40); keep it only for the live-site hyperlink arm.
