### 179. Report frozen 09:36, DAILY EMAIL clicked 17:00: "Closing Runs" and "London EOD" over morning mids, no age anywhere

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** A report frozen at 09:36 and an email clicked at 17:00 — what date and what claim does the desk read, and does any surface carry the PRE-CLOSE warning the run generated?

**Set-up.** Mon 31-Aug-26, no decision. The desk runs DAILY at 09:36 London to get the blast out early. SnapDiscipline resolves PreClose (09:36 < 15:30) and adds "CHECK: PRE-CLOSE RUN — London 09:36, marks are live mids, not closes — verify before distribution" to rep.Notes; ConfirmChecks filters that exact note out, so no modal appears and the run completes. FOMC front rung (Oct-26, USSOFED2) is 3.905 at 09:36; EFFR (FEDL01) 4.330. Through the afternoon a strong payroll revision sells the strip: the 16:15 snap is 3.855. The app stays open; at 17:00 the desk clicks DAILY EMAIL and sends to the BCC list.

**What must publish.** Either the surfaces say what they are — subject "DRAX Swaps Closing Runs - 31 Aug 2026 (09:36 PRE-CLOSE)", blast headline naming 09:36 rather than EOD, and a PRE-CLOSE line in the body — or the click is blocked until a post-16:15 run exists. The number itself is honest for 09:36: Priced = (3.905 − 4.330) × 100 = −42.5bp. What the close-titled product must not do is publish it as the close, which is −47.5bp = (3.855 − 4.330) × 100. 5.0bp on the front rung, and the whole strip with it.

**How it breaks.** The blast headline is hardcoded "London EOD OIS Run — 31-Aug-26" (DailyBlast.cs:42-43); the sheet email's title row is "DRAX OIS Runs 31Aug26" (RunsTable.cs:65, SheetEmail.cs:198); the subject is "DRAX Swaps Closing Runs - 31 Aug 2026" (MainWindow.xaml.cs:888). rep.AsOf carries 09:36 (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:163 sets DateTime.Now) but every renderer formats it dd-MMM-yy / dMMMyy and drops the time, so the build hour reaches no surface. rep.Notes never reach any surface at all — DailyBuilder writes no notes file and SheetEmail.Body takes only (rep, front, runs) (SheetEmail.cs:124). The PRE-CLOSE note is deliberately excluded from the only modal that could have carried it (MainWindow.xaml.cs:334-336), and DAILY EMAIL's status line omits the build time that COPY BLAST does print ("built {File.GetLastWriteTime(blast):HH:mm}", MainWindow.xaml.cs:838, vs MainWindow.xaml.cs:933). So a client reads "Closing Runs" over 3.905 and prices a −42.5bp October when the close was −47.5bp, with nothing on the page to date the marks to the morning.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 45 (notes are not content) and 25 (the 16:15 snap anchors, the close never does). 45 proves diagnostics must not become content; this is the opposite question — the one note that is a claim ABOUT the marks, which every surface therefore has to carry. The README notes SnapDiscipline is not applied in the harness, so the scenario needs a seeded PRE-CLOSE note plus assertions on the three title strings and the subject.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Every cite holds — DailyBlast.cs:42-43 hardcodes "London EOD", MainWindow.xaml.cs:888 the "Closing Runs" subject, and MainWindow.xaml.cs:334-335 excludes PRE-CLOSE from the modal — and the arithmetic (-42.5 vs -47.5) is self-consistent. But the exclusion is a recorded desk decision and scenario 45 already locks "notes are not content", so whether this note must become a published claim is undecided, not a defect.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SnapDiscipline.cs:27` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SnapDiscipline.cs:41` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:334` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:838` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:888` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:933` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBlast.cs:42` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/RunsTable.cs:65` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SheetEmail.cs:124` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:163`

### 180. Surprise 50bp ECB cut: the front line reads −192% of 25bp and the footnote vouches for a base the code knows is stale

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** When the Priced re-base could only reach a PRE-STATEMENT mark, does the CB front table say so — and is its "% of 25bp" headline still meaningful?

**Set-up.** Thu 10-Sep-26, 13:15 London: the ECB cuts 50bp against an unchanged consensus (config ECB: tickers EESF{N}A, dates carry the 16-Sep-26 maintenance-period start six days after the 10-Sep announcement, decisionTimeLondon on file). The desk runs DAILY at 16:40. €STR still prints the old 2.000 and will until the new period starts. The decided period's rung is not separately quoted (the feed has not re-pointed) and has no post-13:15 close, so the re-base falls to path 3: the last close of that contract BEFORE the decision, 1.960 — the market had priced 4bp of the 25bp nobody expected. RefRebased = true, RefRebasedStale = true. The next rung (04-Nov-26 period) prints 1.480; the market's honest view of the decided period post-statement is ~1.505.

**What must publish.** The front line must qualify its base the way the run block does: fixing cell "1.960† (rebased, pre-statement)" and a footnote saying the base predates the statement, with % 25bp suppressed or flagged, because against the real post-cut base the December pricing is (1.480 − 1.505) × 100 = −2.5bp, i.e. −10% of 25bp — not (1.480 − 1.960) × 100 = −48.0bp, i.e. −192%.

**How it breaks.** WeeklyFront has no RefRebasedStale field — PricingServiceWeekly.cs:239-245 copies RefRebased and drops the stale flag, which only WeeklyRun carries (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:86-92 RebasedLabel). So the run block below prints "€STR fixing (rebased, pre-statement)" (SheetEmail.cs:206, RunsTable.Build passes run.RebasedLabel at RunsTable.cs:86) while the front table one inch above prints a bare "1.960†" (SheetEmail.cs:164-165; WeeklyEmail.cs:174) under a footnote that reads "† fixing re-based onto the just-decided period's OIS — the new rate has not printed yet" (WeeklyEmail.cs:185; SheetEmail.cs:183) — the precise claim RefRebasedStale exists to retract (see the field's own comment, "Surfaces say so instead of claiming the base is the decided period's current OIS"). The desk and the client read "ECB EUR … Priced −48.0 … −192%" on the headline line and conclude the market prices nearly two further cuts by November, when it prices a tenth of one. The suite cannot see it: Invariants.FrontTable asserts only `fr.RefRebased ? "†"` for the fixing cell and derives % 25bp from the same PricedBp (Invariants.cs:305-311).

**Against the existing suite.** Same mechanism as 19 (surprise 50bp cut), 61 (re-base fallback after a surprise) and 49 (front ordering and the sign of % of 25bp) — attacked from a new angle: those ask which VALUE the re-base picks; this asks whether the front table is allowed to render a stale base without the qualifier the run block gets, and what % of 25bp means when it is. 19/61 pass today because the invariants never compare the front's rebase LABEL against the run's.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** WeeklyFront (PricingServiceWeekly.cs:97-118) genuinely has no RefRebasedStale and the Fronts.Add at :239-246 drops it, while RebasedLabel at :91-92 gives the run block " (rebased, pre-statement)"; SheetEmail.cs:183 / WeeklyEmail.cs:185 print the bare "re-based onto the just-decided period's OIS" footnote the flag exists to retract. Arithmetic checks: -48.0bp = -192%, -2.5bp = -10%.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:86` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:239` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:960` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:972` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SheetEmail.cs:164` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SheetEmail.cs:171` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SheetEmail.cs:183` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/SheetEmail.cs:206` | `src/RateDesk.Core/WeeklyEmail.cs:161` | `src/RateDesk.Core/WeeklyEmail.cs:174` | `src/RateDesk.Core/WeeklyEmail.cs:185` | `tests/RateDesk.Scenarios/Harness/Invariants.cs:305`

### 181. Recipients: the weekly has no BCC path at all, and an unreadable list silently becomes zero addresses

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | moderate confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** Does the recipient plumbing make the desk's own rule — always BCC, never To/Cc — impossible to break, and does it distinguish "no list" from "list I could not read"?

**Set-up.** One morning, three things in sequence. (a) A junior edits %APPDATA%\RatesWeekly\recipients.json by hand into the shape a JSON file normally has — {"recipients": [ …19 client addresses… ]} — instead of a bare array. (b) The desk clicks DAILY EMAIL. (c) The head of desk then wants the weekly out to the same 19 clients plus two internal, opens RECIPIENTS to copy the list, and clicks CREATE EMAIL. One of the 19 is an ex-employee at a firm the desk stopped covering in June; nobody has looked at the list since it was pasted in from the incumbent workbook's VBA in April.

**What must publish.** (a) An unreadable list must stop composition — "recipients.json is unreadable, fix it before emailing" — the same discipline DAILY EMAIL already applies to an unreadable daily_report.json. (b) A run that would go to zero recipients must say so before Outlook opens, not in a status line after. (c) The weekly must apply Recipients.Bcc the way the daily does, or refuse to open a draft the operator has to address by hand.

**How it breaks.** (a) Recipients.Load deserialises to List<string>; the object shape throws JsonException, the catch turns it into Defaults, and Defaults is deliberately empty (Recipients.cs:28-41) — so the desk's 19 addresses become zero with no distinction between "never configured" and "cannot be read". (b) DailyEmail_Click reads bcc = Recipients.Bcc(AppDataDir), finds length 0, skips the assignment (MainWindow.xaml.cs:906-907) and reports "daily draft opened — 0 recipient(s) in BCC" (MainWindow.xaml.cs:933) — one digit on a status bar between two counts; the operator hits Send and the closing runs reach nobody, silently, for as many days as it takes someone to complain. Compare MainWindow.xaml.cs:874-880, where an unreadable REPORT correctly refuses to compose. (c) CreateEmail_Click has no recipient code whatsoever: it opens a draft and tells the operator "add recipients and send" (MainWindow.xaml.cs:735). Recipients.Bcc is called only from the daily path, and the dialog is titled "Daily email recipients" (MainWindow.xaml.cs:274), so there is no weekly list to apply — the more external product (dashboard links, investor-facing pack) has zero enforcement of the rule Recipients.cs:8-9 states emphatically, and the 19 client addresses go wherever the operator's cursor is, i.e. To. Nineteen hedge funds then see each other on a JB Drax distribution. (d) Nothing dates, reviews or backs up the list: Recipients.Save overwrites in place (Recipients.cs:43-48) and the dialog's Save has no confirmation and no diff (MainWindow.xaml.cs:290-297), so a stray keystroke over a select-all reduces 19 to 1 and the only feedback is "recipients saved — 1 address(es)"; the ex-employee's NDR lands in one sender's inbox and the entry survives indefinitely.

**Against the existing suite.** None — no scenario touches recipients or either Outlook path. Arms (a), (b) and (d) are pure-function tests of Recipients that the harness could run today (Load on a wrong-shaped file must not return an empty list indistinguishably from "unconfigured"); arm (c) is a design gap that needs a desk call on whether the weekly gets its own list or shares the daily's.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Recipients.cs:39-40 does conflate an unreadable file with "unconfigured" (both return the empty Defaults), and CreateEmail_Click (MainWindow.xaml.cs:709-742) contains no recipient code at all against the daily's :906-907 — but two break-mode claims are wrong, so confidence drops. **Change before writing it:** Arm (b): Outlook refuses to send a mail item with zero recipients, so "the closing runs reach nobody, silently, for days" is false — the honest break is that 19 addresses became 0 with a status-bar digit as the only signal. Arm (c): the weekly draft opens with NO recipient field set, so the addresses reach To only by operator action, not by code.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Recipients.cs:14` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Recipients.cs:28` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Recipients.cs:30` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Recipients.cs:43` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Recipients.cs:52` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Recipients.cs:57` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:274` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:290` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:735` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:874` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:906` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:933`

### 182. A 10bp trend week prints '12.5 sigma' as a hero card, next to a wk-vol cell reading 1.0x, on a public page

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** DESIGN.md 0a says movers must rank by RAW bp with the |z| column BLANK until the store holds ~1y. Is any sigma-derived number reachable and rendered today, and how wrong can it be?

**Set-up.** Store at its designed 45-day depth (UpdateEngine.SeedDays = 45), ~32 business closes. Seed USD 10Y (USOSFR10 BGN Curncy) trending steadily lower on a 5-day repeating step pattern of -1.6, -2.4, -2.4, -1.6, -2.0 bp/day, i.e. exactly -10.0bp every week for six weeks. Everything else in the universe quiet. asOf = the newest close.

**What must publish.** Per DESIGN.md:53-54 the movers hub must rank USD 10Y on -10.0bp of raw move with the z cell BLANK. And on the merits: a series that has fallen 10bp every week for six weeks has a weekly-change sigma of ~0 around its own mean of -10bp, so a -10bp week is the most ordinary week this instrument has — the honest |z| is near zero, not extreme. Hand check of the code's own numbers: diffs mean -2.0, deviations +0.4/-0.4/-0.4/+0.4/0.0; sum of squared deviations over 30 diffs = 3.84; /(31-1) = 0.128; sdDaily = 0.3578bp, just above the 0.3 floor. sigmaEst = 0.3578 x sqrt(5) = 0.8000bp. w1 = -10.0bp. The strict weekly sigma is unreachable: the marks loop needs 41 marks reaching 280 days back and the store holds 45, so it breaks at k=7 with 7 marks and sigmaWeekly stays null.

**How it breaks.** index.html renders the hero card 'USD 10Y — -10.0bp on the week · 12.5σ est' (z = -10.0 / 0.8000 = -12.50) and the ranked table shows '12.5σest' in the z column, ranking USD 10Y first in DM ahead of any instrument with a genuine 3-sigma shock. MoverScan.cs:101 builds sigmaEst = sqrt(5) x daily sigma, line 129-131 divides w1 by it, line 327 ranks on |z|, MoversPage.cs:96 and :160 print it, and MoversPage carries NO method note, NO legend and NO explanation of 'est' (a 9.5px superscript) because the desk's 2026-08-11 no-blurb rule strips MethodNote, G3Line and Notes from the page. The failure is exactly the one MoverScan's own doc comment (lines 42-44) predicts — 'the estimate understates sigma when weekly changes trend' — and DESIGN.md 0a's answer was to blank the column, not to label it. Worse, the same card contradicts itself: RMS(last 5 diffs) = sqrt(20.64/5) = 2.0318 and RMS(prior 25) = 2.0318, so 'wk vol vs norm' prints 1.0x — 'a completely normal week' beside '12.5 sigma'. movers.json carries z = -12.5 and est = true to disk as well.

**Against the existing suite.** None of the 64 touch MoverScan or the movers hub at all — the catalogue's dashboard group (51-53, 59) only drives RollingStrip.ForMeetings via Surfaces.Strips. Needs MoverScan.Scan wired into Surfaces plus arbitrary-ticker history seeding (Spec has ExtraQuote for live quotes only).

**Critic (CORRECTED).** MoverScan.cs:101 and :129-131 do build z off √5×daily σ and MoversPage.cs:96/:160 print it with only a 9.5px 'est' (Page.cs:170), DESIGN.md:53-54 does say blank the column, and the 32-close/31-diff setup really gives sdDaily 0.3578 → 12.5σ. But every market number on the card (-10.0bp, level, 45d range) is correct; what is absent is DESIGN §0a's depth gate and any legend, which is the P1 definition, not a wrong number. **Change before writing it:** MoverScan.cs:109 prior window is diffs.SkipLast(5).TakeLast(60) = 26 diffs here, not 25, so RMS(prior)=2.0169 and the ratio is 1.007 — still prints '1.0×', but fix the intermediate.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/MoverScan.cs:99` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/MoverScan.cs:101` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/MoverScan.cs:108` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/MoverScan.cs:116` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/MoverScan.cs:129` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/MoverScan.cs:327` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Series/MoverScan.cs:362` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Render/MoversPage.cs:60` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Render/MoversPage.cs:96` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Render/MoversPage.cs:160` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/UpdateEngine.cs:26` | `DESIGN.md:49` | `DESIGN.md:53`

### 183. The BOJ +62.6bp phantom 1w change the email fixed is still live on all 28 public pages

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | confirmed`

**The question.** The email caps a change anchor at 10 days older than its target because a far JPY generic's BDH gap once printed live-minus-a-year-ago as a 1w change. Do the dashboards cap anything?

**Set-up.** BOJ / JPY. The quoted forward S0195FS 20Y10Y BLC Curncy — the exact 'far generic' class the fix comment names — has stored closes daily up to 24 days ago at 1.980%, then its BDH returns nothing for three weeks (UpdateEngine.FetchAndStore counts it in noHistory and moves on), then a fresh close yesterday at 2.560% after a real 58bp yen long-end selloff across the gap. A close at day-32 exists at 1.975%. asOf = yesterday (store.LatestDate()). Faked: Bloomberg only.

**What must publish.** 1w: ForwardLadder asks for the value as of asOf-7. The newest close at or before that target is the day-24 close, i.e. 17 days OLDER than the target. The email's own rule (PricingServiceWeekly.ChangeToBp, maxStaleDays = 10) blanks a change whose anchor is more than 10 days behind its target, so the honest 1w cell is BLANK. 1m: the target is asOf-31; the anchor found is the day-32 close at 1.975, 1 day behind the target, inside the cap — so 1m legitimately publishes (2.560 - 1.975) x 100 = +58.5bp. Correct panel row: '20y10y   2.560   —   +58.5'.

**How it breaks.** jpy.html prints '20y10y   2.560   +58.0   +58.5'. HistoryStore.ValueAsOf (line 422) is an unbounded 'last close at or before' walk and every dashboard series reads it raw: ForwardLadder.cs:98 and :118 for the forward ladder, WeeklyCurves.cs:135 and :146 for the par curve, RollingStrip.cs:98 and :165 for the meeting strip, plus FraRun.cs:58 and Inflation.cs:71/:122. So (2.560 - 1.980) x 100 = +58.0bp — a 24-day move — is published under a column headed '1w', and the tell that would give it away (1w and 1m almost equal) is exactly what a reader dismisses as a month with all its move in the last week. The email's corresponding cell is blank; the two products, same click, same instrument, one blank and one +58.0. ForwardLadder's Notes never reach the page, so nothing says the anchor was stretched. Note the asymmetry is documented on the fixed side only: PricingServiceWeekly.cs:524-528 names the failure ('BOJ's far rows printed +62.6bp 1w changes that were live-minus-a-year-ago... A close more than 10 days older than the target is a different world: publish a blank, never that') and nobody carried it across.

**Against the existing suite.** Deliberately the same mechanism as 24 ('staleness cap: three-week hole must blank 1w'), from the opposite side: 24 proves the cap works on the EMAIL's meeting series; this shows the cap does not exist on any dashboard series. Needs arbitrary-ticker history seeding plus WeeklyCurves/ForwardLadder in Surfaces; the RollingStrip arm (a meeting rung with a three-week rung hole) is implementable as-is against Surfaces.Strips today.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** PricingServiceWeekly.cs:528-535 caps the email anchor at maxStaleDays=10 exactly as quoted, while HistoryStore.cs:422 ValueAsOf is an unbounded walk-back and ForwardLadder.cs:98→:118 feeds it straight into Panels.cs:66's '1w' column with no cap (same for WeeklyCurves.cs:135, RollingStrip.cs:98, FraRun.cs:58, Inflation.cs:71/:122). A 24-day move published as +58.0 under a header reading 1w on jpy.html is a wrong number on an investor page; the 1m +58.5 arm is self-consistent.

### 184. For five renders after an ECB surprise the movers hub prints the pre-statement level and a -1.0bp week

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** After an ECB decision, the roll-corrected series legitimately refuses every day from the announcement to the period start. What does the movers hub then publish as the instrument's LEVEL and its move on the week?

**Set-up.** ECB, 6-day lag. Surprise 25bp cut announced today-2 (a Wednesday); period start today+4, so MeetingRungMap._mixed = today-1, today, today+1, today+2, today+3, and today-2 is a boundary. The 17-Dec-26 rung closed 1.910 a week before the statement, drifted to 1.900 by the last pre-statement close (today-3), and is quoted 1.650 now. Other rungs the same shape. Desk runs WEEKLY on today (Friday); the store's newest close, and therefore asOf, is today-1. Faked: Bloomberg only.

**What must publish.** The honest answer for the week is (1.650 - 1.910) x 100 = -26.0bp — comfortably the largest move in the DM universe that week — and the level is 1.650, agreeing with the ECB card in the same email. If the roll-corrected series cannot reach a clean post-statement mark, the row must be EXCLUDED with the exclusion said out loud, not published on a pre-statement level.

**How it breaks.** MoverScan.MeetingSeries (line 186-188) correctly drops the boundary day and every mixed-state day, so the series' last usable point is today-3 — the last pre-statement close. Stats' freshness gate (line 79) is `(asOf - last) > 4` and asOf is today-1, so the gap is 2 days and the row PASSES. `now` = 1.900, At(asOf-7) = 1.910, so the hub publishes 'ECB 17-Dec-26 · level 1.900% · 1w -1.0 · z 1.0σ est · 45d range 1.890 … 1.915' — a level 25.0bp from the market, a range that excludes the market by 24bp, and a week that ranks nowhere while the biggest DM repricing of the week goes unreported. The same email's ECB meeting card prints 1.650. The window is five consecutive renders (the store's newest close anywhere from today-1 to today+3 leaves the last clean point within 4 days); on the sixth the row vanishes into `excluded++` (line 223), and MoversPage drops mv.Notes so the gate count never reaches the page. Aggravating: MoverScan.cs:310 builds its MeetingRungMap with no recordedEffective, the inference-only path the boards replaced on 2026-08-27 after it published -10.6bp where the truth was -0.6bp.

**Against the existing suite.** Deliberately the same mixed-state window as 14 (ECB hiked 3 days ago, run inside the mixed-state window) and 59 (dashboard strip after an ECB announcement, feed not re-pointed by close), attacked through a surface neither reaches: 14 and 59 test the email run and RollingStrip's strip, this tests MoverScan's ranking and its published LEVEL/RANGE. Needs MoverScan.Scan added to Surfaces.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** MeetingRungMap.cs:84-92 does put today-1..today+3 in _mixed with today-2 a boundary, MoverScan.cs:186/:188 drops them so h[^1]=today-3, and MoverScan.cs:79's gate is relative to asOf=today-1 so the row passes and publishes the pre-statement close as 'level' while the same email's ECB card prints 1.650. MoverScan.cs:310 does construct MeetingRungMap with no recordedEffective, so the aggravating point stands too. **Change before writing it:** The publish window is THREE renders, not five: with last clean point today-3 the gate (asOf-last) > 4 fails once asOf reaches today+2, so only asOf ∈ {today-1, today, today+1} passes. Also pin the today-8 close explicitly at 1.910 — 'closed 1.910 a week before the statement, drifted to 1.900' does not fix At(asOf-7).

### 185. Operator error: a throttled pull writes zero rows, and an undated public site presents a three-week-old week as this week

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | live-or-manual-only | corrected`

**The question.** The movers freshness gate rejects a close more than 4 days old. Four days older than WHAT — today, or the store's own newest row?

**Set-up.** Friday afternoon. The operator has been on leave; the last successful UPDATE was 21 days ago. The terminal is up and logged in but BDH is throttled (the shared allowance CLAUDE.md warns about): Prefetch throws, then 40 consecutive per-ticker fetches fail, so UpdateEngine hits MaxSingleFetchFailures, adds a warning and RETURNS NORMALLY with rows = 0. Assume USD 10Y fell 14.0bp in the week ending 21 days ago and has risen 3.0bp since. Faked: Bloomberg only; the store, engine, renderers and the WPF flow are production.

**What must publish.** Nothing publishable. A run that wrote zero rows and whose newest close is 21 days old must (i) block, or at minimum raise a modal rather than a log line, (ii) leave OPEN OUTPUT locked the way a failed email leg locks the email buttons, and (iii) stamp every rendered page with 'as of 10-Aug-26' so a stale upload is visible on the page itself. The movers freshness gate must measure against DateTime.Today, so with the newest close 21 days back every instrument fails the 4-day gate and the hub renders 'no instruments passed the data gates'.

**How it breaks.** asOf = store.LatestDate() (MainWindow.xaml.cs:620), i.e. 21 days ago, and Stats' gate is `(asOf.Date - h[^1].Date.Date).TotalDays > StaleDays` — measured against that same asOf. A uniformly 21-day-stale store is therefore uniformly 0 days 'fresh': nothing is excluded. The hub publishes 'USD 10Y · -14.0bp on the week · level 3.910%' for a week that ended three weeks ago, and the 28 currency pages publish three-week-old closes as levels. Neither carries a date anywhere: CurrencyPage.Build passes the heading 'DRAX Swaps - Weekly Rates Analysis - USD' and MoversPage.Build 'DRAX Swaps - Weekly Rates Analysis - Movers', while SiteFile.cs:39-40 — the emailed attachment — is the only surface that stamps 'week to {asOf:dd MMM yyyy}'. The engine's warnings ('stopped after 40 per-ticker fetch failures', 'N ticker(s) deferred') go only to the scrolling log (MainWindow.xaml.cs:593); the status bar reads 'updated 16:42:07 — 989 tickers, 0 rows written, 29 pages rendered' and SetOutputButtons(output: pages > 0) lights OPEN OUTPUT. The desk uploads. An external client with zero sign-in reads last month as this week and cannot tell. Same hole reachable in one step via `RatesWeeklyCli render --out <uploaddir>` with no `update` at all.

**Against the existing suite.** None — no existing scenario exercises the WPF button/gate flow, UpdateEngine's failure budget, or a store that is uniformly stale (the 64 all pin their own market and read today's clock). The MoverScan half of the assertion becomes implementable once MoverScan.Scan is in Surfaces; the button/modal half is manual.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** MoverScan.cs:79 does measure staleness against asOf and asOf is store.LatestDate() (MainWindow.xaml.cs:620, Program.cs:93), so a uniformly stale store is uniformly fresh; UpdateEngine.cs:151/:189 returns normally with warnings, MainWindow.xaml.cs:591-592 logs them and lights OPEN OUTPUT on pages>0, and CurrencyPage.cs:22-24 / MoversPage.cs:15-16 pass undated headings where SiteFile.cs:39 stamps one. Every published close is true as of its own date, so the fault is a missing gate, an undated page and a log-only warning — P1 by the given definition.

### 186. One email, three as-of times: 16:15 meeting cards, live-at-press forward grid, and five pinned cells inside that grid

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | high confidence | live-or-manual-only | confirmed`

**The question.** When the desk presses at 18:30 London after a 12bp afternoon selloff, which marks does each part of the email carry, and does anything on the page say so?

**Set-up.** Any Wednesday with a sharp post-16:15 move: the US 10y and 12y swaps sell off 12bp in a parallel shift between 16:15 and 18:30 London. Desk runs WEEKLY (or CREATE EMAIL) at 18:30. All nine banks quiet. NOK, DKK and CZK quoting normally. Store current. Faked: Bloomberg (a 16:15 bar set and an 18:30 live mid set that differ by 12bp on the USD pillars).

**What must publish.** Either every published mark in the body sits on one clock, or the body says which mark is which. Concretely, if the weekly is a 16:15 close product then the DM grid's USD 10y2y must print the 16:15 level: the cell is built from the endpoint pillars' own weights, so a uniform +12bp shift in r10 and r12 moves 10y2y by exactly +12.0bp, and the published cell must be 12.0bp below the 18:30 number.

**How it breaks.** SnapDiscipline.Apply is handed only `svc.MeetingTickers()` + `PricingService.WeeklyExtraTickers` + the twelve inflation fixing roots (EmailBuilder.cs:120-123). The curve pillars that build the forward grid are not in that set, so their snapshot mids stay at 18:30 while every meeting card is pinned to 16:15 — the code comment at EmailBuilder.cs:117-119 states this is deliberate ('The forward grid stays live-at-press — it is not a close product') but nothing labels it, and CLAUDE.md's PRESENTATION RULES forbid the footer/source line that could. Worse, WeeklyExtraTickers IS the five WeeklyCellOverrides securities, so NOK '1y' (NKSW1V3) and '1y1y' (S0312FS 1Y1Y), DKK '1y' and '1y1y' and CZK '1y1y' are 16:15-pinned while their row-mates 2y1y through 20y10y in the SAME ROW are 18:30 — a 12bp step inside one row that reads as curve shape, not as two clocks. And the currency headers hyperlink to dashboards rendered from store.LatestDate(), i.e. YESTERDAY's closes: a third as-of in the same click. A reader differencing the linked USD page's par 10Y against the email's 10y2y cell books 12bp of clock as market.

**Against the existing suite.** Adjacent to 58 (FOMC day: the 16:15 close precedes the 19:00 statement) and 25 (boundary-day sources: the 16:15 snap anchors, the close never does) — both about the meeting boards under SnapDiscipline. This is about the forward grid and the five override cells, which SnapDiscipline treats differently from their own neighbours; the harness README records that SnapDiscipline is not applied there at all, so this needs a manual or unit-test route.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** EmailBuilder.cs:120-123 hands SnapDiscipline.Apply only MeetingTickers + WeeklyExtraTickers + the twelve fixing roots, and PricingServiceWeekly.cs:365 confirms WeeklyExtraTickers IS the five WeeklyCellOverrides (NOK/DKK 1y+1y1y, CZK 1y1y at :353-360), so those cells pin to 16:15 while their curve-built row-mates stay live-at-press. The comment at EmailBuilder.cs:117 says the grid is deliberately live, so P1 / desk-call is the right pair; the +12bp parallel-shift arithmetic is correct.

### 187. '1y1y' means two different securities in one weekly, and four public pages carry a wholly derived forward ladder

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** Does the forward-ladder panel on a public currency page mean the same thing as the forward grid in the email that links to it?

**Set-up.** Two arms, one run. (a) NOK. nok.json: defaultProduct IRS, irs.fwdCurveId = S0313, irs legs 3M (maxTenor 23M) and 6M, and the 1Y pillar NKSW1 carries band '6M' with enabled: false. (b) BRL. brl.json has no fwdCurveId on either the ois or irs block. Seed BRL par pillars 5Y = 12.000% and 7Y = 12.600%; seed both NOK forward surfaces so S0313FS 1Y1Y and S0312FS 1Y1Y both quote. Store current, market quiet. Faked: Bloomberg only.

**What must publish.** (a) One instrument per label. The email prices NOK '1y1y' off S0312FS 1Y1Y BLC Curncy (the vs-3M FWCM surface, per the WeeklyCellOverrides comment, which also records that the vs-6M NKSW1 is 'the documented rich-vs-FRA quote'). The nok.html panel must therefore either read the same security or label itself as the vs-6M curve. Exact bp size of the NIBOR 3s6s surface gap needs a one-off terminal check before an expected number is fixed — the repo's only measured analogue is the EUR audit at +12.2bp at 1y1y for a 3s/6s surface mismatch — but the two cells cannot both be 'NOK 1y1y'. (b) A derived point must be marked as derived. AddApprox(sy=5, ty=2) computes (7 x 12.600 - 5 x 12.000)/2 = (88.200 - 60.000)/2 = 14.100%, which is the annuity-less par approximation the email refuses by name; the page must either mark it or not publish it.

**How it breaks.** (a) nok.html's forward-ladder panel resolves 1y1y via ForwardLadder.IdFor -> preferIrs -> id 'S0313', style Fwcm -> 'S0313FS 1Y1Y BLC Curncy' — the vs-6M surface — under the identical label '1y1y' the email uses for the vs-3M S0312FS. Same weekly, same label, two instruments, and the email's currency header links straight to the page. Additionally NOK's par panel has no 1Y row and the ladder's spot '1y' point is a dash: WeeklyCurves.Pillars marks NKSW1 not-natural (its band '6M' against SelectIrsLeg(1Y) = the 3M leg, maxTenor 23M) and it is enabled:false anyway, while the email prints NKSW1V3 — so the page is half-empty exactly where the email is populated, with the 'X of Y pillars had no stored close' note discarded. Same shape for DKK's DKSW1. (b) brl.html prints 'BRL forward ladder — 5y2y 14.100%' in the identical table shape as USD's quoted S0490FS points, with nothing to distinguish a curve-derived number from a market quote; the note ForwardLadder.cs:111-113 writes for exactly this case ('no quoted forward family for this currency — points derived from the par ladder') goes into LadderTriple.Notes, which Panels.Linked has no parameter for and CurrencyPage never reads. All 11 forward points on four of the 28 public pages (BRL, CLP, HKD, THB) are derived this way. The email's own comment refuses it in terms: 'LEVELS are curve-true... never the annuity-less approximation... an extrapolated number in an investor email is a lie with three decimals.'

**Against the existing suite.** None — no existing scenario touches WeeklyCurves, ForwardLadder or the par/forward panels; the harness README lists forward grids and dashboards as out of scope and builds the report meetingsOnly. Needs arbitrary-ticker history seeding plus CurrencyPage/ForwardLadder in Surfaces, and desk sign-off on the NOK 3s6s magnitude before arm (a)'s bp number is fixed.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** ForwardLadder.cs:41-49 on nok.json (defaultProduct IRS, irs.fwdCurveId S0313, blank style → Fwcm per ForwardTicker.cs:122) really does yield 'S0313FS 1Y1Y BLC Curncy' under the same '1y1y' label PricingServiceWeekly.cs:357 pins to S0312FS, and NKSW1 is both band '6M' vs SelectIrsLeg(1Y)=the 3M leg (SwapBuilder.cs:95) and enabled:false, so the page has no 1Y row; Panels.cs:31 has no parameter for the note ForwardLadder.cs:111 writes. Arm (b)'s mechanism holds but its set-up description does not. **Change before writing it:** brl.json carries NO ois or irs block at all (only a RATE ladder, which WeeklyCurves.cs:69-78 falls back to) — not 'no fwdCurveId on either block'. And WeeklyCurves.InterpAt never extrapolates past the 10Y last DI pillar, so only six grid points derive (1y1y, 2y1y, 3y1y, 4y1y, 5y2y, 7y3y); drop the '11 forward points' figure.

### 188. ECB and NORGES both have a period start with no decisionDate — Priced reads 24bp wrong for two weeks, live in the shipped config

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** A period start sits in `dates` with no matching `decisionDates` entry. The desk knows the front table degrades to `start *`. Does anything else degrade — and does the desk find out?

**Set-up.** Shipped config, LIVE: ECB `dates` carries 2027-05-06 while `decisionDates` jumps 2027-03-18 → 2027-06-10 (config/meetings.json:100,118-120); NORGES `dates` carries 2027-05-06 while `decisionDates` jumps 2027-03-18 → 2027-06-17 (:552,570-571). Both are deliberate — the file's own comments (:111-113, :562-563) say the Ascension-week Thursday start is "left for the official calendar". Harness form, relative to today: ECB, lag 6. Dates = {T-97, T-46, T+3, T+52, T+101}; decisionDates = {T-103, T-52, T+46, T+95} — the announcement for the T+3 start is ABSENT, the ones either side are present. The real ECB cut 25bp at T-3 effective T+3; €STR legitimately still prints the old 1.900 (the new 1.650 applies only from T+3). The feed has re-pointed, so the board's rows are the T+52, T+101, T+150 periods at 1.620 / 1.600 / 1.590; the decided T+3 period's own OIS quotes 1.660.

**What must publish.** Priced must re-base onto the decided period's own OIS, 1.660, and be marked †: (1.620-1.660)*100 = -4.0; (1.600-1.660)*100 = -6.0; (1.590-1.660)*100 = -7.0. Front line: T+52, Priced -4.0, -16% of 25bp. A CHECK-prefixed note must say the calendar has no announcement for the T+3 period.

**How it breaks.** The re-base gate (PricingServiceBoards.cs:895-906) walks `sched.DecisionDates` only: lastDec = T-52, effStart = T-46, windowEnd = T-46, today > windowEnd, so no re-base fires. Published Priced = (1.620-1.900)*100 = -28.0 / -30.0 / -31.0 — every cell 24bp too negative, with NO † and no `(rebased)` on the blast, so every surface asserts the base is current. The blast/xls/email front line reads "16-Jun-27 · 1.620 · -28.0 · -112%" — "another full cut priced for June" when the market prices 4bp. DecisionFor (DecisionClock.cs:46-54) also returns null (nearest decision 49 days away, outside PairToleranceDays=10) so the time-gated roll never fires for this meeting either. The only warning is CalendarHealth's runway line (CalendarHealth.cs:64-67) — un-prefixed, so ConfirmChecks (MainWindow.xaml.cs:334) ignores it, and notes are not content, so it renders nowhere; the `decisions.Count == 0` CHECK at :77-80 cannot fire because the list is non-empty. Meanwhile MeetingCalendar.AnnouncementDates (:96-105) silently DERIVES T-3 as a boundary and MeetingRungMap (:51) uses it, so one module invents the date two others refuse to act on.

**Against the existing suite.** 48 (empty decisionDates → {start}* marker) and findings F1/F6/F12 (scenarios 54, 55, 61) attack the same re-base. Difference: this is a HOLE in a populated list, so every fix that closed F1/F6 is bypassed and no CalendarHealth arm can fire — 48's empty-list warning is gated on Count==0, and F1's fix engages only when the decision is in the list. Nothing in the 64 covers a partially-populated decision calendar.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** config/meetings.json:100 vs :119-120 is a live hole (2027-05-06 start, decisionDates jump 2027-03-18→2027-06-10), and PricingServiceBoards.cs:895-925 picks lastDec from DecisionDates only, so effStart lands on the prior period and windowEnd has long passed — no re-base, Priced measured off the stale fixing. CalendarHealth.cs:64-67 is genuinely un-prefixed and :77 is gated on Count==0, while MeetingCalendar.cs:96-105 does derive start−lag (ECB lag is a stable 6d across all ten pairs) and MeetingRungMap.cs:51 consumes it.

### 189. FOMC decisionDates entered as the first meeting day, not the statement day: the board drops the Fed meeting on Fed day

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | confirmed`

**The question.** A two-day-meeting bank's announcement date is entered one day early — the trap the config's own comment warns about. Which guard catches a 1-day decisionDates error, and what does the blast say on the statement day?

**Set-up.** FOMC, whose 2027 dates were hand-copied from fomccalendars.htm with the note "statement day = second meeting day" (config/meetings.json:53-54). Simulate the copy error: today T is the FOMC statement day and the period start; `decisionDates` carries T-1 (the first meeting day) instead of T; decisionTimeLondon 19:00 (:68); FixingLagDays 1 (:23). Daily run pressed at the 16:15 close, so MarksAsOfLondon = T 16:15 (DailyBuilder.cs:229-231) — i.e. BEFORE the 19:00 statement. USSOFED0 mat T; USSOFED1 eff T mat T+49 mid 3.660; USSOFED2 eff T+49 mid 3.480; USSOFED3 eff T+98 mid 3.400. FEDL01 prints 3.900. Store holds 45d of flat closes.

**What must publish.** At 16:15 on statement day nothing has been announced, so the board must keep the meeting it is trading: T | 3.660 | -24.0 | — ; T+49 | 3.480 | -42.0 | -18.0 ; T+98 | 3.400 | -50.0 | -8.0. Fixing 3.900, no †. Front table: FOMC, decision T, start T, Priced -24.0, -96% of 25bp. (Priced = (mid-3.900)*100; Step = ΔPriced.)

**How it breaks.** Announced(T-1, "19:00", T 16:15) returns true at DecisionClock.cs:37 (nowLondon.Date > decisionDate.Date), so the gateShift loop (PricingServiceBoards.cs:861-864) drops rung 1 and NextDecision becomes T+49. The re-base then fires (lastDec = T-1, effStart = T, windowEnd = T+1 business day, today ≤ windowEnd, gap 1 ≤ 10) and finds the decided period live at quotes[0] (:932-935), so RefPct = 3.660 †. The desk's blast on Fed afternoon reads: header "(EFFR 3.660 rebased)"; rows 17-Mar-27 | 3.480 | -18.0 | — and 28-Apr-27 | 3.400 | -26.0 | -8.0; front table "FOMC · 17-Mar-27 · -18.0 · -72%". Every Priced is 24bp light, the meeting the market is trading has vanished, and the front table names a decision seven weeks away on the day the Fed decides. Nothing catches it: both config invariant tests pass (BasisGuardTests.cs:600 monotonic ✓; :623 pairs T-1 with a start 1 day later, inside the 10-day tolerance ✓); CalendarHealth check 1 pairs the ticker's own eff T with the grid ✓, check 3's window carries ±2 days ✓; and FuturesGuard structurally cannot see it — the front FF window is skipped by `rows[0].Date > a` at FuturesGuard.cs:64 precisely BECAUSE the front row was removed, so the guard blends a later, correct window and reports ok.

**Against the existing suite.** 4 (decision today BEFORE announcement time) and 58 (16:15 close precedes the 19:00 statement) drive the same gate — both with a CORRECT calendar. This attacks the gate from the config side: the mechanism that makes 4 and 58 behave correctly is exactly what makes a 1-day-early calendar entry catastrophic, and the same typo on the BOJ (2-day MPMs, config/meetings.json:447) fires identically.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** DecisionClock.cs:37 returns true on any later date, so the gateShift loop at PricingServiceBoards.cs:861-864 drops rung 1 and the re-base at :925-935 finds eff==T at quotes[0] → RefPct 3.660; arithmetic checks (-24/-42/-50 expected vs -18/-26 published, 24bp light). FuturesGuard.cs:64 does skip the affected front window once rows[0].Date moves past `a`, and BasisGuardTests.cs:623 waves a 1-day lag through (it also skips past decisions entirely).

### 190. Transposed day in the Riksbank far grid publishes an 18-day-wrong tradeable period, because trustConfigDates makes config dates publishable

`P1 - missing gate or note | needs a desk call | moderate confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** trustConfigDates lets desk-verified config dates publish rows. What happens when one of those hand-curated dates has a transposed day, and which guard sees it?

**Set-up.** RIKSBANK exactly as shipped and as pinned by TrustConfigDatesTests.cs:127-136 with the probed 2026-08-25 field state: SKSF0A-4A carry eff/maturity, SKSF5A/6A quote real prices (2.059, 2.155) with NO date fields, so rows 5 and 6 take their dates from `dates` and trustConfigDates adds them to tickerDated. Config far grid: 2027-02-10, then 2027-03-31 typed as 2027-03-13 (31→13, the classic transposition) — config/meetings.json:486-487. markTurnPeriods and rollsAtPeriodStart also as shipped (:514,:518). Fixing SWESTR 1.500. Market flat all month.

**What must publish.** Row 5 publishes 10-Feb-27 | 2.059 | +55.9 | … with EndDate 31-Mar-27, and row 6 publishes 31-Mar-27 | 2.155 | +65.5 | Step +9.6. (Priced = (2.059-1.500)*100 = +55.9 and (2.155-1.500)*100 = +65.5; Step = 65.5-55.9 = +9.6.) A period grid that cannot be corroborated by a ticker field must not silently label a row.

**How it breaks.** The fill loop at PricingServiceBoards.cs:757-776 takes the config date verbatim and :773 marks it publishable, so the blast, the Runs workbook and both emails print "13-Mar-27" as the start of a period that begins 31-Mar-27, and the row above it prints EndDate 13-Mar-27 (:1117). The prices, Priced and Step are all correct — only the DATES lie, which is why it looks right: a client quoting the SEK meeting swap off that row books a 10-Feb→13-Mar (31-day) period against a real 10-Feb→31-Mar (49-day) contract, a 37% error in the period the rate applies over, with a ~10bp step across the boundary. Nothing sees it: CalendarHealth check 1 needs `q?.Effective` and returns at CalendarHealth.cs:48 because SKSF5A/6A publish no SW_EFF_DT (and its ±10-day PairToleranceDays would wave through anything under 10 days even if they did); SEK has no `guardFutures` at all, so the one run allowed to publish config-dated rows is the only run with no independent cross-check; and both config invariant tests pass (13-Mar-27 > 10-Feb-27 is monotonic, 31 days clears the 14-day cluster). ESCALATION, same typo class: if the transposition crosses a year boundary (2027-12-22 typed 2028-01-22), turn0 at :1061 masks the WRONG row — the November meeting publishes "Y/E Turn" with no numbers while the turn-contaminated December print publishes bare, and the step chain (:1119-1122) hangs a cumulative move on a period that does not exist.

**Against the existing suite.** 32 (trustConfigDates priced rungs with no date fields) proves the carve-out WORKS on a correct grid; 33 covers the turn row as the front. Neither perturbs the grid. This is the adversarial complement: 32's premise is that the desk verified the grid, and the scenario tests what the app does when that premise fails by 18 days.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Set-up is faithful to the probed state in TrustConfigDatesTests.cs:127-136 and PricingServiceBoards.cs:773/:1052/:1117 do publish and label config-filled rungs, with no guardFutures on RIKSBANK and CalendarHealth.cs:48 continuing for date-less SKSF5A/6A. But the code implements exactly the carve-out the desk signed off at config/meetings.json:519-523, every published Mid/Priced/Step is correct, and the grid is pinned by test — so this is a desk call on withdrawing trustConfigDates, not a defect, and a mislabelled boundary is P1.

**Code.** `config/meetings.json:486` | `config/meetings.json:514` | `config/meetings.json:519` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:757` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:768` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:773` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1052` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1061` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1117` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CalendarHealth.cs:45` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CalendarHealth.cs:50` | `tests/RateDesk.Tests/TrustConfigDatesTests.cs:127` | `tests/RateDesk.Tests/BasisGuardTests.cs:614`

### 191. FOMC loses its refTicker and Priced silently re-bases onto SOFR, while the blast keeps printing the hardcoded label "EFFR"

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | moderate confidence | needs-harness-extension | confirmed`

**The question.** Eight of the ten runs carry no refTicker and fall back to the currency config's overnight fixing. What does the fallback publish for USD, and can any surface disagree with the label?

**Set-up.** FOMC as shipped except `refTicker: "FEDL01 Index"` is absent — a field lost to a merge resolution, or removed as "redundant" because policyLadder already says FedFunds (config/meetings.json:19,28). `policyLadder` stays, so the mids remain Fed Funds. USD's currency config supplies the fallback: config/currencies/usd.json:19 `onFixingTicker: "SOFRRATE Index"`. Seed a quarter-end/year-end repo print: FEDL01 3.900, SOFRRATE 4.180. USSOFED1 3.900, USSOFED2 3.820, USSOFED3 3.760, USSOFED4 3.720. All closes flat, so every change column is 0.0.

**What must publish.** Priced against EFFR: 0.0, -8.0, -14.0, -18.0; Steps —, -8.0, -6.0, -4.0. Blast header "(EFFR 3.900)". Front table 0.0 / 0%.

**How it breaks.** PricingServiceBoards.cs:822 resolves RefName = cfg.Ois.OnFixingTicker = SOFRRATE, and :825 fills RefPct = 4.180. Published Priced = (3.900-4.180)*100 = -28.0, -36.0, -42.0, -46.0 — the whole column shifted 28bp, the front line reading "-28.0 · -112% of 25bp" on a day the Fed is exactly at target. The Steps are unchanged (-8.0/-6.0/-4.0) and every Δ1d/Δ1w/Δ1m reads 0.0, so the internal invariants Priced == (Mid-Fixing)*100 and Step == ΔPriced all hold and no cross-surface check can fire. There is no guard on Priced at all: OutlierGuard's bars (OutlierGuard.cs:23) apply to Δ1d/Δ1w/Δ1m only. Worse, the label is a lie the code cannot notice — DailyBlast.Blocks hardcodes the string "EFFR" for FOMC (DailyBlast.cs:23) and prints it beside whatever number RefPct holds (:53), while RefName never reaches any client surface (`grep -rn RefName src/` reaches only the CLI board at :1380 and the unquoted-fixing note at PricingServiceWeekly.cs:251). The blast therefore reads "(EFFR 4.180)" — an EFFR label on a SOFR print. On an ordinary day the same fault is a silent 2-3bp shift in every Priced cell, forever, below the desk's noticing threshold. CompoundedFixing.ComputeFor (:125-127) takes the identical fallback, so the trial mechanics move with it.

**Against the existing suite.** 39 and finding 10 cover a DEAD fixing ticker (RefPct null → Priced blank, honest). This is the opposite and worse case: a fixing that quotes fine and is the wrong index. ConfigWriter has DisableGuardFutures but no way to drop refTicker — add `DisableRefTicker` alongside ConfigWriter.cs:54. The 28bp magnitude needs desk sign-off on a representative SOFR/EFFR dislocation; the mechanism and the mislabel do not.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** PricingServiceBoards.cs:822 falls back to cfg.Ois.OnFixingTicker = SOFRRATE (config/currencies/usd.json:19) and ignores the FedFunds ladder's own fixingTicker FEDL01 at usd.json:196; 8 of the 10 meeting runs carry no refTicker as claimed. DailyBlast.cs:23/:52-53 does hardcode "EFFR" beside whatever RefPct holds, and `grep -rn RefName src/` reaches only PricingServiceBoards.cs:1380/:1467 and PricingServiceWeekly.cs:251 — no client surface can contradict the label.

**Code.** `config/meetings.json:19` | `config/meetings.json:25` | `config/currencies/usd.json:19` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:822` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:825` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:1102` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBlast.cs:23` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBlast.cs:52` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:125` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:23` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:251`

### 192. Operator hand-patches one bank's calendar next to the exe and six of nine banks vanish from the blast with zero warnings

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** The documented way to fix a calendar in a hurry is a config\meetings.json next to the exe. It REPLACES the embedded list rather than merging. Does the completeness gate notice the banks that disappear?

**Set-up.** An inter-meeting ECB cut is announced at 11:40 London. The operator needs the boundary in today's calendar before the 16:15 run, opens config/meetings.json in the repo, copies the ECB block plus the two nearest banks he can see on screen (MPC, FOMC) into publish\config\meetings.json, adds the emergency decision, saves, and presses UPDATE. RBA, RBNZ, BOC, NORGES, BOJ, RIKSBANK are not in the file. Market otherwise ordinary, every bank quoting normally.

**What must publish.** Either the override merges by run name so the other six survive, or the run raises a CHECK naming each of the nine expected banks that the active calendar does not carry, and ConfirmChecks blocks the press.

**How it breaks.** MeetingsStore.Load (PricingServiceBoards.cs:228-238) reads the override wholesale — no merge — so Schedules has three runs. The blast prints its hardcoded nine-flag title line "{EU} {GB} {AU} {NZ} {US} {CA} {JN} {NO} {SW} London EOD OIS Run" (DailyBlast.cs:42) and then three blocks, because :48 does `continue` on a run it cannot find. The completeness gate cannot see it: CompoundedFixing.cs:103 derives its roster from `MeetingsStore.Schedules` — the very file it is meant to police — so it checks three banks against three and says nothing; its second arm (:112) runs config→Blocks and never Blocks→config, which is the direction that would have caught it. The Runs workbook, both email bodies, the front table and the per-currency dashboard strips (CurrencyPage.cs:44 iterates Schedules) all lose the same six banks. Zero notes, zero CHECK, no popup, and MeetingsStore.Origin — which records exactly that an override is active (:229) — is read NOWHERE in the codebase (`grep -rn "\.Origin\b" src/` returns nothing), so nothing on any surface says the exe is running a hand-edited calendar. The desk mails a nine-flag header over a three-bank table on the one afternoon everyone reads it.

**Against the existing suite.** 44 (a bank publishes nothing on its decision day) and 46 (nine banks, one decides) exercise the gate for a bank PRESENT in the config that produces no rows. This attacks the gate's roster source instead — and note the harness itself demonstrates the hole: ConfigWriter.cs:36 writes exactly this kind of partial override for all 64 scenarios and the absent banks have never once been flagged.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** Mechanism verified: PricingServiceBoards.cs:229-238 reads the override wholesale with no merge, CompoundedFixing.cs:103 draws its roster from the overridden MeetingsStore.Schedules, DailyBlast.cs:42 prints the nine-flag title while :48 continues past absent runs, and `grep -rn "\.Origin\b" src/` returns nothing. Severity is wrong though — six banks silently vanish and the header misleads, but no published number is wrong, which is the P1 bucket.

**Code.** `config/meetings.json:10` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:228` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:237` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:103` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CompoundedFixing.cs:112` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBlast.cs:17` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBlast.cs:42` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBlast.cs:48` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Render/CurrencyPage.cs:44` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:334` | `tests/RateDesk.Scenarios/Harness/ConfigWriter.cs:36`

### 193. Calendar topped up while the app is open: the Lazy cache serves the stale list and the runway warning repeats verbatim, teaching the desk to ignore it

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | live-or-manual-only | confirmed`

**The question.** CalendarHealth's 90-day runway warning is the whole zero-touch guarantee. If the operator acts on it without restarting the exe, does the warning clear — and can it stop a press if it doesn't?

**Set-up.** Late May 2027. RIKSBANK decisionDates end 2027-06-22 (config/meetings.json:507) while `dates` run to 2027-12-22 (:493), so the first uncovered start is 2027-08-25 and the runway check has just started firing. RatesWeekly.exe has been open since Monday (the release ritual assumes a running app: "exit running app, swap publish\, relaunch"). The operator reads the warning in the status log, edits publish\config\meetings.json to add 2027-08-11 and 2027-09-15 to decisionDates, saves, and presses UPDATE again.

**What must publish.** The second run must read the edited file and drop the warning — or say plainly that the calendar it is using was loaded at NN:NN from <path> and has not been re-read.

**How it breaks.** Schedules is a `Lazy<List<MeetingScheduleDef>>` (PricingServiceBoards.cs:214) evaluated once per process, and nothing reloads it; the second UPDATE runs on the pre-edit calendar and reprints the identical warning. The operator's only evidence that the edit landed is the warning clearing, so an unchanged line reads as a stale or spurious alert. Compounding it, the warning is not CHECK-prefixed (CalendarHealth.cs:64-67 has no OutlierGuard.Prefix, unlike :78 and :82), so ConfirmChecks (MainWindow.xaml.cs:334) never sees it, notes are not content, and the 90-day tripwire is a scrollable log line. Consequence with a date on it: on 2027-08-11 the Riksbank cuts 25bp, DecisionFor (DecisionClock.cs:46) finds no decision within 10 days of the 2027-08-25 start, so the front roll never fires and the re-base never fires; the SEK board publishes the delivered cut as still priced in — with a -25.0 Priced and -100% of 25bp on the front line — for the whole 11-Aug→25-Aug window. Second break on the same path, and sharper: MeetingsStore.Load sets Origin BEFORE parsing (:229) and has no fallback to the embedded resource, so one unparseable date in that hand edit ("2027-09-31", "2027-13-15") throws a JsonException inside a Lazy that CACHES the exception for the life of the process — the app is dead until relaunched, and the failure names the file only if someone reads the exception text. (Comments and trailing commas ARE tolerated, :224-225 — worth locking.)

**Against the existing suite.** 9 (run out of meetings) and 48 (empty decisionDates) cover the downstream state. Nothing in the 64 touches the loader's lifetime — and it cannot, by construction: the harness README pins one process per scenario precisely BECAUSE MeetingsStore caches, so the suite's own design hides this class. Needs a MeetingsStore.Reload() or a two-process runner before it is testable.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** PricingServiceBoards.cs:214 is a plain Lazy with no Reload anywhere in src/, so the second UPDATE reprints CalendarHealth.cs:64-67 verbatim — and that arm carries no OutlierGuard.Prefix, unlike :78/:82, so MainWindow.xaml.cs:334 never sees it. The runway arithmetic is right (dates :490 2027-08-25 is the first start uncovered by decisionDates ending :507 2027-06-22), and Lazy's default ExecutionAndPublication does cache the JsonException for the process life.

**Code.** `config/meetings.json:493` | `config/meetings.json:507` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:214` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:224` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:229` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceBoards.cs:237` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CalendarHealth.cs:56` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CalendarHealth.cs:64` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/CalendarHealth.cs:78` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:334` | `src/RateDesk.Core/Dates/DecisionClock.cs:46`

## Operator error

*The human, at the wrong time, on the wrong machine.*

### 194. Laptop still set to Sydney: every surface stamped tomorrow, Δ1w silently a 6-day change

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | confirmed`

**The question.** The app converts to London time for the decision gates but takes its as-of date and its change anchors from the machine's LOCAL clock. What does a 16:20-London run publish on a machine whose Windows timezone is not London?

**Set-up.** Tue 1-Sep-26. The desk laptop came back from a Sydney trip with its Windows timezone still (UTC+10) AEST; London is BST (UTC+1), so local time runs +9h. No bank decides. Operator presses DAILY RUN at 16:20 London = 01:20 local on Wed 2-Sep. ECB, EESWE family, €STR fixing 1.920. Store: 25-Aug close 1.850 (rung carrying the 17-Sep period), 26-Aug close 1.912 (a +6.2bp ECB-repricing day), 31-Aug close 1.865. Live 1-Sep 16:15 snap for that contract: 1.925. Nothing else is faked.

**What must publish.** SnapDiscipline.Resolve(LondonNow=16:20) returns Snap1615 and pins the mark to 1-Sep's 16:15 bar — correct. Everything dated must then read 1-Sep: blast header "London EOD OIS Run — 01-Sep-26", workbook "DRAX OIS Runs 1Sep26.xlsx", save-down "OIS_Runs_1September26.xlsm", subject "DRAX Swaps Closing Runs - 01 Sep 2026". Δ1w must anchor 25-Aug (London today minus 7): (1.925 − 1.850) × 100 = +7.5bp. Δ1d must anchor 31-Aug: (1.925 − 1.865) × 100 = +6.0bp.

**How it breaks.** PricingServiceWeekly.cs:163 sets AsOf = DateTime.Now = 2-Sep 01:20 local, so DailyBlast.cs:43 prints "London EOD OIS Run — 02-Sep-26", DailyBook.cs:19 writes "DRAX OIS Runs 2Sep26.xlsx", StoreBooks.cs:25 writes "OIS_Runs_2September26.xlsm" into the C+C share, and MainWindow.xaml.cs:888-889 subjects the client mail "02 Sep 2026" — a full day ahead of the marks in it, and colliding with Wednesday's real run under the same filenames (that day's save-down record is overwritten and lost). PricingServiceWeekly.cs:287 targets DateTime.Today.AddDays(-7) = 26-Aug, and ChangeToBp (:532-535) takes the last point at or before it, so Δ1w publishes (1.925 − 1.912) × 100 = +1.3bp — a 6-day change under a 1w label, understated by 6.2bp. Δ1d targets 1-Sep, i.e. the mark's own day: it either finds a 1-Sep row (HistoryStore.cs:384-387 guards on DateTime.Today = 2-Sep, so this machine is also allowed to store an unsettled 1-Sep value as a close) and prints 0.0, or walks to 31-Aug and is right by accident.

**Against the existing suite.** none — the catalogue never varies the machine timezone; the README lists the east-of-London date rollover only as a HARNESS limit, not as a product exposure

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** PricingServiceWeekly.cs:163 does set AsOf = DateTime.Now and :286-288 anchor on DateTime.Today, while DecisionClock.cs:21-29 converts only the gate clock, so a +9h machine stamps every surface (DailyBlast.cs:43, DailyBook.cs:19, StoreBooks.cs:25, MainWindow.xaml.cs:888) a day ahead and ChangeToBp (:532-535) serves a 6-day move as Δ1w. Arithmetic checks: +7.5bp expected vs +1.3bp published, 6.2bp understated.

### 195. 09:40 "quick look" run mailed at 17:05 as Closing Runs — PRE-CLOSE appears on no surface

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** A PRE-CLOSE run is deliberately excluded from the blocking popup and notes are never content. What does the desk send if a morning run is never re-run before the email goes out?

**Set-up.** Tue 1-Sep-26, no decisions. Operator presses DAILY RUN at 09:40 London to see where things are, then leaves the app open. ECB, €STR fixing 1.920; 09:40 live mids: 17-Sep period 1.870, 29-Oct period 1.795. The market rallies through the day; by 16:15 the same contracts are 1.812 and 1.740. Nobody presses DAILY RUN again. At 17:05 the operator presses DAILY EMAIL and sends to the BCC client list.

**What must publish.** Either the app refuses to serve a distribution email off a PRE-CLOSE run, or every surface says so in words a reader cannot miss. The correct closing numbers are Priced = (1.812 − 1.920) × 100 = −10.8bp on the 17-Sep row and (1.740 − 1.920) × 100 = −18.0bp on 29-Oct, Step = −7.2bp; front table "% of 25bp" = −10.8/25 = −43%.

**How it breaks.** SnapDiscipline.cs:41-45 raises the mode note as "CHECK: PRE-CLOSE RUN — London 09:40, marks are live mids, not closes", and MainWindow.xaml.cs:335 filters exactly that string out of ConfirmChecks, so no popup fires at all (checks.Count == 0 → :336 returns true). The note lands in rep.Notes and in one scrolling log line (DailyBuilder.cs:250) and nowhere else — RunsTable.Title, DailyBlast.cs:42-43, the xlsx and the email body carry no mode marker. MainWindow.xaml.cs:799-800 unlocks DAILY EMAIL and nothing ever re-locks it, so at 17:05 the draft opens with subject "DRAX Swaps Closing Runs - 01 Sep 2026" (:888-889, dated from rep.AsOf, which is honest about the DAY and silent about the HOUR) over the 09:40 body: ECB front Mid 1.870, Priced −5.0bp, "% of 25bp" −20%, Step −7.5bp. That is 5.8bp of level and 5.8bp of Priced wrong on the front row, and it halves the priced-in figure a client will quote back. Worse, DailyBuilder.cs:385/392-393/419-422 already put "DRAX OIS Runs 1Sep26.xlsx" into the dailyDir and "OIS_Runs_1September26.xlsm" into the C+C share at 09:40 under today's date, so the shared record of the 1-Sep close is a 09:40 mid unless someone re-runs.

**Against the existing suite.** adjacent to 25 (boundary-day sources: the 16:15 snap anchors, the close never does) but from the operator side: 25 asks which mark anchors, this asks what a PRE-CLOSE mode publishes and whether any surface admits it. No catalogue scenario runs in PreClose mode.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** MainWindow.xaml.cs:335 does filter the PRE-CLOSE string out of the gate and :799-800 never re-lock DAILY EMAIL; grep for "PRE-CLOSE" finds it only in SnapDiscipline.cs:42 and that filter, so no renderer carries it. But the published mids are honest live marks under a misleading "Closing Runs" label on an un-gated stale run — the brief's own P1 case, not P0.

### 196. A year-old config\meetings.json left beside the exe silently replaces the calendar; Origin is dead code

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | moderate confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** MeetingsStore prefers an external config\meetings.json over the embedded one and records where it came from. Is that fact visible anywhere, and does a stale grid block distribution?

**Set-up.** During a release the operator followed the relaunch ritual but the staging folder still contained a config\ subfolder from a Sep-2025 build, so publish\config\meetings.json sits next to RatesWeekly.exe with last year's ECB grid (Sep/Oct/Dec-2025 starts and decisionDates, none after Dec-2025). Today 1-Sep-26. Live EESWE1 quotes SW_EFF_DT 17-Sep-26; the next real ECB decision is 10-Sep-26; €STR 1.920. Operator presses DAILY RUN at 16:20 and everything looks normal.

**What must publish.** The run must say, loudly and on a surface, that it is reading an external calendar dated 2025. Two of CalendarHealth's arms do detect the damage — :50-52 ("ECB: ticker period starting 17-Sep-26 is not in config\meetings.json — grid drifted or missing (phantom-meeting risk)") and :64-67 (no decision date for the period) — so the correct behaviour is a blocking gate before any file is written, and an ECB front line rendered honestly as "17-Sep-26*" with no decision date rather than paired against a 2025 meeting.

**How it breaks.** PricingServiceBoards.cs:228-229 takes the external file whenever it exists and stores the path in Origin (:217) — which grep shows is read NOWHERE in src, tests or tools. MainWindow.xaml.cs:124 prints only the assembly version, so the window and every output surface are identical whether the calendar is 2026-embedded or 2025-side-loaded. The CalendarHealth warnings at :50-52 and :64-67 carry no OutlierGuard.Prefix, so MainWindow.xaml.cs:334-336 filters them out of the blocking popup entirely; they scroll past as "daily note:" lines (DailyBuilder.cs:250) and, notes not being content, appear on no published surface. The board then runs on a calendar with every decision in the past: PricingServiceBoards.cs:861-864 rolls the front while every decision is Announced, and :871-875 can return "every resolved meeting is already decided", which PricingServiceWeekly.cs:218 turns into a non-CHECK note and drops the ECB block from the blast — while DailyBlast.cs:42 still prints {EU} in the header. The desk blasts an eight-bank table under a nine-flag header, and no gate ever fires.

**Against the existing suite.** 9 runs out of meetings and 48 has empty decisionDates; both are deliberate calendar states inside a current file. This is the OPERATOR case — a whole external calendar a year stale, plus the fact that neither Origin nor the CalendarHealth warnings can reach the gate. Harness already drives the override path (README: the loader is real, contents faked).

**Critic (CORRECTED).** PricingServiceBoards.cs:229 does prefer the external file and set Origin, which grep shows is read nowhere, and CalendarHealth.cs:50-52 carries no CHECK prefix so it cannot reach MainWindow.xaml.cs:334-336 — but the claimed ECB-vanishes break does not happen. **Change before writing it:** Drop the vanishing-ECB break: DecisionClock.DecisionFor (PricingServiceBoards.cs:862, tolerance 10d) returns null for a 2026 period start against 2025 decisions, so gateShift stays 0, :871-875 never fires, and PricingServiceWeekly.cs:235-238 already renders the honest "17-Sep-26*". Also arm 2 (CalendarHealth.cs:59-67) cannot fire — a 2025-only file leaves no future sched.Dates. Re-point the break at the un-gated arm-1/arm-3 warnings and at the stitcher losing every 2026 roll boundary (phantom steps in the change columns).

### 197. SOURCES saved with no terminal: ECB priced from NABZ vanishes from the blast, {EU} stays in the header

`P1 - missing gate or note | regression lock | moderate confidence | implementable-as-is | corrected`

**The question.** The SOURCES combo is pre-filled from a static 18-name catalogue before any probe and saves whatever is showing. What does a run do when the chosen contributor does not price that family at all?

**Set-up.** Mon 31-Aug-26, terminal not yet logged in (early morning, or a restart). Operator opens SOURCES intending to move RBA off NABZ, mis-clicks the row above and sets ECB → NABZ (an Australian bank that does not quote EESWE), then presses Enter. Next day at 16:20, terminal up, DAILY RUN. All eight other banks price normally: e.g. FOMC front Priced −23.0bp, MPC −9.5bp.

**What must publish.** Either the Save is refused for a contributor the live probe never returned for EESWE, or the run treats "the ECB priced nothing" as a distribution-blocking condition. A bank the daily surfaces are built around cannot silently leave the table.

**How it breaks.** MainWindow.xaml.cs:410-412 fills the combo from SourceCatalog.Candidates (SourceCatalog.cs:7-13) unconditionally; :393-397 swallows the no-terminal case with the comment "static candidate list still works"; the probe only runs on DropDownOpened (:449-460) and only ANNOTATES. Save (:476, IsDefault = true, so Enter commits) writes any non-default pick to sources.json (:479-495) with no validation, and SourceStore.Apply (SourceStore.cs:66-77) then reroutes ECB on EVERY subsequent run and every surface. With EESWE{N} NABZ Curncy unquoted, MeetingRun returns zero rows and PricingServiceWeekly.cs:218 adds "ECB: <warning>" — no OutlierGuard.Prefix, so MainWindow.xaml.cs:334-336 keeps it out of the blocking popup. DailyBlast.cs:48 then silently `continue`s the ECB block while :42 still prints the fixed nine-flag header "{EU} {GB} {AU} {NZ} {US} {CA} {JN} {NO} {SW} London EOD OIS Run — 01-Sep-26". The desk pastes a table that promises the ECB in its first flag and contains no ECB rows; the app says DAILY COMPLETE and unlocks COPY BLAST and DAILY EMAIL (:799-800). The only trace of the reroute is one "sources: ECB priced from NABZ (override…)" log line and a status line that has since been overwritten.

**Against the existing suite.** 44 (completeness gate: a bank publishes nothing on its decision day) and 34/36 cover the pricing side. New angle: the header/flag line still promises the missing bank, the zero-rows note cannot reach the blocking gate, and the cause is a persisted operator pick made with no terminal to validate it.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The Save path is unvalidated as described (MainWindow.xaml.cs:476-495, SourceStore.cs:66-77), but the ECB does not leave the table: MeetingTickers snapshots the plain composite spelling whenever a source is active (PricingServiceBoards.cs:581-582, added by the 2026-08-26 audit) and Resolve merges composite mid+dates at :663-668, so MeetingRun returns full rows. **Change before writing it:** Re-point the break: with NABZ unquoted the run still publishes, off the composite fallback, while CompoundedFixing.cs:84 stamps run.Source = "NABZ" and that label reaches the currency dashboards via CurrencyPage.cs:49 — a provenance mislabel, not a missing bank. Keep it as a lock that the fallback holds, and assert the source label.

### 198. SOURCES saved while WEEKLY RUN is grinding: dashboards on one contributor, the email on another

`P0 - wrong number published | suspected defect | high confidence | live-or-manual-only | confirmed`

**The question.** SOURCES is never disabled and has no Busy guard, while RenderAll and EmailBuilder each load sources.json independently, seconds apart. Can one run publish two different contributors?

**Set-up.** Fri 4-Sep-26. Operator presses WEEKLY RUN (a three-minute job: ~989-ticker pull, 28 dashboards, movers hub, single-file pack, then the email). While the log is scrolling they remember the RBA feed was thin yesterday, open SOURCES, set RBA → composite (from the NABZ default) and press Save. The click lands after RenderAll has read sources.json and before EmailBuilder.Build runs. RBA, 30-Sep-26 period: NABZ mid 3.410, composite mid 3.485; RBA cash 3.600.

**What must publish.** One run, one contributor per bank, on every surface — the standing desk rule. Either the SOURCES button is dead for the duration of a run, or the run snapshots the overrides once at the start and hands the same dictionary to the dashboards, the movers hub, the pack and the email.

**How it breaks.** MainWindow.xaml.cs:365 (Sources_Click) has no `if (Busy) return;` — compare Update_Click:532, Daily_Click:774, ExportXls_Click:962 — and the grep of IsEnabled shows SourcesBtn is never disabled anywhere, while the awaited Task.Run at :543 keeps the dispatcher pumping. RenderAll loads the overrides at :630 and uses them for aud.html, index.html and RatesWeekly_Dashboards.html; EmailBuilder.Build, called later at :554, loads them again at EmailBuilder.cs:89. A Save between the two splits the run: the dashboards and the attached pack publish the RBA 30-Sep row at Mid 3.410, Priced (3.410 − 3.600) × 100 = −19.0bp, while the email body — and the pack attached TO that email — publish Mid 3.485, Priced −11.5bp. 7.5bp apart, in the same mail, on the same date stamp, with the run declaring "WEEKLY COMPLETE — email and dashboards rebuilt" (:594). The status line the desk would need ("Applies from the next run", :493) is a lie in this window.

**Against the existing suite.** scenario 51-53 and the SOURCES-carries-everywhere rule assume one source per run; nothing tests concurrent mutation. Adjacent to 34 (contributor vs composite) but the defect is the UI's missing Busy guard, not the pricing.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** Sources_Click at MainWindow.xaml.cs:365 has no Busy guard where :532/:774/:962 all do, SourcesBtn is absent from every IsEnabled site, and the whole run sits inside an awaited Task.Run (:543) that keeps the dispatcher pumping; RenderAll reads sources.json at :630 and EmailBuilder re-reads it at EmailBuilder.cs:89 after :554. Priced arithmetic (-19.0 vs -11.5bp, 7.5bp) is self-consistent.
