### 199. CHECK popup: Enter or Space publishes — the gate's default answer is "distribute"

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | high confidence | live-or-manual-only | confirmed`

**The question.** The one blocking gate in the app is a Yes/No MessageBox. Which button does the keyboard commit, and does anything on the output record that a human accepted the flag?

**Set-up.** Wed 2-Sep-26, 16:20 London. BOJ, TONA family. A mis-rung 1w anchor across the 20-Aug renumber makes the 18-Dec row's Δ1w read +34.0bp while the rest of the strip reads +5.0, +6.0, +6.5, +7.0 (the true 1w change on that contract is +6.0bp). Operator has been typing in another window and, as usual, is clearing dialogs by feel.

**What must publish.** OutlierGuard.cs:41-45 raises "CHECK: BOJ 18-Dec-26 Δ1w +34.0bp exceeds the 30bp sanity bar — verify before distribution" (34.0 > AbsW1Bp = 30.0). A gate whose whole purpose is to stop an unverified number must default to the SAFE answer (No), and if the desk does accept it, the accepted flag should be visible to whoever reads the output.

**How it breaks.** MainWindow.xaml.cs:337-341 calls the MessageBox.Show overload that takes owner/text/caption/button/image and no defaultResult, which maps to Win32 MB_DEFBUTTON1 — for MessageBoxButton.YesNo that is YES. So Enter, Space, or a stray click at the left-hand button position returns MessageBoxResult.Yes at :342 and the run proceeds. The flagged number then publishes completely bare: OutlierGuard only produces notes (:43, :59, :81), the row is not Rejected so none of the MaskLabels marking at PricingServiceWeekly.cs:269-273 applies, DailyBlast prints "+34.0" in the Δ1w column like any other cell, and no surface records that a CHECK was raised or accepted. The desk blasts BOJ Δ1w +34.0 against a strip of +5 to +7 — 28bp of pure anchor error, with a positive human confirmation behind it that took no human attention.

**Against the existing suite.** 40 and 45 establish that the bars fire and that notes never become content. This attacks the human end of the same mechanism: the gate's default answer and the absence of any accepted-flag record on the published surfaces.

**Critic (CONFIRMED).** MainWindow.xaml.cs:337-341 calls the five-arg MessageBox.Show overload with no defaultResult, which WPF maps to MB_DEFBUTTON1 = Yes for MessageBoxButton.YesNo, and the note text/threshold match OutlierGuard.cs:23,41-45 exactly; the row is not Rejected so the PricingServiceWeekly.cs:269-273 marking never applies. P1 is the right call — the gate's default, not the arithmetic, is the fault.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:332` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:337` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:341` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:342` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly/MainWindow.xaml.cs:788` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:23` | `src/RateDesk.Core/OutlierGuard.cs:41` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:269`

### 200. Manual outage rows typed in this morning only reach the change columns on the SECOND press of DAILY RUN

`P1 - missing gate or note | suspected defect | low confidence | needs-harness-extension | corrected`

**The question.** FallbackIngest runs in Render and ExportBook but not in Build. Does a manual row the desk just entered affect the run the desk is pressing for?

**Set-up.** Bloomberg API was refused all of Mon 31-Aug-26 (a real class of outage in this shop), so nothing was published and no closes were stored for the meeting rungs. Tue 1-Sep the desk types Monday's marks into the fallback workbook named in publish.json — ECB 17-Sep period close 1.865 for 31-Aug — and presses DAILY RUN at 16:20. Live 1-Sep 16:15 mark on the same contract: 1.925. The last stored close is Fri 28-Aug at 1.855. Δ1d cap is 5 days.

**What must publish.** With Monday's manual row in place, Δ1d must anchor 31-Aug: (1.925 − 1.865) × 100 = +6.0bp. That is the whole point of the failsafe loop and of the 'xls' source rows.

**How it breaks.** DailyBuilder.Build (:193-250) builds the report — including svc.BuildWeekly and therefore every D1/W1/M1 anchor at PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286-288 — and never calls FallbackIngest; grep shows the only call sites are Render (:370-371) and ExportBook (:111-112). So the first press reads a store that still has no 31-Aug row, ChangeToBp (:532-535) takes the last point at or before 31-Aug = Fri 28-Aug at 1.855, and the blast publishes Δ1d = (1.925 − 1.855) × 100 = +7.0bp — a two-business-day change under a Δ1d label, 1.0bp adrift here and arbitrarily worse on a busier Monday. Render then ingests the manual row, so pressing DAILY RUN a second time prints +6.0bp for the same 16:20 marks. Two presses, two answers, no explanation on any surface; and if the operator presses once, the blast, the workbook, the C+C save-down and the client mail all carry the mislabelled window. The idiot-proofing failure is that the app invites the edit (the log even says "daily: no fallbackBook in publish.json — manual-override ingest off" when it is absent) and then ignores it for one cycle.

**Against the existing suite.** 24 caps a three-week hole and 27 covers the CoD fallback; neither involves the fallback workbook. New: the ordering of FallbackIngest relative to the anchor computation.

**Critic (CORRECTED).** The ordering claim is exactly right — grep shows FallbackIngest.Run only at DailyBuilder.cs:112, :371 and :409, never in Build (:193-250), so the anchors at PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286 see the pre-ingest store. But the premise as written is self-defeating. **Change before writing it:** With the terminal back up on 1-Sep, StoreBackedHistory.GetDaily (StoreBackedHistory.cs:42-61) sees last=28-Aug < PrevBd and gap-fills 31-Aug from BDH before any anchor is computed, after which FallbackIngest.cs:126 skips the manual row as "engine data exists". Re-set the scenario on a day Bloomberg's own history cannot supply for that rung (a thin contributor spelling, or a terminal still refused), which is the only state the fallback book exists for.

**Code.** `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:193` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:234` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:370` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:373` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/DailyBuilder.cs:111` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:286` | `src/RateDesk.Core/PricingServiceWeekly.cs:532` | `src/RateDesk.Weekly.Core/Daily/FallbackIngest.cs:133`

# What would make it idiot-proof

The fleet returned 212 guardrail proposals; the full list is in `GUARDRAILS_RAW.md` beside this
file. Distilled, they are eighteen changes, ordered by ratio of harm prevented to work required.
Nothing here changes a published number — every one of them is a refusal, a stamp or a note.

### Refuse to publish without these

1. **One clock per run.** Resolve London-now **once**, at the top of a run, and pass that value
   everywhere: `SnapDiscipline`, `MarksAsOfLondon`, the date stamps, the anchor arithmetic. Today
   `DecisionClock.LondonNow()` is called at least three times in a daily build and a run spanning
   midnight gets three different answers. Assert in the `MarksAsOfLondon` setter that it is never
   later than now — a gate dated after its own prices is always a bug.
2. **`MarksAsOfLondon` in `EmailBuilder`, and `LateAnnouncementNotes` with it.** Finding 1. This is
   a four-line change and it is the single highest-value item in this document.
3. **One rung map, fully armed.** Give `MeetingRungMap` a static factory that takes the store and
   builds the `recordedEffective` lambda and the `ObservedShifts` binding itself, then make it the
   *only* way to construct one. Four of the five unarmed sites are drift; the fifth (`DailyBook`)
   passes an explicit opt-out with the `FINDINGS.md` reason as its argument name.
4. **A stamped as-of on every artefact.** Every page, workbook, blast and email carries the marks'
   own timestamp *and* the press time when they differ, and the public pages carry it in the
   header, not the footer. Half a dozen scenarios below reduce to "the desk could not tell which
   day this was."
5. **Gate the public site the way the email is gated.** Render the dashboards to a staging
   directory, run `ConfirmChecks`, and only then swap them in. Today `No` withholds the email and
   publishes the pages.
6. **Make the CHECK modal's default answer `No`,** and make it list-scrollable. A gate whose
   default is "distribute" is not a gate.
7. **A machine-timezone assertion at start-up.** If the box's local date differs from London's,
   say so in the status bar and in the log, permanently. Several independent scenarios below are
   the same UTC+3 desk box.
8. **Refuse to email a report older than N minutes** without an explicit override, and put the
   report's build time in the subject when it is not today's close.

### Say out loud what is currently only true in a comment

9.  **A calendar self-check on start-up:** every `decisionTimeLondon` validated against a recorded
    source local time plus an IANA zone, so DST divergence is caught by arithmetic rather than by
    a trader noticing. Store the source time in config (`"decisionLocal": "14:00 America/New_York"`)
    and derive the London string; never hand-maintain both.
10. **Name the calendar in every output.** Which `meetings.json` loaded, embedded or override, with
    its hash and mtime. Four scenarios are an override file nobody knew was there.
11. **Route notes by consequence, not by prefix.** Three severities — BLOCKS, WARNS, INFORMS —
    chosen at the call site. `CoherenceNotes` is currently a blocking gate that documents itself as
    non-blocking, because the only way to be noticed was to borrow the `CHECK` prefix.
12. **Never report a skipped guard as a pass.** `"futures guard X: no covered, quoted contract
    window found — skipped"` should be a WARN. The guard silently switching itself off is
    indistinguishable, in the log, from it agreeing.
13. **Mark provenance in the cell, not the note.** A synthesized mid, a re-based fixing, a
    hand-typed outage mark and a Bloomberg close must be visually distinct on every surface
    including the archive-of-record workbooks. The dagger exists for the re-base; nothing else has
    one.

### Structural

14. **Pin the harness's own weekday.** `FINDINGS.md` already records 8 of 64 scenarios failing on
    Mondays or Fridays. Until `Cal.D(n)` stops reading `DateTime.Today`, the suite cannot be a
    release gate — and that is a prerequisite for everything in this catalogue.
15. **Test the wiring, not just the engine.** Add harness entry points that drive
    `EmailBuilder.Build` and `DailyBuilder.Build` as the shipping app calls them, rather than
    constructing a `PricingService` and pre-setting its state. Finding 1 is invisible to 64
    scenarios for exactly this reason.
16. **A run ledger.** One row per run: timestamp, marks-as-of, config hash, store row count, source
    overrides, notes raised, what was written and where. Nothing today can answer "which store
    state produced Wednesday's blast?"
17. **Make the snapshot rotation safe.** Two runs on a fresh machine currently roll the only
    irreplaceable backup out of both generations. Keep dated generations and never let a shallow
    store overwrite a deep one — compare row counts before rotating.
18. **A cross-surface parity assertion in the product,** not only in the tests: before writing, the
    daily builder should verify that the blast, the workbook and the sheet email are the same table
    cell-for-cell, and refuse to write if they are not. The harness already checks this; the
    shipping app does not.

# Held back

Fifty-nine further scenarios were generated and cut for space or overlap. They are listed here by
title so nothing is lost; several are strong, particularly in the four vetted domains.

- **[The announcement clock]** On a machine east of London the 16:20 close run books today's intraday print as today's close and prints every change as 0.0  `P0 · wrong number published`
- **[The announcement clock]** A single BOJ announcement time cannot be right: the statement lands anywhere from 03:30 to 04:30 London and the gate has no tolerance  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[The rate that does not bite yet]** Release ritual deletes publish\config\meetings.json - the emergency cut vanishes and nothing says which calendar loaded  `P0 · wrong number published`
- **[The rate that does not bite yet]** Inter-meeting cut then a scheduled meeting 12 days later: every public dashboard row reads the wrong contract  `P0 · wrong number published`
- **[The rate that does not bite yet]** BOJ: a rung with no SW_EFF_DT publishes a config date as its Start, up to 10 days from ticker truth  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[The rate that does not bite yet]** Compounded fixing: the FOMC window opens on the decision day, so one old-rate day is inside it (silent today)  `P2 · cosmetic / ops`
- **[How the tickers roll]** A leftover config\meetings.json beside the exe silently becomes the calendar - no surface says so  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[How the tickers roll]** Outage marks from the fallback workbook are filed on a rung the history reader resolves to another meeting  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[How the tickers roll]** Day after a Riksbank roll, the dashboard's hard-data gate tests the wrong rung and drops the last row  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[The change columns]** A 14-day change publishes under the 1w header: the cap allows a gap of exactly 7, and 8 blanks it  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[The change columns]** 29, 30 and 31-Mar all anchor the 1m on 26-Feb; on 1-Apr it jumps three days forward  `P2 · cosmetic / ops`
- **[Unscheduled and inter-meeting moves]** An inter-meeting decision whose effective date is more than 10 days out switches the gate AND the re-base off, silently  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[The guards and the pre-publish gate]** RBA period start published one day late: the IB futures guard prints "ok" at 0.8bp, and cannot resolve a boundary error under three days  `P2 · cosmetic / ops`
- **[The guards and the pre-publish gate]** Quiet MPC strip, three body rows identical: MAD is structurally zero, the adaptive term is dead, and 4.0bp is silent while 4.5bp fires a modal  `P2 · cosmetic / ops`
- **[The guards and the pre-publish gate]** Delete a bank from meetings.json and it vanishes from every surface with no note — the gate checks configured-then-empty, never nine-then-configured  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[The guards and the pre-publish gate]** The Comdty block misses the snapshot: all five wired futures guards report "skipped", none of them CHECK-prefixed, and a broken decision-day board goes out uncrossed  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[The guards and the pre-publish gate]** Morning after an MPC cut: the re-base window closed last night because the shipped MPC entry has no fixingLagDays, so Priced is a full step wrong with no dagger  `P0 · wrong number published`
- **[The guards and the pre-publish gate]** A contributor page that publishes no LAST_UPDATE gets no staleness watch at all, and the run says nothing about not knowing  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[Inflation fixings: RPI noise, rolls and prints]** OPERATOR: the daily run at 08:05 publishes yesterday's quotes under today's date, and Delta1d is the snap-vs-close residue on every tenor  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[Inflation fixings: RPI noise, rolls and prints]** OPERATOR: a weekly UPDATE then EXPORT XLS rewrites yesterday's already-emailed Fixing Runs workbook with today's marks, same filename  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[The compounded o/n fixing]** A hand-edited config\meetings.json beside the exe moves the Riksbank window a week; the board's Bloomberg dates stay right and the compounded fixing is 29bp wrong with no note naming the calendar  `P0 · wrong number published`
- **[The compounded o/n fixing]** BOJ Golden Week is a genuine six-day gap that must publish; the same six-day gap with a fixing shift inside it publishes 7.5bp wrong, and the code cannot tell them apart  `P1 · missing gate or note`
- **[The compounded o/n fixing]** Five days into a Fed cut the compounded EFFR must read 3.8006 against a 3.75 spot — the one stale day is real, and fixingLagDays must stay out of the compounded window  `P1 · missing gate or note`
